- 📋 **Superseded (2026-08-10) — the publisher's own partition tree is ~4x finer than we can infer, and 87% of it is still behind a click.** *(Harvested and resolved the same day — see the entry above. Kept because it dates the id-gap prediction of 21,676 hidden children, which the harvest then confirmed at 26,031 total nodes.)* Harvested from the site's Telerik `ClientState`: 92/92 works, 3,104 nodes — but only 36 works returned any depth-1, because children load on demand. The `value` ids are allocated depth-first in reading order (monotonic in 92 of 92), so the gap between consecutive top-level ids counts the hidden children exactly: **21,676**, for a real tree of ~24,780 nodes against our 5,968 inferred divisions. This corrects the earlier read that "most works show missed=0, we sit one level below the site's navigation top" — that was measured on depth-0 only and is backwards. **The tree still carries no text position**, so anchoring on it (rather than on the markers or the pages) needs its own binding step and is NOT a prerequisite for shipping: the page grain above supersedes it for addressing. Reopen only if the labels themselves are wanted.
  - **The genuine either/or set is small:** of 74,876 multi-alignment rows, only **6,272 (8.4%)** contain a pair sharing ≥50% of the shorter page interval AND naming disjoint units; 90,813 same-page pairs AGREE on the unit and are already folded by `merge_witnessed_spans`. Policies: TODAY 102,154 unit-citations / BAKE-ALL 148,450 / refuse-contested 140,718 (83.3% of the gain).
  - **Density points backwards on the one graded case** (card 6's false piece is denser than the true one; corpus-wide non-primary alignments are denser than primaries, median 0.2559 vs 0.2132, because they are shorter). And **the frozen primary is itself one side of a disagreement on 5,507 rows (7.4%)** — the shipped `w_start`/`w_end` may already name the wrong unit there. Codex: refuse-contested is "conservative about volume, not truth"; defer until a TRACKED multi-piece render path exists (`render_locus`/`extra_pieces` live only in the untracked deck; `compress_pieces`/`split_at_citation_breaks` have no caller anywhere; every `citation_runs` test passes ONE piece), conflicts get per-piece grading from a stratified sample (5 multi-piece cards ever judged), and either a validated tiebreaker or an explicit disjunction display exists (`;` reads as "and").
  - **Conflict is per-WORK, not per-genre — the owner's read, confirmed and refined.** Under a length control the quoter genres are NOT above the primary texts (0.88% vs 1.15% at 20–60k letters; 1.64% vs 1.86% at 60–200k). But **34 works at ≥2x their length peers hold 53% of all conflicts on 15% of the rows** — half compilations repeating their sources (`משנה תורה, ספר אהבה` 14.7%, 1,062 conflicts = 17% of the corpus total; `רי"ף כתובות` 10.6%; `ילקוט שמעוני` ~5%), half **the text's own doublets** (`תנ"ך, שמות` 859 — the tabernacle command//execution repetition; `ויקרא`, `מלכים ב`, `ירמיהו` י//נא), where naming both places is CORRECT and refusing them would delete a true fact. The compilation half belongs to the quotation problem, not the citation problem.
  - **Owner refinement (2026-08-11), MEASURED AND CONFIRMED: the curation unit is the SHARED TEXT (a locus-unit REGION), not the work.** His claim — the phenomenon lives in specific parts (tefillot, berakhot, the Haggadah, embedded Mishnah, Bible verses) — tested by distributing every gen-2 alignment over the locus units: `ספר אהבה`'s `סדר התפילה` units are **20.0% of the text and 73.7% of the matched mass** (81% of rows sit ≥80% inside); `ספר זמנים` concentrates **49.0% of its mass in ONE unit at 22.3x** (`הלכות חמץ ומצה, פרק ח` — the Haggadah text); and the `ילקוט שמעוני` contrast case half-failed INFORMATIVELY — its one hotspot (`רמז תתקלח`, the opening of כי תבוא = the מקרא-ביכורים midrash) is the Haggadah's own maggid, with 535 of its 700 pages also claiming `הגדה של פסח`: the SAME shared text spiking in a different container. Weak cases stated plainly: `ספר נשים` shows only mild concentration (max 3.5x, `הלכות אישות` ב–ו), and `משנה, יומא` is FLAT across its chapters (max 1.5x) — its off-ness is the container/liturgy effect spread over the whole tractate, consistent with the owner's "no real difference between יומא and סוכה" as works. Consequence: a shared-text REGION table over the locus units (`locus_ref_id`, unit range, kind in {tefillah, berakhah, haggadah, formula, mishnah-embedded, verse}) classifies any match by WHERE it lands — offsets only, no re-match, no reference-text read; the work-level flag stays only for through-and-through quoters. Probe: `probe_region_concentration.py` (session tmp).
  - **Reducer:** `pytest tests/render_smoke/test_panel_render_smoke.py tests/test_findings_page.py -q`
  - **Mechanism (measured, not inferred).** After the panel capture runs, `web.discovery._service._heavy_sem` is at `value=0` against `_heavy_capacity=4` — all four slots held by nobody. `_run_off_loop` deliberately transfers slot release to `fut.add_done_callback` rather than a `finally`, so a timed-out read keeps its slot until the worker thread ACTUALLY finishes (correct: `run_in_executor` threads are not cancellable). When a test's `asyncio.run()` loop is torn down with that future still pending, the callback never fires and the slot is gone for the life of the process. The next findings read then hits `sem.locked()` and returns `busy` **without dispatching** — which is what the dispatch counter reports as zero.
  - **NOT the same cause as the 2026-08-04 fix below,** whose `sys.modules` re-import trap remains fixed. Note that hypothesis (4) of that investigation — "a leaked heavy-concurrency slot" — was refuted THEN by measuring `_heavy_sem` at 4/4 after the polluting suite. It is the right answer NOW, for a different polluting suite. The `HONEST SCOPE` comment in `shared/discovery_service.py::_run_off_loop` already records that the zero-dispatch failure was left open.
  - **Production is NOT affected**, and the reason is the whole diagnosis: the serving loop lives for the process lifetime, so the done-callback always fires. This is a test-isolation defect.
  - **Beware the worktree trap when bisecting this.** A `git worktree` has no gitignored data, so `discovery_data/` is absent, `discovery_available()` is False, and the reads short-circuit BEFORE taking a slot — the semaphore then reads a clean 4/4 and an innocent commit looks guilty. Revert source files inside the MAIN tree instead. (Cost me one wrong attribution on 2026-08-09; see [[reference_worktrees_lack_gitignored_data]].)
  - **Likely fix directions (none attempted):** give each test a service instance rather than sharing the module singleton across loops; or have `_acquire_slot` detect a semaphore bound to a dead loop and rebuild it; or drain-and-reset the budget in an autouse fixture. Whichever is chosen, the assertion must stay — zero dispatches is the signature of a synchronous call on the single-uvicorn-worker event loop.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04, commit `886353a3`) — `test_exactly_one_executor_dispatch_per_enveloped_read_when_available` measured ZERO dispatches under a multi-file run and passed alone.** Found by CI's first run over the phase-136 work (run 30916034727, `tests (ubuntu-latest)`); it also took down `tests/test_joins_builder.py` and `tests/test_joins_lab_new_search_reset.py` with a cascade of `RuntimeError: Event loop is closed`. One cause for both.
  - **Root cause — a `sys.modules` trap worth knowing repo-wide.** `_reimport_web_discovery()` in `tests/test_discovery_assets_audience.py` (and its source idiom in `tests/test_discovery_composition.py`) did `sys.modules.pop("web.discovery")` + re-import. That builds a **second module object**, and restoring `sys.modules` does **not** undo it: the import machinery ALSO rebinds the submodule as an **attribute of the parent package**, and `import web.discovery as x` reads that package attribute. So `sys.modules["web.discovery"]` and `web.discovery` ended up as different objects. `web/pages/findings.py` from-imports `get_findings_enveloped`, whose `__globals__` *is* the dict of whichever object was live at first import — so the test patched `discovery_available` on one object while the page read the other, took the unavailable branch, and dispatched nothing. The second module-level execution of `web/discovery.py` is what leaked a closed loop into the joins suites.
  - **Fix:** `importlib.reload()` in both helpers — it re-executes the module in its existing namespace, so exactly one object stays live in `sys.modules`, on the package, and as the page's `__globals__`, while the module-level `DiscoveryService` those helpers exist to rebuild is still rebuilt. Caveat (same as the `shared.local_indexer` precedent in `tests/conftest.py`): a reload does NOT rebind names another module already from-imported, so a caller needing the new *function object* must rebind it itself.
  - **Why it mattered:** zero dispatches is the signature of a synchronous call on the event loop — on a single-uvicorn-worker server that stalls every concurrent request while burning no CPU, invisible in load average. The assertion was never silenced.
  - **Reducer (now green):** `pytest tests/test_discovery_assets_audience.py tests/test_findings_page.py -p no:randomly -k "executor_dispatch or audience"`.
  - **Five hypotheses refuted before the real one — do not re-try.** (1) *Sidecar absence*: reproduced CI's condition with `GENIZAH_DISCOVERY_DATA_DIR` at an empty directory; passes. (2) *Warm `_browse_cached_call` LRU*: clearing `_browse_lru` changes nothing — and `get_findings_enveloped_async` passes no `cache_name` at all. (3) *`_run_off_loop` using `asyncio.get_event_loop()`*: changed and KEPT (correct on its own merits) but not the fix. (4) *A leaked heavy-concurrency slot*: measured `_heavy_sem` at 4/4 unlocked after the polluting suite. (5) *Restoring `sys.modules` only*: still failed — that is what exposed the package-attribute half of the trap.
  - **What actually found it:** printing module identity and the returned envelope *inside* the failing test (`unavailable / sidecar_not_serving`, `fp globals is wd: False`, `wd is sysmod: False`). Three prints beat five hypotheses.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-05) — the panel masking capture's "derived" coverage was a NAMING CONVENTION, and it measured function ENTRY rather than what ran.** Codex code review round 3 (round 15 overall), BLOCKER — this is the D-25 gate, so a hole in it is the serious kind. `tests/render_smoke/test_panel_render_smoke.py::_panel_render_entry_points` collected top-level functions whose name began with `render_` and called that a derivation. It is a convention, and the same module already broke it twice: **`_neutral_chip`** (the relation chip) and **`_service_state_block`** (every outage state) both draw reader-visible text and neither was collected. Worse, the instrumentation recorded that a function was ENTERED — so an unexercised BRANCH inside a captured function was invisible, and measurement found real ones: `granularity_subline` and `low_coverage_note` (both model-emitted text on a shipped row), the whole collapsed "more matches" level including its row loop, three of the manuscript pane's four states, and **every surface painted by a click** — the per-work expansion body, the related-page rows and the pane's overflow, i.e. exactly where projection text arrives. A restricted corpus name on any of them would have passed the scan with `expected == exercised`. **Now:** the set is derived from what each function CALLS (`ui.*`, transitively) so renaming everything changes nothing; the capture is TRACED with `sys.settrace` and every executable line of every UI-emitting function must run, taken from Python's own compiler (`co_lines()`) rather than an AST guess; and a generic sweep CLICKS every control the render produced, twice, under three lazy-read regimes (answer / outage / raise), so a disclosure added later is driven without the capture being edited. `coverage` was rejected as the measurement tool: it is in no requirements file, and a gate that only runs where an optional package happens to be installed is the same defect. **Result:** 12 UI-emitting functions (was 10 "render_" names), **245 of 246 required lines executed**, capture 109,031 → 181,227 characters. The one exemption is `ui.label(title)` in `_render_expansion_envelope`, unreachable BY CONTRACT (`SURFACE_EXPANSION_FIELDS` carries no title), named with its reason and **pinned by count** so the hatch cannot be widened quietly.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-05) — the benchmark's shape reader was a THIRD attempt at parsing SQL, and it too defaulted to "row set" for the grammar it had not enumerated.** Codex code review round 4 (round 16 overall), MEDIUM, on the fix recorded immediately below. `scripts/bench_discovery.py::sql_result_shape` recognised a scalar aggregate only when a top-level select expression BEGAN with an aggregate call, so `SELECT CASE WHEN 1 THEN COUNT(*) ELSE 9 END`, `SELECT -- comment\n COUNT(*)` and `SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM t)` — each returning exactly one scalar row — were filed as ROW SETS and recorded `population = 1` for a count of nothing (the round-13 vacuous assertion, restored a third time), while a LEADING `/*comment*/` returned `unknown` and would have aborted a legitimate statement. Those four outcomes were MEASURED against SQLite before the reader was deleted, not argued from the review text; the same measurement acquitted it on `COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE ...)` and nested CTEs, which it classified correctly. **A third parser was not written.** Parsing SQL text was the wrong authority: writing one asserts that SQL's expression grammar has been enumerated, which is an assertion nobody can check. The shape is now (1) **DECLARED** by the spec, at the call site that CHOSE it — the same statement that passes `count_only=True` to the shipped builder says `SHAPE_SCALAR` in the same breath, via `_scalar_spec` / `_row_spec` / `_skipped_spec`; and (2) **VERIFIED against the executed result** (`cursor.description` + the fetched rows), which no syntax can fool because no syntax is read. What the result can and cannot decide is stated rather than overclaimed: it refutes a declared scalar decisively (an un-grouped aggregate returns exactly ONE row carrying a NUMBER — anything else, incl. a window function or a `UNION`, contradicts it), but it CANNOT refute a declared row set, since one row of one integer column is character-for-character what a scalar returns — so every row-set spec carries an `expected_rows` computed independently of the statement being timed (the filter state's own count at that unit, through the builder's own `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`), and a mismatch aborts. A declared scalar reading column 0 of a MULTI-column row also aborts unless the spec names the column, closing the `SELECT 1, COUNT(*)` door by refusal rather than by inference. A spec that declares NO shape is `unknown` and **ABORTS by name** — there is no default to fall into, which is what produced this finding three times. Controls execute all nine named syntaxes (`CASE WHEN`, leading + interleaved comments, scalar subquery, `FILTER (WHERE ...)`, nested CTEs, aggregate inside a string literal, `UNION`, `COUNT(*) OVER ()`, and a genuine row query returning one single-column integer row) against real SQLite, assert the population is the VALUE for scalars and the ROW COUNT for row sets, and show the row-set reading of each scalar to be the false green. Mutation-proved twice: the deleted reader driven over the same statements goes red on 5 of 12, and defaulting an undeclared shape (or accepting a row set with no expectation) turns the named controls red. **Re-measured on the 375 MB public artifact: 483 enumerated / 337 measured / 146 skipped / 0 over cap — every label, every POPULATION figure and every verdict byte-identical to the previous run,** now with 256 row-set specs cross-checked against an independent expected row count and 81 scalar specs cross-checked for one-row-plus-integer (79 of them against an independently executed count).
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-05) — the benchmark's "is this an aggregate" derivation was a two-line regex that got three real shapes wrong, silently.** *(Superseded by the entry above — its replacement shape reader was itself defeated one round later.)* Codex code review round 3 (round 15 overall), MEDIUM. `scripts/bench_discovery.py::_is_scalar_aggregate` matched `^SELECT COUNT\(` and "no outer-level GROUP BY", so `WITH x AS (...) SELECT COUNT(*) ...` and `SELECT n FROM (SELECT COUNT(*) AS n ...)` both classified as ROW SETS — each recording `population == 1` for a count of nothing, i.e. exactly the vacuous assertion the round-13 fix was written to eliminate — while `SELECT COUNT(*) OVER ()` classified as SCALAR though a window function produces one value per row. Today's statements dodge all three by luck; a CTE or an outer wrapper is one refactor away. Replaced by a small explicit shape reader (`sql_result_shape`) that walks parenthesis depth outside string literals and comments, strips a CTE prefix, refuses compound statements, requires POSITIVE evidence for scalar (a top-level aggregate CALL, never a windowed one, with no outer GROUP BY), looks through a sole-parenthesised-subquery wrapper, and returns **which column** carries the aggregate — `SELECT 1, COUNT(*)` would otherwise have read the literal as the population. Its third state is `unknown`, and the benchmark **ABORTS by name** on it rather than defaulting to row counting: guessing is how the assertion went vacuous the first time. `spec['kind']` was considered as the authority and rejected — it is a budget class, not a shape (`findings_launch_contribution_*` is `kind='count'` and returns one row per shade). Controls execute each shape against real SQLite and assert the naive reading is the false green. Re-measured on the 375 MB public artifact: 483 enumerated / 337 measured / 146 skipped, unchanged, 0 over cap.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — a reader could drive the findings page into an unhandled `ValueError` with two ordinary clicks.** Codex code review round 3 (round 15 overall), HIGH, and a **user-reachable crash**, not a benchmark nit. `web/pages/findings.py` left the candidacy switch live and its "Show as" control changed the row unit underneath it, so turning **Candidates for new finds** on and then choosing **one row per work** made `fetch_findings` hand `_build_findings_query` a combination it deliberately REFUSES (novelty is a verdict about one work on one fragment; a work row has none). The `ValueError` escaped into the background task NiceGUI schedules an async handler on, `core.app.handle_exception` turned it into a log line, and the refresh died half-way — from the reader's side the page simply stopped responding. **Fixed on the page, not by legalising the combination:** the rule now lives in ONE place (`shared/discovery_service.py::findings_novelty_offered`) used by all three parties that need it — the builder that raises, the envelope's `meta['novelty_offered']`, and the page, which now settles the pair in `normalise_state()` from `read_state` and from the one refresh path, disables the switch, drops the stored selection, dims the card and renders the reason in words (a filter that silently stopped filtering while still showing itself as on would be worse than the crash). The facet cascade drops the selection too, so counts keep describing the rows beside them. **Control:** `test_turning_candidates_on_then_switching_to_one_row_per_work_does_not_break_the_page` drives the real sequence through the simulated user in both languages and asserts the rendered outcome — a test that called the builder directly would not have caught this, because the builder was never wrong. `scripts/bench_discovery.py` called those 80 states "unreachable" while the page could reach them; the skip reason now derives from the same predicate.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — the Phase-136 connections panel's D-25 masking test SKIPPED on a clean checkout, and scanned the repository instead of the surface.** Codex review of wave 9, BLOCKER. `tests/render_smoke/test_panel_render_smoke.py` called `pytest.skip` when the gitignored pattern file was unavailable, and when configured ran only `--scan-repo` — so a restricted corpus name reaching the rendered panel or one of its envelopes (from the sidecar, or assembled at render time) was invisible to it, and on CI the required check reported a skip on every run. A skip is a silent green, the one thing the standing rule forbids of this gate. Split into three tests by what each can actually prove: a capture of the WHOLE surface (7 profiles × 2 languages × 4 service states of rendered panel + every envelope + all six error paths, written outside the working tree) with its own contents asserted; an ALWAYS-RUNNING positive control that plants a FABRICATED needle and requires the real scanner to report it (proving the render→capture→scan pipeline can return non-zero, with no secret); and the real scan over BOTH `--scan-asset` and `--scan-repo`, which now **FAILS rather than skips** when the pattern file is absent, with the provisioning step named in the failure. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` now provisions the set from a `MASKING_SCAN_PATTERNS` repository secret and deletes it in an `if: always()` step — the "no tracked provisioning" half of the finding. **⚠ Operational: if that secret is not configured, the `render-smoke-tests` job goes RED.** That is the fail-closed posture the rule requires; the remedy is to add the secret, never to restore the skip.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — three of the connections panel's four outage paths offered no retry, and one hid the panel entirely.** Codex review of wave 9, HIGH. Only the claims-level outage was given the retry callback; the manuscript-works, related-count and page-scope outages all took `_service_state_block`'s `on_retry=None` default, so a reader who hit one saw a named temporary condition and no way out. Worse, a claims-level TRUE zero plus a page-scope outage hid the entry control — which is the only door to the panel that carries the retry at all (F-14's false zero, relocated to the page-ID read). The renderer now threads the handler to every eager-read outage (it re-issues all four reads, so one control recovers whichever failed); `("ok", SCOPE_OUTAGE)` loses its hide permission while `SCOPE_UNRESOLVED` keeps it, because an unresolvable page scope is a FACT about the manuscript and a failed read is our plumbing — and only the second gets a retry. **The test gap that hid it is the real fix:** every panel fixture in the phase assigned ONE status to claims, works and the related count together, so mixed states were structurally unreachable. A `mixed_bundle(failing, status)` now fails exactly one of the four, with a control proving the fixture really does vary them independently, plus an AST guard that no service-state block can be rendered without a retry handler.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — the documented Discovery concurrency cap was not applied to the browse path at all.** Codex review of wave 9, HIGH. `docs/specs/discovery-budgets.md` §2 documents "max simultaneous DiscoveryService queries in flight = 4", and `_acquire_heavy_slot` was entered ONLY when `heavy=True` — which no browse caller passed. So the connections-panel path (three concurrent reads per cold load plus a fourth) dispatched unbounded: `run_in_executor` threads are not cancellable, so a timed-out read keeps its threadpool worker and a retry burst could sustain a backlog on the single uvicorn worker instead of failing fast as `busy`. Applying the cap of 4 was considered and REJECTED — it would put the second simultaneous browse visitor into `busy`, shipping an outage in the name of bounding one. There are now two budgets and **every** executor crossing takes one: `heavy` (4, unchanged) and `browse` (**new `DISCOVERY_MAX_CONCURRENT_BROWSE_QUERIES`, default 24**); a cache hit still takes none. `busy` consequently stops being a status only an injection could produce — two new tests drive the real gate end to end. **Owed:** a matching row in `docs/specs/discovery-budgets.md` §2/§3; that file was owned by a concurrently-running plan and was deliberately not edited. The knob is documented in `CLAUDE.md`, which names the debt.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — the discovery honesty gate missed a percentage spelled as a word, and a rate rounded to one decimal place.** Codex review of wave 9, HIGH; this gate enforces the non-negotiable "no precision percentages, intervals or accuracy rates on any user-facing surface" constraint, so a hole in it is serious. (1) `accuracy is 91 percent` passed **all six** detectors — `_PERCENT_RE` requires the `%` glyph and a bare integer is not a rate-shaped quantity, so the proximity rule had nothing to sit beside either. Both spellings now flow from one shared authority, arming the percentage check, the accuracy detector and the D-06a words-only test together; a NUMBER is required before the word in both languages, so the shipped methods page's own promise ("never as a percentage or an interval" / "לא כאחוז או כטווח") does not read as one. (2) The numeric envelope rule accepted `{"quality": 0.9}` and `{"score": 0.9}` because it demanded more than one significant decimal place; it now flags any fractional float in [0, 1] (integral `0.0`/`1.0` excluded — a counter arriving as a float is their realistic source). Both pre-existing boundaries still hold and are still asserted: the decimal pattern keeps its explicit integer part (without it `MS Heb c.57` reads as the rate `.57`) and takes no lookbehind (without that `accuracy score0.9` escapes).
  - **`w001151` — `יהושע הנגיד, אלמסאיל (תשובות)`, author `Moses b. Maimon, Rambam`, 9 claims. Probable mis-attribution.** The title names **Joshua ha-Nagid** (Nagid of Egypt, d. 1355, Maimonides' great-great-grandson); the author column names **Maimonides**. Not resolvable from the catalogue: the 8 manuscripts carrying this work are mixed-content Genizah fragments, and one of them (`990001231840205171`) carries `דלאלה אלחאירין` — the *Guide for the Perplexed* — i.e. exactly the manuscript-level adjacency that produces this class of error. **Someone who knows the material should decide whether this is Joshua ha-Nagid's own responsa or a Maimonidean recension.** The domain is unaffected either way (both sit in `Responsa and Halakhic Decisions / Responsa- Rishonim and Aharonim`).
  - **`w000065` — `יהודה ראש הסדר, ספר השנים`, author `Judah ha-Kohen`.** Probably CORRECT on identity — Judah ha-Kohen Rosh ha-Seder is one person — but still off-norm in format.
  - **The format problem is separate from the attribution problem and bites regardless.** `author` is a facet on the findings page (136-16/136-18). `Moses b. Maimon, Rambam` does not string-match `משה בן מימון (רמב"ם)`, which carries **29 canonical works and 17,867 claims** — so Maimonides currently renders as **two distinct author entries**, the big one and a singleton. Check whether 136-09's author alias map (`scripts/curate_work_domains.py::resolve_author_alias`, which bridges discovery author strings to FJMS `genizah_persons` ids) already folds the Latin form; if it does not, it should, because that map is what the facet cascade resolves through.
  **Scope is smaller than the raw 181 suggests — split by visibility before starting.** Of the 181 private-artifact works, **123 are M-source with `identity_visibility='private'`**, so VIS-01 excludes them from the public artifact entirely; they affect only the private verifier run. **The public release gate needs 58 works curated** — 56 Sefaria + 2 JA, 370 claims between them, no restricted titles among them. All 181 are review-only (zero carry shipped or human-confirmed evidence), which is consistent with their being reachable only through the opt-in read.
  Worklists generated 2026-08-04 into gitignored `_tmp/` (UTF-8 BOM, opens clean in Excel), with an empty `genre_to_assign` column to fill as `'{parent} / {leaf}'` or `Unassigned`:
  - `_tmp/genre-curation-public-58.csv` — the 58 that gate the public release. Safe to share.
  - `_tmp/genre-curation-restricted.PRIVATE.csv` — the 123 M-source works. **Titles are masked content: never commit, never paste into a shared channel.**
  Once filled, feed them through the 136-09 curated-artifact path (`scripts/build_discovery_sidecar.py::apply_work_genres` assigns at the `canonical_work_id` grain and re-pins the content hash), then re-bake. Note `apply_work_genres` writes only works matched in the curated artifact — that omission is the root cause here, so confirm the new artifact covers every claim-bearing work rather than assuming it does.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-04) — `/computed-identifications` was shaped like a draft, and its main facet was English on a Hebrew page.** Owner verdict after screenshotting it beside `/catalog-browse`: *"not shaped at all… A bunch of headlines, then long domains list, then dropdown of works."* Three changes, no CSS added (`web/static/common.css` untouched; two tests assert it) and no wording changed. **(1) Layout:** the filter bar is now `/catalog-browse`'s left column of white cards — one titled card per filter, the same `text-sm font-bold uppercase tracking-wide` header treatment (pinned to that page's source at test time), with the results region beside it; the domain facet is a two-level tree with facet **counts** and a parent→child collapse instead of a flat unstyled stack. The collapse is a plain visibility toggle with a vertical chevron rather than a `q-expansion-item`, because an expansion swallows its children's clicks — `/catalog-browse` hangs its own domain-select handler on the expansion and has exactly that hazard. **(2) Prose demoted, not deleted:** the recall disclaimer, the candidacy sub-line, the checked-sources/as-of help and the two-bucket rule sentence moved verbatim into a collapsed *"How to read this page"* panel (`expanded=false`); the permanent caveat and ruling U's headline stay visible, the headline now in one bounded block instead of seven stacked lines. The ruling-T "more matches" control stayed a first-class, non-disclosed control. **(3) i18n:** `works.genre` is 100% English, so a Hebrew reader got an English filter list; new `web/discovery_genre_labels.py` maps each part of the stored `Parent / Leaf` string to FJMS `DomainHeb` / `ParentDomainHeb` at **display time** (new cached `FjmsService.get_domain_translations`, primed once per process off the event loop through `web/bounded_io.py`, fail-open to English, never blank). The stored VALUE is untouched, so a domain chosen in Hebrew filters and persists exactly as the same domain chosen in English. Live coverage measured 38/38 public and 61/61 rebuild genre strings, and all 188 live domain names pass the six-detector honesty gate in both languages. Deliberately NOT `get_domain_hierarchy()` — it nests and de-duplicates for the browse tree and yields only 126 of 188 names, which would have left a quarter of the facet silently English. Tests: `tests/test_discovery_genre_labels.py` (16 new) + a new section in `tests/test_findings_page.py` + one in the findings render-smoke suite; each control watched fail against a deliberate mutation. **Not deployed.**
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-05) — the findings page had NO automated masking gate at all, and neither surface had ever had its click-painted output scanned.** Plan 136-19, the cross-surface sweep (`tests/render_smoke/test_discovery_masking_sweep.py`, 31 tests). D-25 was being checked per surface and per rendered page; a leak does not care which door it uses. Three real gaps closed. **(1)** 136-18 wrote `capture_rendered_output` for the findings page and ran the scan **by hand** — nothing called it, so on a clean checkout the findings surface had no masking gate that could run itself. This is its first caller. **(2)** Neither surface's CLICK handlers had ever been driven for masking, and the findings page's unit/sort controls are `ui.select`s whose handler is a CHANGE handler, so a click sweep alone would still have missed them: the domain facet tree and its leaves, the two selects, the pager, the bucket control and the candidacy switch all paint artifact-derived text and **not one of them had ever been in a masking capture**. Rendered coverage is now checked at LINE granularity, derived from what each function CALLS (`ui.*`, transitively) with five exemptions that each paint nothing. **(3)** Two egress classes had never been scanned by anything: JSON payloads as an enumerated set (the read list is DERIVED by parsing every `web/` module that imports `web.discovery`, so an unscanned new read fails by name — it found two on its first run and they are recorded as out of scope rather than dropped), and the copy path (neither surface has a clipboard or download API — that absence is now derived and asserted, and the one thing a reader can copy, the shelfmark link target, is scanned). Four positive controls, one per class, each seeded from the pattern file at test time and each asserting WHICH file the scan named. An unset `MASKING_SCAN_PATTERNS_FILE` FAILS the sweep and blocks readiness — never a skip, never a pass. Thirteen mutations run, all observed red by name, then reverted.
- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-05) — the masking sweep leaked the restricted pattern into its own test log, and it was found by the sweep's own mutation run rather than by review.** `assert needle in text` is rewritten by pytest to print BOTH operands, so the first positive control to go red published the restricted corpus name — and ~2 MB of captured surface — into the pytest output. Nothing about the line looks wrong; the fix therefore had to be structural, not careful. Membership now goes through a helper returning a bool (`assert survived` prints `False`), and `test_no_assertion_in_this_module_can_ECHO_the_needle` walks the module's AST and refuses any `assert` whose test expression references a needle-bearing name. **Worth generalising:** any test that asserts a secret is or is not present in a haystack has this shape.

## 1. Outstanding Bugs

| **Password-reset link is a dead end — clicking the Supabase recovery email link (from the desktop "Forgot password?" flow) redirects to the plain homepage with no set-new-password UI; web had NO recovery entry point or consumer at all** | `web/main.py` (`dashboard_page`, `_complete_password_recovery_session`, `_handle_recovery_payload`, `_render_password_recovery_handler`), `web/supabase_client.py` (`request_password_reset`), `web/auth_state.py` (`create_forgot_password_dialog`), `genizah_translations.py`, `supabase_corrections_client.py` (desktop, unchanged) | ✅ Fixed (2026-07-28) | `/gsd:debug` session `password-reset-link-dead-end` (`.planning/debug/resolved/password-reset-link-dead-end.md`). **Root cause (two stacked defects, either alone would break the flow):** (1) `reset_password_for_email` (desktop `supabase_corrections_client.py` — the only pre-existing caller) never registers a PKCE `code_challenge` and passes no `redirect_to`, so Supabase always issues an **implicit-flow** confirmation link that 302s to the bare Site URL (confirmed via a 2026-07-28 dashboard read-back: Site URL = `https://genizahsearch.com`; Redirect URLs allowlist has only `/auth/callback` variants — a `redirect_to=/reset-password` would have been silently downgraded to Site URL anyway, so that "easy" fix was a trap) with session tokens in the URL **fragment** (`#access_token=...&refresh_token=...&type=recovery`), or an error fragment (`#error=access_denied&error_code=otp_expired&...`) for an expired/consumed/invalid token. (2) NiceGUI is server-rendered and never sees URL fragments; no page anywhere in `web/` consumed one — `set_session_from_url()` existed in `web/supabase_client.py` (allowlisted by the Phase-90 D-15 AST guard) but had ZERO callers, showing the consumer was anticipated but never built. **Fix (Path A, web-only, owner-approved; Path B — a dashboard email-template edit to `token_hash`/`verify_otp` — explicitly DEFERRED, see the follow-up row below):** a client-side JS interceptor (`_RECOVERY_FRAGMENT_INTERCEPT_SCRIPT`) is emitted as the FIRST inline `<script>` in `dashboard_page()`'s `<head>` (before `ANALYTICS_SCRIPT`/`POSTHOG_SCRIPT`, which are async/deferred) — it reads `window.location.hash`, classifies it (recovery vs. error vs. ordinary load), scrubs it via `history.replaceState` SYNCHRONOUSLY (so GA/PostHog never observe the tokens in `location.href`, and session-replay — `maskAllInputs: true` already masks the new password fields — never sees them either), and stashes the classified payload in a transient `window.__genizahRecovery` global. Python reads it exactly once via `ui.run_javascript` (the existing NiceGUI websocket channel, not a new HTTP request or query string) and deletes it. `_complete_password_recovery_session()` (factored out mirroring the Phase-91 AUTHW-02 `_oauth_complete_login` pattern) calls the pre-existing `set_session_from_url()` and persists the session through the `safe_storage` chokepoint ONLY (`tests/test_no_raw_storage_access.py` allowlist stays `[]`); `_handle_recovery_payload()` dispatches to either a "Set a new password" dialog (submits via the existing `change_password()` REST helper) or a "This link has expired or was already used" dialog with a path back to a fresh reset request — the expired/consumed-token case is treated as a REQUIRED path, not an edge case, since recovery tokens are single-use and the reporter had "tried several times." The web app also gained its first-ever recovery entry point: a "Forgot password?" link in the login dialog (`web/auth_state.py::create_forgot_password_dialog`) calling a new `web/supabase_client.py::request_password_reset(email)` — deliberately sent with **no `redirect_to`** (same as the desktop caller) so both apps' recovery emails land on the exact same tested consumer path. 6 new bilingual `tr()` keys (`genizah_translations.py`); 17 new tests in `tests/test_password_reset_recovery.py` (no-redirect_to assertion, happy-path session establishment + storage persistence, the error-fragment/expired-token dispatch branch, safe_storage prune-race + defensive-rollback resilience mirroring `test_auth_callback_resilience.py`, translation-key presence in both languages, embedded-JS structural regression guards). No env var added; no DB/index change; no desktop code change (works with the recovery email the current shipped desktop build already sends). **Post-fix refinement (perf):** the three recovery dialogs are built LAZILY inside a memoized `_build_dialogs()` closure entered via a captured hidden anchor `ui.element` (the `content_container` pattern from `web/pages/browse.py::update_content`, including its `is_deleted`/`_deleted` teardown guard) — `/` is the highest-traffic route and is hammered by crawlers, and this site has a documented bot-driven RSS history (2026-07-08 allocator-ratchet remediation), so an ordinary homepage load now costs one hidden `<div>` + one websocket JS round-trip instead of three fully-built dialogs. Pinned by an AST guard (`test_recovery_dialogs_are_built_lazily_not_on_every_homepage_load`). **External Codex code review (2026-07-28) returned REJECT and caught 1 BLOCKER + 1 HIGH + 2 MEDIUM — all four now fixed or logged:** (BLOCKER-1, confirmed independently) `handle_set_password` called `change_password` via `run.io_bound` → `loop.run_in_executor`, which does NOT propagate contextvars; `nicegui.storage.Storage.user` reads `request_contextvar` and raises "can only be used within a UI context", which `safe_user_get` deliberately degrades to `{}` (its own docstring names `run.io_bound` explicitly) — so `change_password` found no token and returned `{'error': 'Not logged in'}` on EVERY attempt, i.e. the dialog would have appeared correctly and then never worked, burning a single-use link per retry. `web/pages/profile.py:153` calls `change_password` DIRECTLY on the event-loop thread, which is why the pre-existing profile flow was unaffected. Fixed by adding an optional `access_token` parameter to `change_password` (defaulting to `None`, so profile.py is byte-for-byte unchanged) and reading `auth_session` on the event-loop thread before dispatching to the worker. (HIGH-2) the JS read-and-delete destroyed the only browser copy of the payload before the server acknowledged it, so a reload / websocket drop / >5s `run_javascript` timeout resurrected the original silent dead end with the email link already spent — fixed with a durable non-secret `password_recovery_pending` boolean written via `safe_user_set` once the session is established; any later `/` render re-offers the set-password dialog (no token exchange needed, the session already exists), cleared on success or an explicit user Cancel so it cannot nag forever. (MEDIUM-4) raw Supabase/httpx error strings were assigned straight to UI labels — English-only (leaking into the Hebrew UI) and exposing backend operational detail on an anonymous form; now mapped to the existing translated `Failed to send reset email` / `Failed to change password` keys with the detail logged instead. (MEDIUM-3 → logged as a follow-up, not fixed) the anonymous reset-email action has no app-side rate limit. Codex independently CONFIRMED the head-ordering security claim (fragments are never sent in HTTP requests or referrers and replay does not record JS heap globals), the Phase-87 `safe_storage` chokepoint, the unchanged Phase-90 D-15 guard, and that the lazy-build AST guard has real teeth. 26 tests total in `tests/test_password_reset_recovery.py` (Group F covers the review regressions, including the event-handler↔storage boundary the pure-helper tests structurally could not reach); `ruff` clean. **Needs a live-site smoke test after deploy** (cannot be verified headless — see the debug session's final entry for exact steps: request a fresh reset, click the link, confirm the set-password dialog appears AND the password actually changes and logs in; then deliberately reuse/expire a link and confirm the "expired or already used" dialog + fresh-link path; also reload mid-exchange to exercise the `password_recovery_pending` resume path, and check GA/PostHog `$current_url` + a replay for any fragment leakage). |

| **Phase 97.1: MyLibrary UI freezes after Cancel→Discard on huge folders; thousands of `scan_all: stat failed: [WinError 3]` for Hebrew-named PDFs in Dropbox** | `shared/local_sys_id.py:_canonical_filepath`, `shared/local_indexer.py:_iterate_supported_files`, `desktop/my_library_tab.py:_on_cancel_clicked` | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-26) | Three compounding bugs surfaced immediately on first real-world LOCAL scan of a 100K-file Dropbox tree (paths > 260 chars, many failed with `[WinError 3] The system cannot find the path specified`). FIX-1 — `_canonical_filepath` now prepends `\\?\` for Windows absolute paths > 248 chars (`_WIN_LONG_PATH_THRESHOLD` + `_WIN_LONG_PATH_PREFIX`); without the prefix, `os.stat`/`GetFileAttributesEx` hard-failed for files that `os.walk`/`FindFirstFile` enumerated fine. FIX-2b — `_iterate_supported_files` now checks `cancel_check()` per-file inside the inner `for fname in files:` loop (was per-directory only). | **Phase 90 regression: ~13 reader functions in `web/supabase_client.py` use `get_client()` (anonymous singleton) instead of `get_user_client()`; RLS-`TO authenticated` SELECT policies return 0 rows for logged-in users** | `web/supabase_client.py` (lines 711, 752, 783, 899, 958, 1004, 1067, 1171, 1191, 1243, 1326, 1461, 1579), `web/components/add_to_list_dialog.py` (secondary UI-context error in post-create path) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-18, Phase 92.1 + SWEEP-05 smoke run 2 PASS) | **Code shipped by Phase 92.1 (2026-05-17 plan / 2026-05-17 code-ship)** — 12 reader call sites migrated to `get_user_client()` (6 KEEP sites annotated per Reviews M2: lines 490/502/711/721/1326/1579; the 1579 `get_feed_items` KEEP rationale strengthened with explicit RLS evidence per Reviews R2-1 Option C — verified `docs/guides/SUPABASE_GUIDE.md:498-502` + `scripts/fix_rls_policies.sql:60-95` show the ONLY SELECT policy on discoveries is `TO public USING (is_hidden=false)`, NO admin SELECT branch exists). AST scanner `tests/test_no_anonymous_reads_on_authenticated_tables.py` (BANNED_TABLES = {user_lists, list_items, recent_items, projects}; 6 seed traps + positive control + exempt-files self-check + partial-auth-table test + 2 xfail-strict blind-spot tests per Reviews M scanner) + behavioral tests `tests/test_supabase_client_reader_rls.py` (5 parametrized readers + 1 anonymous-fallback negative control with Reviews L1 `get_client_mock.called is True` assertion) + Reviews M scanner blind-spot xfails + SUPABASE_GUIDE.md BANNED_TABLES extension protocol installed as permanent CI guards. Symptom-3 (UI-context error in add-to-list-dialog) diagnosed via instrumented 3-point `request_contextvar` capture per round-2 Reviews R2-2 — **NO-REPRO branch authorized**: Hillel's 2026-05-17 09:01-09:21 UTC reproduction yielded all three required evidence pieces (3 successful list creations `92.1-diag-1`/`92.1-norepro-2`/`92.1-norepro-3` persisted to Supabase `user_lists` rows 279/280/281 with `user_id=f8371f69-b7b8-49e9-bbd9-d79dfeecd7b8`; ZERO `safe_user_get('auth_session') unexpected failure` WARNINGs in server log; contextvar bound stably across P1/P2/P3/P3.5 with same `id()`). Symptom 3 was eliminated as a side-effect of Plan 92.1-01's reader migration. Revision Blocker 2 mandatory `_create_and_add_handler` module-level helper refactor + 5-test regression suite `tests/test_add_to_list_dialog_ui_context.py` installed per Reviews H1 + Reviews R2-3 (exercises REAL `lists_mgr.create_list → get_user_client → safe_user_get('auth_session')` chain AND directly controls `nicegui.storage.request_contextvar` via `.set(None)` vs `.set(fake_request)`). Case-driven binding fix (cases a/b/c/d per Reviews R2-2) correctly SKIPPED — speculative `copy_context()` wrap or retry/wrap fallback would constitute Rule 4 architectural change without evidence AND could mask future regressions by hiding the precise Symptom-3 WARNING signal under fallback logic. `web/supabase_client.py` UNCHANGED in Plan 92.1-02. **Verification pending:** SWEEP-05 smoke run 2 owned by Hillel — when smoke PASS is committed, this entry flips to ✅ Fixed (date) and the counts decrement (P1 2→1, Total Open 34→33). Plan 92-02 v7.12 closeout still blocked behind smoke run 2 PASS commit. See `.planning/phases/92.1-reader-client-retrofit/92.1-SUMMARY.md`. **Original incident (preserved for context):** Surfaced 2026-05-17 during Phase 92 SWEEP-05 smoke run 1. Symptoms: (1) logged-in users see no lists on `/lists` (existing lists not fetched); (2) "new list" from `/lists` page silently fails (toast says added but nothing persists, no console log); (3) "new list" from search-results → Add to list → Create new list emits console error `safe_user_get('auth_session') unexpected failure: app.storage.user can only be used within a UI context` and the list is not created. **Root cause:** Phase 90 (Plan 90-01) D-09 / D-10 "singleton-anonymous-only" invariant routed all five bootstrap helpers (`sign_in` / `sign_up` / `set_session_from_url` / `exchange_code_for_session` / `get_oauth_url`) through throwaway clients to close the multitenant SIGNED_IN-event-listener leak. Correct security fix in isolation -- but Phase 90 did NOT migrate the ~13 reader functions in `web/supabase_client.py` that still use `get_client()` instead of `get_user_client()`. Before Phase 90 those reads worked because the SIGNED_IN listener auto-authenticated the singleton after login. After Phase 90, `get_client()` stays anonymous, and `user_lists` / `user_projects` / `user_list_items` / `recent_items` SELECT RLS policies (all `TO authenticated USING (auth.uid()=user_id)` per `docs/guides/SUPABASE_GUIDE.md:430-451`) filter all rows out for the anon role. **Smoking gun in planning artifact:** `.planning/phases/90-auth-caching-rewrite-no-set-session/90-DISCUSSION-LOG.md:147` records the false assumption "`get_user_lists` reads work anonymously; only writes need auth". That assumption ignored the `TO authenticated` policy. **Note `get_profile` (line 711) is masked:** the `profiles` SELECT policy is `TO public USING (true)`, so anonymous reads succeed there -- which is why login itself appears to work end-to-end (`do_login` fetches the profile, calls `set_auth`). Only the user-scoped tables (lists, projects, items, recent items, deleted lists, list-items etc.) are broken. **Why no gate caught this:** Phase 87 AST lint scanner only checks raw `app.storage.user` access (not RLS reachability); Phase 92-01 SWEEP-01 AST scan correctly verified zero violations of the same invariant; Phase 90 tests likely mocked Supabase or exercised write paths only; no live cross-user smoke test was run between Phase 90 ship (2026-05-14) and Phase 92-01 closeout (2026-05-17). **Secondary symptom (the UI-context console error):** likely a context-loss in `web/components/add_to_list_dialog.py` post-create UI re-render path -- requires separate trace; primary regression fix may or may not resolve it. **Impact:** v7.12 Path B milestone NOT shippable; `deploy.sh` stays blocked; Plan 92-02 (closeout docs) gated by Plan 92-01's SWEEP-05 smoke = FAIL. **Fix path:** Insert Phase 92.1 (decided 2026-05-17 with user; pending `/gsd-insert-phase 92.5`) to (a) migrate ~13 reader functions from `get_client()` to `get_user_client()`, (b) audit-and-test that authenticated reads RLS-pass for the logged-in user, (c) trace and fix the secondary UI-context error, (d) install a regression test that mocks an authenticated session and asserts SELECT-RLS reachability against an anon-singleton baseline, (e) redo SWEEP-05 (smoke run 2) and verify PASS before Plan 92-02 ships. |
