| **Web server memory leak: process grows unbounded under sustained load** | `web/main.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/browse.py`, `web/export_state.py`, `web/export_service.py`, `web/storage_diagnostics.py`, NiceGUI `app.storage.user`, image/manifest caches | ✅ Resolved — definitively attributed & remediated (2026-07-08): allocator-arena high-water (dead-but-resident, NOT live objects) driven to the MemoryHigh cap in ~12h by SEO-crawler load; Tier-1 ops (bot block, MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2, 8G swap, cache caps, daily restart) + Tier-2 code (malloc_trim loop, SEO-bot policy in app, storage retention) deployed — see 2026-07-08 addendum in Notes. Previously: operationally bounded (2026-06-08): NOT a runaway leak. Live re-attribution on the prod process refuted the 2026-05-19 ObservableDict-wrapping verdict (storage payloads total 39 KB; ObservableDict count flat/down at 37K) and the `PROPOSED-FIX.md` JSON-string change was never shipped and would not help. Two tracemalloc windows + a daytime-peak systemd check show RSS PLATEAUS (~12–13.5G on a 15.4G box) — front-loaded warmup, not linear retention. The one genuinely unbounded surface, `MetadataManager.nli_cache`, is now LRU-bounded (commit `592c984e`, ships next web deploy). Safety net LIVE on prod 2026-06-08: systemd `MemoryHigh=13800M`/`MemoryMax=14500M` + a twice-weekly Sun/Wed 00:00 UTC low-traffic restart. | **2026-07-08 ADDENDUM — DEFINITIVE ATTRIBUTION + REMEDIATION (the 2026-06-08 mechanism verdict below stands; its cap-sizing assumptions are superseded).** Fresh process (benign unattended-upgrade restart 06:02 UTC) hit the MemoryHigh cap 13.4G in <13h — June took 3 days. Live probes on PID 1801084: cgroup memory.stat anon 13.17G vs file 1.25G (kernel squeezed Tantivy page cache 3.5G→1.0G; memory.events high=7,079 breaches in 12h); smaps: main [heap] only 510 MB, ~12G across ~300 anon mmap regions of 8–128MB (allocator-arena signature); DECISIVE tracemalloc-UNDER-LOAD (the experiment June never ran — both its windows were quiet-traffic): RssAnon +18.2MB in an exact 3-min traced window while live Python allocations grew only ~4MB ⇒ the bulk is dead-but-resident pymalloc/glibc arena high-water from per-request transient churn (browse renders, IIIF manifest JSON, search full_text hydration), NOT live retention — all bounded caches honoring caps (nli_cache 37.8K/75K, manifests 3.5K/5K, storage payloads 72KB total, objgraph lists 2.0M ≈ June's 1.88M). **Workload driver: SemrushBot** — ~7K req/day at a constant ~280/hr, 24/7 for ≥6 days, 41% of all traffic, walking /browse?sys_id= through the ~255K-manuscript sitemap (robots.txt was Allow:/ for it); plus bursty human search traffic (thumbnail loads). NOT the public Search API (PostHog: 0 search_api_request Jul 6–8) and NOT Claude sessions (ClaudeBot ~20–40 req/day on robots/sitemap only; python-requests 1–2/day). June's "MemoryHigh above the plateau" sizing is INVALID under crawl load — the process pegged the ceiling within hours of every restart on a zero-swap box ~1.4G from cgroup OOM. **Tier-1 OPS (live 2026-07-08 19:24 UTC):** nginx UA map+403 for SEO-tool crawlers (SemrushBot/AhrefsBot/MJ12bot/DotBot/BLEXBot/DataForSeoBot/serpstatbot — Googlebot/bingbot/ClaudeBot unaffected) + static robots.txt Disallow group (conf: /etc/nginx/conf.d/seo_bot_block_map.conf + snippets/genizah_seo_bot_block.conf; vhost backup genizah.bak-20260708); systemd drop-in 20-malloc-tuning.conf `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` (31 threads on 4 cores allowed up to 32 arenas); 8G swapfile + fstab (swappiness kept at 60 deliberately — dead anon should swap before hot Tantivy file pages evict); prod .env NLI_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=20000 + IIIF_MANIFEST_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=1500; restart timer daily 00:00 UTC via drop-in override (revert to Sun/Wed once RSS proves stable between restarts); service restarted 13.9G→1.67G baseline. **Tier-2 CODE (Codex pre-flight review: APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES ×4; brief _tmp/codex-tier2-memory-plan.md, critique _tmp/codex-tier2-critique-2026-07-08.md):** (1) web/malloc_trim.py — periodic glibc malloc_trim(0) daemon thread started only from app.on_startup (GENIZAH_MALLOC_TRIM_SECONDS default 300, 0 disables; adaptive GENIZAH_MALLOC_TRIM_MIN_GROWTH_MB default 64 — trims only when RssAnon grew that much since the last trim), stats (runs/skips/duration/freed) surfaced in /_internal/memstat via the runtime-cache registry (new public alias web/api.py::register_runtime_cache_stats); caveat: does NOT reclaim partially-occupied pymalloc arenas or non-glibc native allocations — the daily restart stays the backstop until 48h of data proves the plateau is gone. (2) SEO-bot policy in app code: web/crawler_visibility.py SEO_TOOL_USER_AGENT_TOKENS + SEO_TOOL_ROBOTS_AGENTS + is_seo_tool_crawler/should_block_seo_tool_request; 403 branch at the top of the noindex middleware in web/main.py with /robots.txt EXEMPT (RFC 9309: a 4xx robots.txt means "allow all" — crawlers must be able to read their Disallow group to deregister); robots.txt generation in web/api.py emits the group; nginx remains the PRIMARY enforcement (app 403 is defense-in-depth). (3) .nicegui storage-user file retention: GENIZAH_STORAGE_RETENTION_DAYS (default 90, 0 disables) wired into the startup compaction pass (web/export_state.py::compact_nicegui_export_storage — delete-before-parse, loaded-session guard, files_deleted/bytes_deleted counters); NiceGUI refreshes mtime on session LOAD so mtime = last visit; NOTE deleting a file clears that browser's auth_session → users absent longer than the window are logged out on return (Supabase-side data unaffected); 53,592 files / 260MB at ship time. Tests: tests/test_seo_bot_block.py + tests/test_malloc_trim.py + tests/test_storage_retention.py (52 new) + existing test_crawler_visibility/test_export_state_cap/test_openapi_scope regressions green; ruff clean. **Post-deploy steps:** verify the app-served robots.txt contains the SEO group, then remove the nginx robots.txt alias override and move the nginx `if` from server context into `location /` (so the exact-match robots.txt location is exempt at nginx too). Watch /_internal/memstat malloc_trim stats + RssAnon trend for 48h. **2026-06-08 UPDATE — RE-ATTRIBUTED on the live process; NOT a runaway leak; P1 closed; safety net deployed (supersedes the framing below).** Trigger: "still leaking" — systemd showed 11.2G@3d → 12.2G@5d. Live attribution via `/_internal/memstat` + `/_internal/objgraph` + `/_internal/tracemalloc` (prod PID 3418627, MEMSTAT_SECRET, port 8081); Codex (gpt-5.5, xhigh) premise-challenged the proposed JSON-string fix and was right. **(1) ObservableDict-wrapping verdict REFUTED:** live `app.storage.user` payloads total only **39 KB** across all sessions (SEED-002 + cap worked); ObservableDict count **37,277** (DOWN from ~44K, flat). `PROPOSED-FIX.md` JSON-string change was NEVER shipped and would not help. **(2) Plateau, not runaway:** two tracemalloc windows (9 min + 6.6 h, same PID, no restart) both show RSS essentially flat (<1 MB/hr); the 2026-06-08 daytime-peak systemd check shows the growth rate fell ~6× (≈130 MB/hr days 0-3 → ≈21 MB/hr days 3-5) while the level barely moved (11.2→12.2G). The 1.78→13.5G rise was front-loaded warmup that PLATEAUED, not linear retention. (objgraph is blind to str/bytes, but tracemalloc — which sees bytes — confirmed flat.) **(3) nli_cache bounded:** `MetadataManager.nli_cache` (`genizah_core.py`) was the one genuinely unbounded surface (plain dict, 50.9K→52.6K entries, no eviction); replaced with a thread-safe `_BoundedLRUCache` (env `NLI_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` default 75000; pickles as plain dict for desktop/older-build compat) — commit `592c984e` on master-main + 10 tests (`tests/test_nli_cache_bounded_lru.py`); hygiene, ships next web deploy (tracemalloc confirms it is NOT the multi-GB driver). **(4) Safety net LIVE on prod 2026-06-08:** box is 15.4G **NO-SWAP**, cgroup already ~12.2G, so `/etc/systemd/system/genizah-web.service.d/10-memory-cap.conf` sets `MemoryHigh=13800M`/`MemoryMax=14500M` (above the plateau so it won't evict the mmap'd index page-cache, below the ceiling with ~0.9G left for the OS; a Max breach is cgroup-scoped + `Restart=always` auto-recovers in 5s, instead of the box-wide OOM killer with no swap) + `genizah-web-restart.{service,timer}` doing a twice-weekly Sun/Wed 00:00 UTC (03:00 Israel, low-traffic) restart that resets the plateau to ~1.8G baseline before it nears the ceiling. Applied via `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restarting (MainPID 3418627 unchanged). Caveat: tracemalloc windows were off-peak; the 06-08 daytime systemd peak corroborates the plateau. Artifacts: `.planning/quick/260519-hoi-investigate-framework-retention/{TRACEMALLOC-2026-06-06.md, CODEX-CRITIQUE-2026-06-06.md, CODEX-REVIEW-BRIEF-2026-06-06.md}`. **— Historical updates below (pre-2026-06-08 framing, superseded):** **2026-05-19 PM UPDATE — substantially mitigated.** SEED-002 fix shipped (commits `2a7440d6` + `43a0fa0a` + `7633a40e` + `0aa92f82` deployed 2026-05-19 PM). Post-deploy investigation (see `.planning/quick/260519-hoi-investigate-framework-retention/INVESTIGATION.md`): 25 heavy UI searches → RSS 1.13 GB → 8.7 GB (~200 MB/search); 20 heavy API calls of the same Tantivy work → RSS +34 MB total (1.7 MB/call). 117× difference attributes the residual leak to **NiceGUI ObservableDict wrapping work on `safe_user_set` (UI path only, not API)** — the act of wrapping 5000 row dicts in ObservableDicts pressures pymalloc, which never returns pages to the OS. Backref tracing confirmed: all 44K ObservableDicts are anchored at `storage._users` or `storage._tabs` (live working set, not orphans). **Verdict:** SEED-002 achieved its scope; residual is allocator behavior + framework wrapping cost, not retention. Closes the attribution loop. Future-phase fix in `PROPOSED-FIX.md`: JSON-string storage bypasses ObservableDict wrapping entirely. P1 stays open until that fix ships AND verifies plateau under heavy-search soak — but the leak is now **characterized** and **operationally bounded** by periodic restart. **FIELD-STRIP FIX IN CODE 2026-05-19** -- commit `ed6f89c4` ships the missing half of the 2026-05-18 cap fix: row-level stripping of `full_text` / `raw_file_hl` / `content` at write time in `web/export_state.py` (capping by count alone could not bound multi-MB transcripts × few rows -- 11h post-deploy soak measured 411 MB/hr growth, worse than the 300 MB/hr pre-fix baseline). Lazy Tantivy rehydration via `searcher.get_full_text_by_id(uid)` in `web/export_service.py:_resolve_result_full_text` keeps Excel downloads complete without parking transcripts in session storage. `app.on_startup` hook `compact_export_storage_on_startup` in `web/main.py` rewrites legacy oversized `.nicegui/storage-user-*.json` files via `tmp + os.replace`. `web/storage_diagnostics.py` now reports `top_keys` per user payload so post-deploy we can observe whether `export_search_payload` / `export_parallels_payload` are still dominant. 12 files / 546 insertions / 21 deletions; full pytest suite 2051 passed / 20 skipped / 2 xfailed / 0 failures (3m 56s); ruff clean. Authored by Codex CLI; applied to master-main via `git apply` after Claude verified test coverage. **Verdict gate (awaiting):** pull `/_internal/memstat` after >=2h uptime under real traffic; success criteria = (a) `export_search_payload` / `export_parallels_payload` no longer appear as dominant `top_keys`, AND (b) RSS growth rate < 30 MB/hr per the verdict thresholds at `.planning/todos/done/2026-05-18-verify-memstat-after-export-cap-fix.md`. If both pass, flip status to ✅ Fixed (2026-05-19, ed6f89c4) and decrement P1 Open 1->0 / Total Open 34->33. If either fails, the underlying surface enumeration in `.planning/todos/pending/2026-05-19-leak-attribution-phase.md` (NLI/IIIF manifest cache, csv_bank, detached `asyncio.ensure_future`/`ui.timer` callbacks, image-byte buffers on puzzle/visual-similarity, image-adjustment LUTs, Phase 92.2 WeakKeyDictionary task-memo) becomes the next attribution path. **Earlier 2026-05-19 (RE-OPENED):** -- 11-hour post-deploy soak verdict: secondary leak detected. After the 2026-05-18 cap-fix ship (commit f2e456d4, restart at 15:41:13 UTC), systemctl status genizah-web.service at +11h reported Memory 6.3G (peak 6.8G), Tasks 31, PID 2332735. Growth rate = (6.3 GB - 1.78 GB) / 11 h ~= **411 MB/hr** -- deep in the >100 MB/hr secondary-leak band defined by .planning/todos/done/2026-05-18-verify-memstat-after-export-cap-fix.md, and actually WORSE than the pre-fix 300 MB/hr observation that originally triggered the hotfix. **The cap fix is confirmed working for its surface:** post-deploy top live user payload dropped 498 MB -> 512 KB, top file on disk 906 MB -> 4.5 MB, .nicegui/ total 2.0 GB -> 198 MB. So export_search_payload.results is no longer the dominant retention surface -- but another surface is leaking ~411 MB/hr. **Candidate suspect surfaces to investigate** (preserving the list from the original 2026-04-28 entry below, plus one new candidate added 2026-05-19): (1) NLI/IIIF manifest cache -- unbounded module-level dict in shared/nli_crossref_service.py; (2) csv_bank shelfmark variants in genizah_core.py; (3) detached asyncio.ensure_future / ui.timer callbacks holding closure refs to large objects; (4) image-byte buffers on puzzle / visual-similarity paths; (5) per-user puzzle image-adjustment LUTs / canvas state; (6) **NEW 2026-05-19:** Phase 92.2 WeakKeyDictionary task-memo on get_user_client() in web/supabase_client.py keyed by (get_persisted_session_uuid(), access_token) -- should be GC-safe by construction (entries die when the asyncio.Task is GC'd), but worth verifying tasks actually finalize and the memo dict shrinks in real traffic. **Next step:** follow-up scoped at .planning/todos/pending/2026-05-19-leak-attribution-phase.md -- add /_internal/objgraph + /_internal/tracemalloc companion endpoints next to the existing /_internal/memstat (gated by MEMSTAT_SECRET header) to get live attribution data, then audit each candidate surface in order of signal-per-effort. NOT scoped into v7.13 (roadmap locked at Phase 93 + 94); will be inserted as a separate phase once v7.13 ships or as an urgent insertion if growth rate worsens. Mitigation in the meantime: continue manual sudo systemctl restart genizah-web.service when RSS climbs past ~6 GB. **Original 2026-05-18 fix writeup preserved below -- the cap is still valid, just no longer sufficient:** **Root cause identified and fixed 2026-05-18:** `web/export_state.py:set_search_export()` (and `set_parallels_export()`, plus the two `update_*` siblings) wrote the full search/parallels result list verbatim into `app.storage.user['export_search_payload']`. A Hebrew wildcard search returning 31,572 results produced a 498 MB `results` list inside a 864 MB user-storage JSON file; NiceGUI rehydrated this into RAM on every session load, accounting for the ~5 GB Python heap (writable-anon per `pmap -x`). Forensic capture on production process PID 2249648 (uptime 25h, RSS 7.5 GB, growth rate ~300 MB/hr): top 3 user-storage files on disk were 865 MB / 528 MB / 65 MB, all dominated by `export_search_payload.results`; a fresh post-Phase-88 file from 2026-05-18 still held a 24 MB envelope confirming the leak was actively writing, not legacy. **Fix:** `_EXPORT_RESULTS_CAP = 5000` constant + `_cap_results()` helper applied in all four mutators (`set_search_export`, `update_search_export_results`, `set_parallels_export`, `update_parallels_export_filtered`). Worst-case payload now ~80 MB; typical export <5 MB. `truncated` and `total_count` metadata fields added so future UX can surface "showing first 5K of N" hints. Tests: `tests/test_export_state_cap.py` (11 new tests covering all 4 mutators + empty/non-list defenses + constant-bounds sanity). All existing export tests still pass (`test_export_state_selection.py` + `test_export_cross_user_isolation.py` + `test_api_export_json.py` + `test_api_legacy_unchanged.py`, 22 total). Phase 87/88 invariant scanners (`test_no_raw_storage_access.py`, `test_no_appstate_export_fields.py`, `test_no_deleted_state_references.py`, 21 tests) still green. **Deploy:** ship code, restart `genizah-web.service`, then `rm /home/ubuntu/GenizahSearch/.nicegui/storage-user-*.json` files >5 MB (legacy oversized files from pre-fix sessions; NiceGUI recreates empty on next session). **Symptom (2026-04-28):** `genizah-web.service` reached **13.1 GB RSS after 4 days uptime** on v7.9.x (single `python -m web.main` process, PID 632605). This is **worse** than the pre-v7.9.0 baseline of 8.7 GB after 5 days, so the v7.9 page-scoped state refactor (which reduced `app.storage.user` sprawl in search/browse and detached stray `asyncio.ensure_future` retentions) did **not** fix the leak. **Mitigation for now:** manual `sudo systemctl restart genizah-web.service` when memory climbs. **Diagnostic in place:** `/_internal/memstat` endpoint (`web/main.py:125`, gated by `MEMSTAT_SECRET` header) returns `VmRSS / VmData / VmSize / RssAnon / RssFile` from `/proc/self/status` — added 2026-04-19 as the leak-investigation baseline. **Follow-up finding (2026-04-30):** the installed NiceGUI version already schedules built-in `prune_user_storage` and `prune_tab_storage` every 10 seconds, so the issue is no longer framed as “framework never evicts session storage.” The stronger hypothesis is retained clients/background tasks preventing those pruners from reclaiming sessions, with oversized `app.storage.user` payloads amplifying the memory cost while they linger. The active leak-fix worktree now adds three first-pass mitigations: (1) search/parallels persistent history is compacted so heavy result arrays no longer live in `app.storage.user`, while same-tab instant restore moved to `app.storage.tab`; (2) `/_internal/memstat` now reports NiceGUI client/user/tab counts plus top live user payload sizes and top `.nicegui` files; (3) teardown guards were added to several detached loops (`web/main.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `web/components/text_editor.py`, `web/pages/home.py`, `web/pages/puzzle.py`). Keep this issue **open** until a soak test shows RSS no longer grows linearly. **Symptom-of-symptom log spam:** journalctl shows recurring `AssertionError: user storage for {uuid} should be created before accessing it` — a NiceGUI assertion that fires when code touches `app.storage.user` outside a request context. Indicates a background task or stale callback still reaches for user storage after the user has disconnected; same family of issue as the sprawl the v7.9 refactor targeted, just at a different site. **Suspect surfaces (none confirmed yet — no attribution data):** (1) remaining `app.storage.user` callsites that retain references after disconnect (despite v7.9 page-scoped state refactor); (2) per-process caches that grow without bound — `nli_crossref_service` IIIF manifest cache, `csv_bank` shelfmark variants, FJMS enrichment lookups, translation memo, Tantivy reader handles, Supabase client pool; (3) detached `asyncio.ensure_future` / `ui.timer` callbacks still capturing closures with large objects (search.py, browse.py, parallels.py, visual_similarity_dialog.py, puzzle.py); (4) image-bytes buffers retained on the per-user puzzle / visual-similarity paths; (5) image adjustment LUTs or canvas state on the v7.2.0 image-adjustment path. **Approaches to investigate (ordered by signal-per-effort):** (a) **Live attribution** — add a `/_internal/objgraph` companion endpoint next to `/_internal/memstat` that returns `objgraph.show_growth(limit=30)` between two snapshots, and a `/_internal/tracemalloc` endpoint that snapshots+diffs top allocators after a fixed traffic window. Gives concrete class names instead of guesses. (b) **Audit `app.storage.user` writes** — grep all `app.storage.user[...] = ...` and `storage.user.update(...)` callsites; verify no large object (full result list, image bytes, raw FJMS rows) is stored there, and that every page-load handler clears ephemeral keys on disconnect. (c) **Audit detached background tasks** — grep `asyncio.ensure_future(`, `asyncio.create_task(`, and `ui.timer(`; every detached task should hold only weakrefs to UI elements, never closure-capture large dicts/lists. Convert to `run.io_bound()` where possible (already done at some sites in v7.9). (d) **Bound the caches** — replace unbounded module-level `dict` caches in `shared/nli_crossref_service.py`, `shared/fjms_service.py`, `genizah_core.py` (csv_bank) with `functools.lru_cache(maxsize=N)` or `cachetools.LRUCache`; pick maxsize from observed hit-rate data. (e) **Audit Supabase / HTTP clients** — confirm `supabase` and `httpx` clients are reused (not re-instantiated per request) and that response objects don't retain unbounded body refs; check `web/supabase_client.py` and any per-user Supabase session logic. (f) **Audit puzzle / visual-similarity / image-adjustment paths** specifically — they handle large image byte buffers and IIIF canvas objects. (g) **Worst-case fallback** — add a systemd `MemoryHigh=` / `MemoryMax=` limit (or watchdog) so the unit auto-restarts at, say, 6 GB instead of climbing to 13 GB. Band-aid only, not a fix. **Owner / priority:** needs a dedicated GSD phase once current API-port work lands. Do **not** investigate inside the API-port phase. |
| **Deferred renders never ran — `client.has_been_deleted` is not a NiceGUI attribute (web)** | `web/client_guard.py` (new), `web/pages/corrections.py`, `web/pages/home.py`, `web/pages/discoveries.py`, `tests/test_client_guard.py` (new) | ✅ Fixed (2026-07-31, same-day hotfix `b3faedd8`, deployed + owner-confirmed) | **Reported by the owner minutes after the v8.5.2 deploy: `/corrections` stuck on "Loading your edits...".** Nine sites guarded their deferred render with `if container.client.has_been_deleted:`. That attribute **does not exist** on `nicegui.Client` (3.8.0) and never has, so reading it raised `AttributeError` on **every** invocation — the ordinary happy path, not just teardown. Each caller's `except Exception` swallowed it, so the render never ran and the loading spinner stayed up forever: My Edits, My Comments, homepage Recently Viewed and discovery replies all silently empty. The leaderboard was worse — its *error* branch re-read the same dead attribute, so that one escaped as an unhandled traceback. **Two of the nine sites predate the perf work** (`c39838c3`, present at v8.5.1) where they were latent; the 2026-07-30 perf work copied the name onto four main-path loaders and converted a latent bug into total failure. **Fix:** one shared `client_gone()` testing membership in `Client.instances` — the public signal `Client.delete()` actually manipulates (`del Client.instances[self.id]`); there is no public `deleted` property in 3.8.0, only a private `_deleted`. Deliberately **NOT** `has_socket_connection`: that is `False` during initial page construction, before the websocket handshake, so it would skip the first render of every page — the same class of bug in the opposite direction. Fails **open** so a bad guard can never silently blank a working surface. Also added `show_load_error()`: a swallowed failure now replaces the spinner with a visible bilingual notice, closing the "looks slow, is actually broken" presentation that let this reach production. **LESSON — why 601 tests + 9/9 CI were green:** the regression tests asserted the *string* `'has_been_deleted'` appeared in the loader source, i.e. they **pinned the misspelling**; no test ever executed a guard against a real `Client`, and a bare `Mock()` answers to any attribute name (see [[feedback_test_shared_function_callers_before_push]]). A source-text assertion proves a guard is *present*, never that it *works* — pair it with behavioural coverage. `tests/test_client_guard.py` now drives real `Client` objects (13 tests, incl. a fail-loud check that NiceGUI still lacks the attribute) + an AST guard forbidding `.has_been_deleted` across `web/`. |
| **Composition Search printed filter never filtered (desktop) — wrong Qt data role** | `genizah_app.py` (`_comp_data_matches_filters`, the results-tree `visit`/`unhide` walk), `desktop/dialogs_filter.py` (`PreSearchFilterDialog`) | ✅ Fixed (2026-07-31) — merged (#318), shipped in v8.5.2 | Found and fixed via PR #318 (Codex), UAT'd in the desktop app against the FJMS enrichment sidecar. The Composition Search results tree stores the manuscript record in `Qt.ItemDataRole.UserRole` and the preview text in `UserRole + 1`, but the filter walk read `UserRole + 1` as though it held the record — so `data.get('sys_id')` was always empty and **every row looked unidentified/unprinted**. Consequence: the three-state Printed column filter was inert in both directions ("Only printed" hid everything, hiding printed hid nothing), and **"Exclude printed" on a standard Composition Search silently did not constrain the run** — inconsistent with the main Search tab, where the same filter worked. **Fixed:** filtering reads the record role, falls back to the rendered column text for Library / Shelfmark / Title, keeps `UserRole + 1` for the two context columns, and child rows inherit the parent manuscript's title (Title filter) and `sys_id` (printed lookup) so fragments are no longer hidden for lacking their own. **Second defect, same PR:** `PreSearchFilterDialog` (Focus Search) let OK be pressed before its background `FilterCountWorker` finished, passing `result_set=None` — i.e. an **unrestricted** search when a narrow scope had been requested; OK is now disabled while the count runs and a `_count_generation` token discards superseded worker results when filters change quickly. Tests: `tests/test_composition_printed_filters.py` (11, GUI-marked). |
| **Site-wide slowness, cause 1 of 2: synchronous Supabase I/O on the single event loop stalls EVERY concurrent request** | `web/pages/corrections.py`, `web/pages/home.py`, `web/pages/discoveries.py`, `web/components/joins_panel.py`, `web/perf_watch.py` (new) | ✅ Fixed (2026-07-31) — all 5 `await <sync fn>()` sites + the `/corrections` N+1 off-loop, auth-scoped readers take an explicit `client=`; merged (#319), shipped in v8.5.2 | Found while diagnosing "the website is slow" (2026-07-30), and **only after a Codex adversarial review rejected the first diagnosis**, which had wrongly cleared the application ("the app is fast, the network is slow"). uvicorn runs a **SINGLE worker** (`ui.run` in `web/main.py`, no `workers=`) and NiceGUI calls sync page builders and sync event callbacks directly on the event loop, so one blocking Supabase/NLI call stalls every other in-flight request — including unrelated static assets and `/api/*`. Because a process blocked on network I/O burns no CPU, this is **invisible in load average**: prod read `load average: 0.03` with 12.8G free while serving 9-second TTFBs, which is exactly why the first pass mis-attributed it to geography. Two concrete defects: **(1) `/corrections` leaderboard N+1** — `create_leaderboard_view` ran one `profiles` query plus **up to 20 sequential `get_user_corrections_count()` queries**, synchronously, during initial page construction and therefore *before* the HTML was returned. That is why `/corrections` was the slowest route in RUM (p75 LCP 5981ms / FCP 6998ms) — a gap the first pass explicitly punted on. Its `# Batch-fetch` comment was simply wrong. **FIXED:** extracted `fetch_leaderboard_users()`, which fans the counts out concurrently through `run.io_bound` (~2 round trips instead of ~21, none on the loop) behind a spinner. Counts stay exact (per-user `count='exact'`) rather than collapsing into one `in_()` query, which would be subject to PostGREST's default row cap and could silently undercount a prolific author. **(2) awaiting a synchronous function — 5 sites** (found by AST scan; Codex named 2): the body ran to completion *on the loop*, then `await None` raised `TypeError: object NoneType can't be used in 'await' expression`, swallowed by `except Exception: pass`. So the "deferred" loaders were never deferred, AND every genuine load failure was invisible — while the data still appeared (the body had run), a textbook looks-like-it-works trap. **FIXED at all 5** (`corrections.py` ×2, `home.py`, `discoveries.py`, `joins_panel.py`): call directly, log failures instead of discarding them. **`joins_panel` additionally moved off-loop** — safe there because the joins RLS is `USING(true)` (all rows publicly readable) so losing the per-user auth context in a worker cannot change the result. **ALSO FIXED (2026-07-31, post-Codex-review — the four remaining auth-scoped loaders):** `corrections.load_edits`, `corrections.load_comments`, `home.load_recent` and `discoveries.do_load_responses` reach Supabase through `get_user_client()`, which reads `safe_user_get('auth_session')`. `run.io_bound` uses `loop.run_in_executor`, which does **not** propagate contextvars, so in a worker thread that read has no UI context and degrades to **anonymous** — documented in `web/safe_storage.py:58-67`. Given corrections/comments have dual RLS (`TO public` approved + `TO authenticated` own-any-status), a blind `run.io_bound` wrap would have silently dropped the user's own **pending** corrections and **private** comments, and `get_discovery_responses` could have returned zero rows for signed-in users — **silent data loss, not an error**, which is why these were held back from the first pass rather than wrapped. **Resolved properly:** the five user-scoped readers gained a keyword-only `client=None`; every call site builds the client **on the event loop** and passes it into the worker; the discovery-response and leaderboard fan-outs run under a process-wide `bounded_io_bound` permit (`web/bounded_io.py`, max 4) that is held until the worker actually finishes, not until the awaiting coroutine unwinds. **Live signed-in smoke still worth doing after deploy** (My Edits, My Comments, homepage Recently Viewed, discovery replies, Leaderboard) — the failure mode here is *silently empty*, not an error. Regression guard: `tests/test_no_await_sync_function.py` (AST, forbids the pattern repo-wide in `web/`, with synthetic violation/pass sub-tests) + leaderboard unit tests. |
| **Visual Genizah Atlas unusable on phones — fixed-720px canvas + `touch-action:none` made the page a scroll trap (mobile is 68% of atlas traffic)** | `web/pages/atlas.py` (`_ATLAS_CANVAS_HEIGHT_CSS`), `web/static/js/atlas_decode.js` (`applyTouchAction`, `endPointer`), `genizah_translations.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-07-29) — merged (#315, #316) + **deployed**; owner-confirmed on a phone, with ONE accepted residual (see "Residual", end of entry) | Owner received complaints from mobile users. PostHog (30d) shows mobile is the atlas's PRIMARY audience, not an edge case: **1,587 of 2,364 `/atlas` pageviews (68%) and 972 of 1,447 people**, driven by the homepage/Facebook announcement — with 640 mobile `atlas_cluster_focused` events, so they actively try to use it. Two defects: **(1) scroll trap** — `_ATLAS_CANVAS_HEIGHT_PX = 720` was applied as a FLAT `height:720px` to both the reserved box and the `<canvas>`, with no media query and no responsive override (`resizeCanvas` merely reads `clientHeight` back; `state.narrow` only swaps the focus-panel UX, never the geometry). On a phone that canvas is taller than the visible viewport, and because the renderer sets `canvas.style.touchAction='none'` to own pan/pinch, every vertical swipe panned the map instead of scrolling the page — users could not scroll past the atlas. Only 6% of mobile loads (86/1373) ever entered fullscreen, where that gesture model would be appropriate. Fixed by capping the reservation at `min(720px, 60vh)` on **touch-primary devices only** (a stylesheet rule gated on `(hover: none) and (pointer: coarse)`; the inline reservation stays a flat 720px, so pointer layouts are byte-for-byte unchanged and CLS still holds) and, on any device WITH a touchscreen outside fullscreen, handing vertical panning back to the browser with `touch-action: pan-y` (horizontal drag still pans, two-finger pinch still zooms; fullscreen restores `none`), re-derived on fullscreen-change and resize via a single `applyTouchAction()` helper. **The two gates use deliberately DIFFERENT, NESTED queries** — height cap on `(hover: none) and (pointer: coarse)` (touch-primary), gesture on `(any-pointer: coarse)` (has a touchscreen at all) — because `height` is global to the layout (widening it would shrink the canvas for mouse users of any touchscreen laptop) whereas `touch-action` is input-scoped and never affects mouse input (so the wide gate is free, and is required to cover hybrids). Touch-primary implies any-coarse-pointer, so every device that gets the shorter canvas also gets page-scroll; **do not collapse the two into one query in either direction** (guarded by `test_the_two_gates_are_deliberately_nested_not_equal`). **Codex review on PR #315 rejected THREE successive gates — all signal bugs, each now pinned by a regression guard:** (a) a viewport-relative INLINE `min(720px, 60vh)` applied to every device — `60vh` is below 720px for any viewport under 1200px tall, so it silently shrank nearly every desktop (648px at 1920×1080, 461px at a 1366×768 laptop) while the change claimed desktop was untouched; (b) gating the gesture on `state.narrow` (the focus-panel breakpoint, canvas width ≤640px), which left LANDSCAPE phones and tablets — a 720px box over a ~390px-tall viewport, the most trapping geometry there is — in the exact trap being fixed; (c) gating the gesture on the touch-PRIMARY query, which describes only the primary pointer, so HYBRIDS (touchscreen laptops, Surface, iPad + trackpad) reported `hover: hover`/`pointer: fine` and kept `touch-action: none` with a finger on the canvas — the same trap, and a regression against (b)'s behaviour for narrow hybrids. Finding (c) also caught an internal inconsistency: the code comment argued "being liberal here is cheap" and then used the restrictive query. The cap deliberately uses a CLASS selector (0-1-0) so `atlas_decode.js`'s `.atlas-fs-box:fullscreen` rules (0-2-0/0-2-1, also `!important`) still out-specify it and fullscreen keeps filling the screen on a phone. Note `touch-action` constrains touch/pen input only, so a touchscreen laptop still pans by mouse drag. **(2) ghost taps** — `pan-y` means the browser CLAIMS a one-finger swipe and fires `pointercancel`, usually before the 2px that sets `dragMoved`; `endPointer` was wired to `pointercancel` and would have treated that as a tap, focusing a cluster under the user's finger on every scroll. `endPointer(ev, canceled)` now excludes canceled pointers from the tap path. **Deliberately plain `vh`, not `dvh`:** `dvh` is unparseable on iOS < 15.4, which drops the declaration and collapses the reserved box. (Related NiceGUI trap hit en route: `.style()` is parsed into a dict keyed by property name, so an inline `vh`→`dvh` progressive-enhancement pair does NOT survive — only the last value is kept, i.e. `dvh` alone. A media query cannot be expressed inline either, which is why the cap lives in a real stylesheet rule.) Copy also revised: the intro told the mobile majority to "view the atlas on a computer"; it now points them at the Full screen button (EN + HE, wording pending owner sign-off). Tests: new `tests/test_atlas_mobile_gestures.py` (15 static/structural guards — the renderer is browser-only, no JS harness in repo — including a regression guard for each of the three gates Codex rejected, plus one pinning the nesting of the two surviving queries); the two existing CLS guards (`tests/test_atlas_flag_gating.py`, `tests/render_smoke/test_atlas_render_smoke.py`) keep asserting the flat pixel reservation, which the final design preserves. Gates: 311 atlas+translation+render-smoke tests green, ruff clean, `node --check` clean, masking `--scan-repo` clean, `check_docs.py` healthy. **Still open:** the ~13% of mobile pageviews with no `atlas_loaded`, and the silent stuck-loading path (if `atlas_decode.js` itself fails to load, the `atlas.py` bootstrap poll gives up after 200×50ms and returns silently, leaving "Loading the atlas…" with no error overlay and no telemetry — genuine fetch/decode failures ARE handled via `init().catch()`). The 12.6 MB asset (2.26 MB brotli) is a plausible mobile contributor. Related: the opaque `Script error.` issue in PostHog error tracking is this bug's signature (30 occurrences / 13 users, 100% on `/atlas`, 100% mobile) and was deliberately NOT suppressed. **Deployed 2026-07-29**; prod serving verified by fetching the live page + asset (`any-pointer: coarse` and `_hasCoarsePointer` in `/static/js/atlas_decode.js`, `endPointer(ev, canceled)`, and the `<style id="atlas-height-cap">` rule in the served `<head>` — note NiceGUI delivers element styles and label text over the websocket, so those are NOT in the fetched HTML and cannot be checked this way). **Owner phone check (2026-07-29) — what was ACTUALLY exercised:** ✅ pinch still zooms the map; ✅ swiping does NOT jump into a cluster (the `pointercancel` ghost-tap guard holds — that bug was introduced BY this fix, so it is a real result); ⚠️ page scroll works **"sometimes"** in both portrait and landscape. NOT exercised: full-screen pan/pinch, and any hybrid/touchscreen-laptop device (the `(any-pointer: coarse)` widening from #316 is therefore code-correct but UNVERIFIED in the wild). **RESIDUAL — KNOWN AND OWNER-ACCEPTED, do not silently "fix" it:** the intermittency is inherent to `touch-action: pan-y`, not a coding slip. `pan-y` lets the browser *decide at touch-start* whether to claim the gesture based on its initial direction, so a cleanly vertical swipe scrolls the page while a swipe with horizontal drift stays with the canvas and pans the map — and thumb swipes are rarely straight. Our `pointermove` also pans for the few pixels before the browser decides, so a successful scroll may feel slightly jittery. The deterministic alternative (inline: one finger always = page, two fingers = pan the map, pinch = zoom; full-screen unchanged — the embedded-Google-Maps convention) was proposed and **owner declined 2026-07-29: "we can leave it that way, it's not too troubling"** — the total trap is gone, which was the complaint. Revisit only if mobile users complain again; the alternative would cost one-finger map panning outside full screen and wants a short on-canvas hint. |
| **Web Joins Lab — SEED-008 fire-and-forget guard (async tasks that mutate UI after await in client-teardown context)** | `web/pages/joins_lab.py` (all `asyncio.ensure_future` callers), `web/components/compare_modal.py`, `web/components/candidate_grid.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-06-20, Phase 120 Plan 02) | All `asyncio.ensure_future(...)` coroutines in `joins_lab.py` and the candidate/compare components that mutate NiceGUI UI elements after an `await` now wrap the body in `try/except RuntimeError: return  # SEED-008`. Without this guard, a coroutine that `await`s I/O and then modifies a NiceGUI element (e.g. `label.set_text(...)`) raises `RuntimeError: No socket found for NiceGUI task` when the client tab has been closed or destroyed in the interim (server-side component teardown during long Supabase fetches or cross-tab navigation). The guard is also enforced by the off-loop live-file tests in `tests/test_joins_lab_off_loop.py`. AST static guard (`tests/test_joins_lab_off_loop.py::test_list_picker_seed008_guard_present`) pins the pattern. |

Now every branch (Restart/Skip/Resume) loops over all running runs. (2) The LD-6 clean-shutdown sweep (`running→completed`) lived in `MyLibraryTab.closeEvent`, but a child widget never receives `closeEvent` on app exit, so it was dead code and orphans accumulated across hard kills (e.g. the 2026-05-25 NLI-hang SIGKILL). UAT 2026-05-27: hard-kill orphan → Skip marked it `canceled`; a live run flipped `running→completed` on clean X-close; 0 orphans remain. Tests: `tests/test_recovery_scan_runs_cleanup.py`. |

Tests in `tests/test_local_lab_invalidation.py` (replace-not-append + survivor-UID set + deleted-page-gone + empty-source-abort), each verified to fail without its fix. |

Bug E: "Stuck at 0%" during enumeration — fixed by new `LocalIndexerWorker.status_updated(str)` signal emitting bilingual "Discovering files… / מאתר קבצים…" before `scan_all` enumeration loop + `QProgressBar.setRange(0, 0)` indeterminate busy mode + first `progress_updated` flips to `(0, 100)` determinate + reset to `(0, 100)` on finish/cancel/error (D-21) (R97.3-E).
