| **Desktop ResultDialog bright-mode unreadable: "Sys: ... FL: ..." metadata line is rendered white-on-white** | `desktop/result_dialog.py` (the header/metadata label that displays system_number + fl_id near the top of the dialog) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-18) | Set explicit theme-aware color on `self.lbl_info` at `desktop/result_dialog.py:214` — stylesheet now reads `"font-size: 11px; color: palette(text);"` so the label foreground follows the active Qt palette in both bright and dark modes (same pattern already used at `genizah_app.py:16571`). Surfaced 2026-05-18 during Phase 999.4 Plan 02 desktop smoke check. In bright (light) mode, the small grey "Sys: <system_number> FL: <fl_id>" diagnostic label is invisible — text color appears to be `white` (or a near-white token from dark-mode QSS) while the dialog background is also white in bright mode. Pure-dark-mode users wouldn't have noticed because the white-on-dark contrast is fine. **Likely root cause:** a Qt stylesheet rule that hardcodes `color: white` or `color: #ffffff` on this label without a corresponding bright-mode override; OR a QSS that uses a palette token that resolves correctly only in dark mode. **Fix candidates:** (a) use a theme-aware token (e.g. `palette(text)` or the project's existing `--text-secondary` equivalent for QSS) so the color follows the active palette; (b) add a bright-mode override that explicitly sets a readable grey/charcoal; (c) audit `genizah_app.py` `setStyleSheet` calls for any other hardcoded `color: white` on metadata labels. **Severity P2 Medium:** the data (sys_id and fl_id) is essential for debugging / cross-app reference but completely invisible in bright mode. **Owner:** dedicated bright-mode QSS audit follow-up — bundles naturally with the next bug (dark toolbars in bright mode). |
| **Image-number chip on web search-result cards (Phase 999.1 FOLIO-01) cannot display 3-digit page numbers — the chip is too narrow and the digits get clipped on both `/browse` (next to shelfmark, post-Phase 92.1 reader migration era) and `/search` Quick View result cards** | `web/pages/search_results.py:469-481` (the `_img_num` chip block introduced in commit `8368a962`, tooltip added in `9db7b18e`), plus any analogous renderer in `web/pages/browse.py` if the chip is duplicated there | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-18) | Added `min-width: 2.4em; text-align: center;` to the `_img_num` chip style at `web/pages/search_results.py:479-481`, reserving room for up to 3 characters. No duplicate chip renderer found in `web/pages/browse.py`. Surfaced 2026-05-18 during Phase 999.4 Plan 01 (web line numbers) smoke check. The chip uses `text-xs px-2 py-0.5 rounded shrink-0` classes — `shrink-0` was added intentionally to prevent flex collapse when shelfmarks are long, but it does NOT widen the chip to fit a 3-digit number; the `px-2` padding leaves only enough room for 1-2 characters of `text-xs` Tailwind sizing. Manuscripts with page numbers ≥ 100 (e.g. siddurim / responsa volumes / multi-folio T-S Box collections) will display a truncated number. **Fix candidates:** (a) drop `text-xs` to `text-sm` so the chip auto-sizes a little wider (still subordinate to the bold shelfmark); (b) widen `px-2` → `px-3`; (c) add `min-width: 2.4em` so the chip reserves space for up to 3 characters and centers them; (d) keep current styling but rely on intrinsic content sizing — verify `shrink-0` does not also clip overflow. **No regression in the Plan 999.1 D-04 / D-05 invariants** — the chip's truthy gate and CSS-token scheme are not affected. **Severity P2 Medium:** data is technically there but visually clipped, which can mislead users about which folio a hit came from. **Owner:** small follow-up; not blocking Phase 999.4. |
| **Tabular Responsa search → Lines tab: "Distance" input field (lines distance between components) is too narrow to display 2-digit numbers; the digits get clipped** | `web/pages/search.py` (Responsa-mode tabular UI; "Distance" `ui.number` input next to each `Component` block in the Lines tab) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-18) | Widened the Distance `ui.number` input from Tailwind class `w-16` (4rem) to `w-32` (8rem) at `web/pages/search.py:2676`, and bumped the surrounding column `min-width` from `60px` to `130px` so the suffix (English `lines` / `words`, Hebrew `שורות` / `מילים`) AND a 2-digit number both render. As part of the broader sweep flagged in the original report, also widened two other clipped `ui.number` inputs: browse-page navigation input at `web/pages/browse.py:4014` from `w-14` → `w-24` (the previous width clipped the 3rd digit on manuscripts with ≥100 pages, e.g. 224/111), and inline page-jump input in Quick-View result cards at `web/pages/search_results.py:1872` from `w-12` → `w-20`. Surfaced 2026-05-18 during Phase 999.4 Plan 01 smoke check (same triage session that surfaced the chip-overflow above). User report: in the tabular search UI, when Responsa search is selected and the "Lines" tab is active, the per-component "Distance" field used to specify the maximum line-gap between two adjacent components in the L<N>: parser is too narrow to display a 2-digit value entered by the user. Likely the same family of bug as the chip-overflow: a `ui.number` rendered with a Tailwind `w-12` / `w-16` class that pre-dates 2-digit input requirements. **Fix candidates:** widen the input to `w-20` or use `min-width: 4ch`. **Severity P2 Medium:** data is preserved in state but visually clipped on re-display. **Note:** user explicitly observed there are "perhaps more places with this problem" — schedule a quick visual sweep of numeric input fields across `/search` tabular UI and result-toolbar widgets as part of the eventual fix. |
| **Tabular Responsa search → Lines + Word Range tabs: redundant "Distance" fields appear before any component is added via "Add Component"; same redundant fields also appear in Word Range tab** | `web/pages/search.py` (Responsa tabular search UI — Lines tab + Word Range tab component layout; condition-on-component-count for the inter-component Distance field) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-18) | `on_scope_change` at `web/pages/search.py:2488-2493` now respects `builder_state['num_components']` when toggling Distance-spinner visibility — `ds.set_visibility(show_dists and i < n - 1)` — so switching tabs (Word Range / Lines / Within Document) never reveals more Distance widgets than there are component-pairs. Initial render (2 components, 1 distance) and `add_component` / `remove_component` already maintained correct per-index visibility; the bug was purely in `on_scope_change` unconditionally showing all 3 distance spinners. Surfaced 2026-05-18 during Phase 999.4 Plan 01 smoke check. User report with screenshot: in the Lines tab, BEFORE clicking "Add Component" to introduce a second component, the UI already shows 3 "Distance / lines" label-input pairs at the top-left of the panel. These distance fields are conceptually between-component constraints — they should only appear ONCE per pair of adjacent components, AND only AFTER "Add Component" introduces a 2nd, 3rd, etc. component. The redundant Distance fields also appear when the user switches to the Word Range tab. **Expected behavior:** Distance fields render only as `n_components - 1` pairwise constraints between adjacent components (Component 1 → Component 2 = 1 Distance; Components 1→2 AND 2→3 = 2 Distances; etc.). With 1 component, ZERO Distance fields should be visible. Switching to Word Range should not preserve Lines-tab Distance widgets. **Likely cause:** the Distance fields are unconditionally rendered as part of the Component column scaffolding, rather than gated on `len(components) > 1`. **Severity P2 Medium:** confusing UX; users could enter values into orphan fields that are never consumed by the search pipeline. **Fix candidates:** (a) only render a Distance field where it sits between two real components (so `n - 1` total); (b) clear / hide Lines-tab Distance widgets when switching to Word Range tab; (c) on initial mount with 1 component, render zero Distance fields. Same root may also explain unwanted Distance widgets bleeding into Word Range tab — verify the tab-switch handler resets the component-related local state. |
| **`/lists` page rendering slowness: lists take long to display, and clicking a list takes long to show its contents** | `web/pages/lists.py`, `web/user_lists.py`, `web/supabase_client.py` (per-request authenticated client construction) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-17, Phase 92.2) | **Closed by Phase 92.2 lists-performance-investigation.** Pre-fix `92.2-01-BASELINE.json`: 3 warm `/lists` samples at 35.5s / 34.2s / 45.8s (mean 38.5s, max 45.8s, `query_count == client_build_count == 26` per render proving every Supabase reader call was rebuilding a fresh authenticated Client). Post-fix `92.2-02-POSTFIX.json`: 3 warm samples at 1.9s / 1.9s / 2.2s (mean 2.0s, max 2.2s, `query_count = 2` and `client_build_count = 1` per render — **19.3x mean speedup, 21.1x max speedup, 26x reduction in Client builds**). HARD GATE `all_warm_total_wall_clock_ms_lte_5000 = TRUE` (met by 2.3x margin). Fix shape: (a) task-scoped `WeakKeyDictionary` memo on `get_user_client()` keyed by `(get_persisted_session_uuid(), access_token)` with `asyncio.current_task()` lookup; sync-context bypass; token-rotation invalidation; (b) batched zero-arg `get_list_item_counts_for_user()` RPC (SECURITY INVOKER, STABLE, `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon`, `GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role`, `auth.uid()`-derived identity, `idx_list_items_list_id` index); (c) `data`+`counts` threaded through `render_lists_sidebar`/`render_list_content`/`select_list`/`refresh_ui`/`async_refresh_ui`; (d) `UserListsManager.refresh_data() -> Dict` returns the fetched data so `async_refresh_ui` consumes the return value without double-fetch; (e) `_resolve_list_item_count` helper distinguishes `counts is None` (legacy per-list fallback) from `counts == {}` (valid batched empty result) per Reviews MUST-FIX 1. RPC pushed to production Supabase 2026-05-17 before post-fix capture. Plan 92.2-01 also shipped permanent forensic instrumentation behind `GS_LISTS_PERF_INSTRUMENTATION=1` opt-in env var (ASGI middleware emits `lists_perf_baseline=<json>`, sidebar emits `lists_sidebar_decomposition=<json>`, `_lists_task_probe` emits at 4 lifecycle points). **Original entry retained for evidence:** Observed 2026-05-17 during Plan 92.1-02 Symptom-3 reproduction testing. User report: "/lists page and selected-list rendering 'take long time to show lists and to show the list that was clicked.'" This is UNRELATED to Symptom 3 (the original Phase 90 reader-client regression bug was an error + missing list, not slowness). Possibly related to Plan 92.1-01's per-request authenticated client overhead — every `get_user_lists` / `get_list_items` call now constructs a fresh `Client` instance, applies user auth headers via `_apply_user_auth_to_client`, and tears down on return. Pre-Phase-90, the anonymous singleton was reused across requests; post-Phase 90 + post-Phase 92.1, each reader call builds a Client. Not diagnosed. **Suggested investigation paths:** (a) profile `/lists` page load with `cProfile` / `py-spy` to identify the dominant cost — Supabase HTTP round-trip vs Client construction vs Tantivy/csv_bank enrichment lookups; (b) examine whether `get_list_items` makes per-item enrichment calls (sys_id → catalog metadata lookups); (c) consider request-scoped Client memoization keyed on `_session_uuid` (single Client per request, multiple table queries) — preserves multitenant safety while amortizing construction cost across multiple reader calls in a single page render. **Evidence:** Initial user report 2026-05-17 during Plan 92.1-02 reproduction was perceptual ("take long time"). **UPGRADED to BLOCKER 2026-05-17 evening during interactive UAT via `/gsd-verify-work 92.1`:** quantified measurements — Test 1 cold `/lists` page load = **36 seconds** to render; Test 2 post-create-list navigation back to `/lists` = **>40 seconds with spinner stuck, never resolved** (user gave up). Combined with "6s till dialog opens, 4s till list created" for the Add-to-List flow, the per-request authenticated client overhead from Plan 92.1-01's reader migration is materially impacting list-view UX end-to-end. Lists are now correctly populated (Phase 90 regression closed) but the cost-of-correctness is unusable latency. **Severity escalated from P2 Medium → BLOCKER pending a follow-up gap-closure phase (e.g., 92.2-lists-performance-investigation).** Suggested Task 1 = profile with `cProfile` / `py-spy`, Task 2 = chosen mitigation (request-scoped Client memoization keyed on `_session_uuid`? batch the 8+ reader calls? re-add a TTL cache that respects per-user isolation?), Task 3 = re-verify under same SWEEP-05 R0/R1 protocol — `/lists` must be <5s warm before declaring fix shipped. | | `web/pages/search.py` (export buttons L1467-1473), `web/pages/parallels.py` (export buttons L1321-1327), NiceGUI `client.download` outbox flush | ❌ Open | Surfaced 2026-05-14 during Phase 88 SC#2 smoke check. User flow: opened genizahsearch.com in incognito, ran a search that returned results, clicked Word/Excel/JSON export buttons — nothing happened. Server log showed NO `/api/export/excel` (or other export) request hit the backend for the click. Reproduces on **both local dev (Phase 88 code) AND production (pre-Phase-88 code)**, so this is NOT a Phase 88 regression — it is a pre-existing client-side issue. **Phase 88 implication:** SC#2 cross-user isolation is still effectively confirmed (Browser B got no data instead of Browser A's data — leak-free behavior). **Hypothesis:** the click reaches Python (search button click in the same session worked + showed results), so the WebSocket is alive; but Python's `ui.download(...)` enqueues a `download` outbox message that the JS client either never receives or doesn't act on in incognito. Could be related to NiceGUI WebSocket reconnect state, SameSite cookie behavior, or browser security defaults that block programmatic downloads in private browsing. **Diagnostic confirmation that Phase 88 wiring works:** integration test `tests/test_export_cross_user_isolation.py::test_two_sessions_get_independent_filenames` passes; ad-hoc TestClient repro (set_search_export → /api/export/excel returns 200 with correct Content-Disposition) confirms the server side is sound. **Next steps:** (a) capture WebSocket frames in incognito DevTools while clicking export — look for the `download` outbox message; (b) consider replacing `ui.download(...)` with a plain anchor `<a href="/api/export/excel" download>` since download URLs are relative GET endpoints; (c) check whether NiceGUI 3.8 has any known incognito-related bugs. Scoped for a dedicated quick task — NOT a Phase 88 gap. |
| **Cross-user state contamination: User A's search query appeared as User B's suggested .xlsx filename** | `web/state.py` (singleton fields), `web/api.py` (5 export handlers), `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/search_results.py`, `web/pages/parallels.py`, **new** `web/export_state.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-13, Phase 88 closure) | User report 2026-05-12: User A searched for term X; User B (different device + network) searched for term Y; User B's xlsx download dialog suggested User A's query name as the filename. **Root cause:** `AppState` is a process-wide singleton (`web/state.py:5-10`). All export-relevant fields — the 10 per-user mirror fields listed in Phase 88 STATE-01 — were written there by every search-execute path and read by every `/api/export/*` handler. Last writer wins across all users sharing the process. The browse export endpoint at `web/api.py:2083` was already correctly using per-session `app.storage.user`; search/parallels exports were the outliers. **2026-05-12 hotfix:** new module `web/export_state.py` exposes `set_search_export` / `get_search_export` / `update_search_export_results` / `update_search_export_selection` / `clear_search_export` and parallels variants. All accesses routed through `_backend()` which targeted `app.storage.user` in production (per-session via NiceGUI session cookie) and a test-backend dict for tests. All 5 `/api/export/*` handlers in `web/api.py` (excel/word/json + parallels excel/word/json) read exclusively from the per-session payload; a fresh session with no prior search gets 400 even if another session has results pending. Per-session writes mirrored the existing `state.*` writes at 4 sites in `web/pages/search.py`, 2 sites in `web/pages/search_results.py`, 5 sites in `web/pages/parallels.py`. The legacy `state.*` mirror writes were left as dead code for cleanup-phase removal. **2026-05-13 Phase 88 permanent closure:** Plan 88-01 migrated the 13 writer sites from `state.X = value` to local variables threaded through the export_state setters; Plan 88-02 rewrote `web/export_state.py` to route through the Phase 87 `web.safe_storage` chokepoint, deleted the production-code test-backend selector helper, hardened getters/updaters with `isinstance(payload, dict)` guard + copy-on-update, and rewrote the 4 export-related test files (`test_export_cross_user_isolation`, `test_export_state_selection`, `test_api_export_json`, `test_api_legacy_unchanged`) to monkeypatch `web.safe_storage.app` with SimpleNamespace instance-isolated stubs (no `state.X =` fixture setup remains); Plan 88-03 deleted the 10 AppState mirror fields from `web/state.py`, installed two permanent CI regression guards (`tests/test_no_appstate_export_fields.py` runtime D-06 + `tests/test_no_deleted_state_references.py` static AST D-07 with alias-import coverage per cross-AI Refinement 5), and refreshed the 2 stale docstring/comment sites at `web/api.py:1846-1851` and `web/search_api.py:1198-1204`. **Full suite green at 1894+ passed.** |
| **/browse returns 500 with `AssertionError: user storage for {uuid} should be created before accessing it`** | `web/pages/browse_state.py:122` (restore_browse_snapshot), `web/pages/browse_state.py:165` (persist_browse_snapshot) | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-12) | User report 2026-05-12: ran Responsa Judeo-Arabic search, stopped the search, then navigated to a result on /browse — page returned 500. Stack: `web/main.py:1143` (`browse_page_route`) → `web/pages/browse.py:4621` (`create_browse_page`) → `web/pages/browse_state.py:122` → `app.storage.user.get('browse_snapshot_schema_version', 0)` → NiceGUI `storage.py:121` `assert session_id in self._users, ...`. **Root cause:** NiceGUI's built-in `prune_user_storage` (10s scheduler) races with a fresh page handler — by the time `restore_browse_snapshot` reads `app.storage.user`, the session_id from the request cookie has already been evicted from `_users`. Same family of race as the P1 memory-leak entry's `AssertionError log spam` symptom-of-symptom, but here it surfaces as a 500 instead of a log line because `restore_browse_snapshot:122` had an unprotected `app.storage.user.get(...)` outside any try/except. The same vulnerability existed at `persist_browse_snapshot:165` (`app.storage.user.get('session_persistence_enabled', True)`). **Fix:** wrapped both bare `app.storage.user.get(...)` reads in `try: ... except (AssertionError, Exception) as e: logger.debug(...); return ...` so a pruned-session race degrades to "render with default empty snapshot" / "skip persist" instead of bubbling to NiceGUI's 500 page. Existing `_restore_reading_desk_state` (browse.py:1119) and `show_translations` reads (browse.py:2079 + 2114) already had try/except guards; only the snapshot helpers were unprotected. **Tests:** 2 new `test_browse_state.py` regression cases (`test_restore_tolerates_user_storage_assertion`, `test_persist_tolerates_user_storage_assertion`) mock `app.storage.user` with `side_effect=AssertionError(...)` and assert defaults/no-raise; 7/7 browse_state tests pass. **Not a full fix for the P1 leak family** — this just stops the 500. Background-task callers of `app.storage.user` may still hit the same race; remaining hardening lives in the P1 entry's "audit `app.storage.user` writes" approach. |
| **Web "Sync Now" button labeling implies desktop→web pull but only migrates browser-local lists to cloud; desktop auto-sync failures are silent** | `web/pages/lists.py`, `genizah_translations.py`, `genizah_app.py:11610` (`_lists_auto_sync`), `lists_sync.py` | 🟡 Partially Fixed (2026-05-12) | User report (Hebrew): created lists on desktop while logged in, opened web with same account + good connection, clicked "Sync Now" — web did not show the desktop lists. **Investigation:** Two compounding issues. (1) The web "Sync Now" buttons at `web/pages/lists.py:676` (dialog) and `:722` (card) are wired to `migrate_local_to_user()` — that ONLY migrates *browser-localStorage anonymous* lists into the user's Supabase account. It does NOT pull cloud→view. The actual cloud-pull happens automatically inside `UserListsManager._get_cached_data()` (user_lists.py:116-159) on every `/lists` page load (10s TTL cache). So the button's name mismatched user expectation. (2) Desktop's `_lists_auto_sync()` (genizah_app.py:11610) is fire-and-forget on a background thread; every failure path goes to `logger.debug` — RLS rejections, network blips, missing `user_uuid`, or schema drift are invisible to the user, so a silent push failure would leave cloud empty without any visible signal. **Part A fix shipped 2026-05-12 (web UX honesty):** renamed "Sync Now" → "Move to account" (both callsites); dialog header "Sync Your Lists" → "Move browser lists to your account"; card title "Local Lists Available" → "Browser lists found"; explanatory copy reframed as one-way browser→account move; added a visible "Refresh from Cloud" button (`refresh` icon, `flat round dense`) on the lists page header when logged in that calls `async_refresh_ui` → `state.lists_mgr.refresh_data()` → `invalidate_cache()` + Supabase re-fetch + re-render; reworded the `cloud_done` tooltip to "Lists auto-sync between this site and the desktop app" so users know they don't need a button to receive updates. Added 8 new Hebrew strings to `genizah_translations.py`. Tests: 108 list+translation tests green. **Part B deferred (desktop sync visibility):** still needed — add a "Last synced" timestamp in desktop Lists tab; surface sync failures via toast/badge instead of `logger.debug`; add an on-demand "Sync now" button with success/failure feedback. Without Part B, silent desktop-push failures still strand lists in local-only state with no user-visible signal. Scoped for v7.11.1 desktop installer cycle. |
| **T-S NS 329.96 (and ~107 similar CUDL classmarks) missing from app despite having a CUDL manifest with images** | `libraries.csv`, `fist_data/synthetic_manifest.json`, `fist_data/fjms_enrichment.db`, `scripts/generate_synthetic_rows.py`, `shared/fist_cudl_bridge.py`, `shared/synthetic_sys_id.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-11) | User report: searching `T-S NS 329.96` returned no result despite CUDL having a manifest at `MS-TS-NS-00329-00096`. Investigation traced the root cause to a Phase 84/Phase 85 coverage gap — the CUDL classmark has NO Alma row in FIST.db (an `existing-Alma-only` Phase 85 design excluded it) AND Phase 84's alias index couldn't resolve it. **Fix landed via Phase 86** (5 plans, 2026-05-11 in one day): CUDL-walked synthetic-row generator + bidirectional FIST↔CUDL bridge module. Plan 02 rewrote `_build_qualifying_inventories` to walk the CUDL classmark universe instead of FIST inventories, resolves no-Alma single-bridge hits through the new `shared/fist_cudl_bridge.py::explain_fist_by_cudl` API, and emits a synthetic libraries.csv row for each image-bearing-by-construction CUDL classmark. Plan 04 ran the operational sequence end-to-end: 108 synthetic rows now sit in libraries.csv between `# BEGIN SYNTHETIC` / `# END SYNTHETIC` markers; T-S NS 329.96 specifically resolves as sys_id `990065549106000000` (encoded via `encode_inventory_sys_id(65549106)`); `fist_data/fjms_enrichment.db` regenerated with synthetic AlmaIds injected into all 12 verbatim AlmaId-keyed enrichment tables via the Phase 85 SYNTH-05 UNION ALL pattern. AUDIT-03 regression guard added: `scripts/audit_nli_attribution.py` + `tests/test_nli_oxford_attribution.py` against canonical 461-row fixture from v7.9.4 fix-commit replay. AUDIT-01/02 deliverables: `reports/cudl_coverage.md` 5-tier breakdown — 96.23% phase84_hit + 0.08% phase86_synthetic + 2.39% phase86_existing_alma_candidate (documented candidates, app shelfmark-search does NOT reach these at runtime — needs Phase 84 alias-index extension in a future Phase 87) + 0.18% multi_inventory_ambiguous + 1.13% phase86_residue. ROADMAP success criterion 1 `<200 legacy-orphan` NOT achieved (1,599 residue remains; all 6 D-02c residue pattern families REJECTED in the 2026-05-11 user-adjudication checkpoint; future Phase 87 scoped for bridge rule disambiguation + Phase 84 alias-index extension). **Web deploy step:** scp libraries.csv + fist_data/fjms_enrichment.db to genizahsearch.com, restart web service; desktop installer NOT rebuilt per `feedback_no_github_release_for_web_only.md` — data bundles into next desktop-code release automatically. Pre-flight: 11/11 new Phase 86-04 tests GREEN (8 scanner + 3 attribution); full --apply orchestrator 8/8 steps PASS via `python scripts/phase86_apply.py --apply`. |
| **Phase 77 parallels JSON: `chunk_hits` field-name collision between two producers crashed serializer with `'int' object is not iterable`** | `genizah_core.py` (search_composition_logic + lab_composition_search), `shared/search_serializer.py` (_to_parallels_envelope_item) | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-28) | Surfaced during Phase 77 Plan 05 manual smoke check on /parallels JSON download. Plan 02 extended `lab_composition_search` to populate `chunk_hits` per uid as a list-of-tuples (D-13 Path A) — but the **standard-mode** `search_composition_logic` path (genizah_core.py:~1196) had used `chunk_hits` since 2026-03-12 as an **int counter** tracking how many chunks each uid hit. Both producers store onto the same per-uid item dict, so the serializer's `_to_parallels_envelope_item` (which iterates `for ch_idx, src_text, score, ms_snippet in item['chunk_hits']`) crashed with `TypeError: 'int' object is not iterable` on standard-mode parallels results. Three-stage fix (commits `baf481fb`, `c24fcc48`, `2e2d2b75`, `327aea31`): (1) `baf481fb` — added `isinstance(chunk_hits, list)` guard in serializer + upgraded both JSON handlers' `logger.error` → `logger.exception` so future shape collisions surface stack traces; +1 regression test `test_parallels_chunk_hits_int_falls_back_to_path_b`. (2) `c24fcc48` — extended `search_composition_logic` to populate `chunk_hits` as the same list-of-tuples shape Plan 02 established for `lab_composition_search` (mirroring D-13 Path A), and renamed the int counter to `chunk_count` to free the field name. Also fixed parallels rep-field mapping in `_to_parallels_envelope_item`: `rep['text']` → `synth['snippet']`, `rep['full_text']/'content'/'text'` → `synth['full_text']` so parallels items get meaningful `snippet`/`excerpt`/`match_terms` instead of empty strings; +4 tests. (3) `2e2d2b75` — surfaced Tantivy score on search results (was always `0.0` in JSON because `genizah_core.py:7542` and `:7559` `results.append({...})` sites never recorded the score variable they had in scope) + added per-uid `_chunk_hit_keys` dedup in both producers to prevent the same Tantivy uid emitting duplicate (chunk_index, ms_snippet) entries when returned from multiple segments. (4) `327aea31` — group-level dedup in `_to_parallels_envelope_item` keyed on `(chunk_index, manuscript_snippet)` (highest-scoring entry wins) catches cross-uid duplicates that per-uid dedup couldn't (NLI sometimes catalogs the same physical manuscript under multiple Alma uids on the same sys_id, e.g. Karaite prayer books); matches[] now sorted by `chunk_index` ascending; +2 tests. **Final test count: 1201 passed / 8 skipped** (started at 1162; +39 new tests across Phase 77). **Lesson learned:** when extending a multi-producer field, audit ALL producers writing to the same per-uid item dict — not just the one the new feature targets. The `chunk_hits` name had been claimed by the standard path 6 weeks before Plan 02 borrowed it for D-13. Avoid reusing field names across producers when downstream consumers iterate the field. |
| **Web search Export ignores row checkboxes: with one result checked, Export produces the full list instead of just the selected item** | `web/state.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/search_results.py`, `web/api.py`, `web/pages/search_helpers.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-28) | Surfaced 2026-04-17 during Phase 75 non-regression walkthrough (surface 1 item e). User confirmed this is **pre-existing** behavior on the live website — NOT a v7.9 decomposition regression. Expected: when one or more result checkboxes are ticked, Export should emit only the checked rows. Actual (now fixed): all 3 export handlers (Excel/Word/JSON) honor checkbox selection. **Fix landed in Phase 77 Plan 06 (gap-closure):** new `AppState.last_selected_uids: Optional[List[str]]` field mirrors page-scoped `search_state.selected_indices` to the global singleton via 3 callsites (`toggle_select_all` at search.py, per-row `toggle_card_selection` at search_results.py, `_reset_search` at search.py sets it back to None on New Search). All 3 search-side export handlers in `web/api.py` filter `state.last_results` by uid when `state.last_selected_uids` is non-empty; filename gets `-selected-N` suffix when filtered. Empty list `[]` is treated as None defensively. Helper `compute_selected_uids(search_state)` lives in new `web/pages/search_helpers.py` (separate module to avoid search.py↔search_results.py circular-import). Regression tests: `tests/test_export_state_selection.py` (8 tests covering all 3 formats × 3 selection scenarios + filename-suffix invariants) plus `tests/test_compute_selected_uids.py` (4 tests for the pure helper). Surfaced & closed in same plan: Gap #1 (New Search didn't clear export state — `_reset_search` only cleared page-scoped `search_state` and `app.storage.user`, leaving the global singleton's envelope-echo fields populated from the prior search) — also fixed by mirroring 6 envelope-echo fields plus `last_selected_uids` into the global singleton in `_reset_search`. Final test suite: 1201 → 1213 passed / 8 skipped (+12 tests). |
| **Web Visual Similarity dialog: Firefox hid `Show more`, results could not open in a second tab, and copied selections omitted shelfmarks** | `web/components/visual_similarity_dialog.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-24) | User email surfaced three symptoms from the same component. Root causes: (1) the right results pane used Quasar `ui.scroll_area` without a Firefox-robust full-height scroll container, so the list stopped at the initial 20 rows and the bottom `Show more` control was unreachable; (2) shelfmarks and the browse icon were programmatic `ui.button` navigations, so browser-native Ctrl/Cmd-click and middle-click behavior never fired; (3) Quasar buttons apply `user-select: none`, so manual copy/paste missed the shelfmark text entirely. Fix: replaced the right pane with a plain `div` scroller (`overflow-y: auto; height: 100%`), converted the shelfmark to a real `ui.link('/browse?sys_id=...')`, changed the `open_in_new` control to a real `ui.link(..., new_tab=True)`, and moved the remaining row actions to `click.stop` so row expansion does not interfere with link/button clicks. Result: Firefox can scroll to the pagination control, users can open results in a second tab/window without losing the list, and copied selections now include shelfmarks. |
| **Web Visual Similarity dialog preview images used NLI-only URLs instead of the browse module's source-aware image resolution** | `web/components/visual_similarity_dialog.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-24) | Follow-up after manual UI review. The dialog's original pane and expanded suggestion rows were always rendered from `/api/nli_image_by_sysid/{sys_id}?page=0`, so manuscripts whose primary usable images come from Princeton/JTS, Manchester, Oxford, or Cambridge often showed a permanent loading spinner even though `/browse` displayed them correctly. Fix: added `_pick_preview_image_url()` plus `_resolve_preview_image_url_sync()` to populate `meta_mgr.enrich_metadata(sys_id)` on demand and choose the same source family as browse: Oxford direct/proxy for Oxford shelfmarks, `/api/jts_image` for JTS, `/api/manchester_image` for Manchester, `/api/cambridge_image` for Cambridge/non-NLI-primary external sources, and `/api/nli_image_by_sysid?...&width=1000` fallback when NLI is the right source or Cambridge is marked misaligned for CUL. Expanded-row previews now lazy-load through that resolver and cache both image URLs and text. Added targeted unit tests for JTS, Manchester, Cambridge aligned/misaligned, and Oxford direct-image routing. |
| **JTS browse page missing Princeton DPUL image source switch; image credit shows NLI for a JTS manuscript that should default to DPUL** | `shared/nli_crossref_service.py`, `genizah_core.py` (MARC 942 parser, enrich_metadata JTS branch, NLI fetch timeouts/circuit breaker) | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-22) | Surfaced 2026-04-17 during Phase 75 non-regression walkthrough (surface 2 item f, `/browse?sys_id=990053572370205171` ENA 1052.1). User confirmed this was **pre-existing** behavior, NOT a v7.9 decomposition regression. Investigation on 2026-04-22 surfaced a stack of three independent bugs plus a latency issue: (1) **MARC 942$z last-wins** — NLI MARC for JTS manuscripts returns two 942 datafields, one with the real shelfmark (`Ms. ENA 1052.1`, ind1=1) and one with a numeric FGP photo id (`4677401`, ind1=4). The parser's `if val: result['shelfmark_alt'] = val` overwrote the real shelfmark with the FGP id on the second iteration. Downstream JTS lookup then ran against `"4677401"` and missed. Fix: prefer the first non-numeric `$z`. (2) **`Ms. ` abbreviation prefix** — `jts_dpul.shelfmark` stores the bare JTS form (`"ENA 1052.1"`), but MARC and csv_bank return `"Ms. ENA 1052.1"` / `"Ms. 1052.1"`. Added `_jts_shelfmark_variants()` helper and threaded it through `get_jts_manifest_url` / `get_jts_dpul_url` with deduplicated Ms.-strip + `.N`-leaf-strip variants. (3) **csv_bank picks shortest call_number variant** — libraries.csv has 4 pipe-separated variants for ENA 1052.1 (`"Adler, Elkan Nathan Ms. 1052.1 | Ms. 1052.1 | Ms. ENA 1052.1 | The Jewish Theological Seminary of America Ms. ENA 1052.1"`); csv_bank picks `"Ms. 1052.1"` (shortest) as the preferred shelfmark — no `ENA` anywhere, so even the Ms.-stripping variant helper couldn't resurrect it. Fix: added `get_jts_urls_for_sys_id(sys_id)` that JOINs `nli_images.Shelfmark` (canonical bare form stored per sys_id) against `jts_dpul.shelfmark` in one indexed query. `enrich_metadata`'s JTS branch now uses this sys_id-based method instead of iterating `call_numbers_raw` × variant helper (up to 16 DB queries → 1 JOIN). (4) **Navigation lag** — first-visit per manuscript burned 10s NLI IIIF timeout + 10s MARC timeout + 10s Figgy timeout + 10s image fetch = ~25s per JTS manuscript when NLI was slow/unreachable. Fix: NLI + Figgy HTTP timeouts tightened 10s → 5s; added per-sys_id negative cache (60s TTL) and a class-level circuit breaker that trips after 3 consecutive NLI failures across ANY sys_id, skipping NLI calls for 60s so batch navigation through JTS manuscripts doesn't re-burn the timeout on each one. Success resets the counter. Verification: user confirmed JTS source-switch button now toggles images (not external link) on both web and desktop; navigation lag reduced to ~5s per hop after first 2-3 hops in the circuit-open window. Tests: 6 new `TestJtsShelfmarkPrefix` / `TestGetJtsUrlsForSysId` cases. Full suite 1156 passed / 8 skipped. Commits `6ce42522` (crossref) + `02e65e10` (browse wiring + perf). |
| **Paired-leaf / bifolio folio labels empty on CUL shelfmarks (e.g. T-S NS 158.112)** | `shared/nli_crossref_service.py` | 🟡 Partially Fixed (2026-04-19) | Bug 260419-nwv. `_FOLIO_PATTERN` did not handle paired-leaf NLI ImageName notation `L{first}_{second}F...S{side}`, so `parse_folio_label()` returned `''` and `get_folio_images()` collapsed those rows into the `(999999, 0)` sort fallback. **Fixed:** regex now accepts optional `_{second_leaf}`, primary leaf = first number (conservation page-turning order). 8 new `TestParseFolioLabelPairedLeaf` cases + 1 `get_folio_images` sort regression test, all 77 nli_crossref tests green. Folio picker on T-S NS 158.112 (and other bifolio CUL manuscripts) now displays `1r, 1v, 2r, 2v, ...` instead of `1, 2, 3, ...`. **Reproducer:** `scripts/debug_ts_ns_158_112_image_alignment.py` — prints alignment table for Transcriptions.txt / NLI manifest / CUDL manifest / nli_images for one sys_id. |
| **CUL positional image mismatch: `/api/cambridge_image/{sys_id}?page={p-1}` indexes a CUDL canvas list whose length does not match transcription page count (260419-nwv follow-up)** | `web/api.py` (cambridge_image handler), `web/pages/browse.py:3440-3441` (active_source=cambridge default), `desktop/viewers.py` (ManuscriptViewerWidget.load_images), `genizah_app.py` (browse + nav sites), `genizah_core.py` (enrich_metadata image default) | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-21) | Surfaced 2026-04-19 via the diagnostic in `scripts/debug_ts_ns_158_112_image_alignment.py`. On T-S NS 158.112 (sys_id 990051537270205171, CUL), Transcriptions.txt has 14 pages but the CUDL manifest `MS-TS-NS-00158-00112` only has **12 canvases** (labels `1r,1v,...6r,6v`); positional indexing therefore serves an image for the wrong folio past canvas 12, and the off-by-one can propagate earlier when CUDL inserts cover/binding canvases. **Fixed by 260419-cfx:** (1) `/api/cambridge_image` now resolves transcription page N → CUDL canvas by matching (folio_num, side) against the N-th `nli_images` row (via `resolve_cambridge_canvas_for_page` in `shared/nli_crossref_service.py`); when no CUDL canvas matches, the endpoint falls back to the NLI image with `X-Image-Fallback-Source: nli`, `X-Folio-Matched: {folio}{side}`, `ETag "{sys_id}-p{page}-v2"`, and `X-Image-Resolver-Version: 2`. Desktop mirrors the same logic on both INITIAL load AND prev/next navigation; desktop NLI fallback uses `resolve_volume_suffix(sid, current_browse_volume_ie)` for multi-IE correctness. (2) Follow-up commits `ebf84e1b` + `8bab6263`: when CUDL canvas count != NLI/transcription count, both apps now default the whole manuscript to NLI (CUDL still available via manual source toggle). **H3 retraction:** the prior SUMMARY for 260419-nwv claimed NLI IIIF manifest was resolving the wrong IE for T-S NS 158.112; deeper probing shows the manifest is correct — `Transcriptions.txt` references text-layer FLs (FL167150424–437) that 500 on image GET, while the IIIF manifest returns image-layer FLs (FL167150439–452) in the same IE167150422. Both layers are valid; `fetch_fl_ids_from_nli` is serving the right one. No code change to IE-suffix logic. Verification: `pytest tests/test_nli_crossref_service.py` passes (77 pre-existing + 20 new = 97), `scripts/debug_ts_ns_158_112_image_alignment.py --verify-resolver` emits `RESOLVER CUL-canvas-fix VERIFIED`. **REMAINING GAP (2026-04-20):** the count-match heuristic does not catch the case where CUDL and NLI have the **same count but different canvas order** (e.g. CUDL prepends a binding canvas and drops the last folio — counts still match, but positions `[i]` point to different leaves). For those manuscripts both apps still default to CUDL and can silently show a wrong-leaf image. NLI canvas order is authoritative (transcription FL ids are derived from NLI). Proper fix: align canvases by (folio_num, side) regardless of count, or always use NLI when any per-position (folio, side) disagreement is detectable between the two lists. Needs a follow-up quick task once the affected-shelfmark population is sampled. **Sampling update (2026-04-21):** bounded live check of 25 successful Cambridge manifests already found a real same-count misordered case: `Or.2245` (sys_id `990001332980205171`) has `42` NLI folio rows and `42` CUDL canvases, but position 2 is `NLI=2r` vs `CUDL=1v` and the sequences keep diverging. This confirms the residual bug is production-real, not theoretical. **Implementation caveats surfaced in the review:** (a) scope the default-to-NLI rule to Cambridge/CUL records where NLI is actually authoritative — other codepaths explicitly treat Manchester, JTS, and private CUDL collections (Mosseri/Gaster/etc.) as cases where NLI images may be catalog stubs and external images should stay primary; (b) avoid duplicating ad-hoc length/position logic in `genizah_core.py`, `web/pages/browse.py`, `desktop/viewers.py`, and `genizah_app.py` — compute one shared alignment verdict/reason and have both web and desktop consume it. **Fix landed 2026-04-21 (260421-aln):** new `classify_cambridge_alignment(sys_id, images_ext, *, svc)` helper in `shared/nli_crossref_service.py` returns a 3-valued verdict (`aligned | misaligned | unknown`) with reason (`ok | count_mismatch | position_mismatch | no_sidecar | no_ext`), computed once in `enrich_metadata` and gated on `external_provider == 'cambridge' and lib_code == 'CUL'` so non-CUL Cambridge (Mosseri/Gaster/private CUDL) is untouched — Codex caveat (a) preserved. The verdict is stored as `current_meta['cambridge_alignment']` and surfaced onto the web `Page` dataclass via `browse_enrichment.py`. All 5 previously-duplicated decision sites (genizah_core enrichment, browse.py:3445, desktop/viewers.py:917, genizah_app.py:7188 + 9394) now read the shared verdict (Codex caveat (b)) and fall back to the legacy length check only when the verdict was not computed. Unparseable CUDL canvases (binding/cover) are skipped per-position rather than forced into a mismatch, so an aligned manuscript with a leading binding still defaults to CUDL. Per-page `resolve_cambridge_canvas_for_page` is unchanged — still used for manual Cambridge viewing. Tests: 7 new `TestClassifyCambridgeAlignment` cases covering aligned/ok, count_mismatch (T-S NS 158.112 fixture, 14 vs 12), position_mismatch (Or.2245 pattern, diverges at index 2), unparseable-position skip (with real-mismatch followup), and unknown/no_ext + unknown/no_sidecar edge cases. Full suite 1129 passed / 8 skipped. |
| **FJMS catalog dialog is empty for ~30.6K manuscripts because their source is labeled 'Instatution' (Institution typo) and the dialog's GENERIC_SOURCE_NAMES filter drops those rows** | `shared/fjms_service.py` (`GENERIC_SOURCE_NAMES` filter), `scripts/fix_instatution_sources.py` (migration), `fist_data/fjms_enrichment.db` (`catalog.SourceName`, `catalog_free_desc.SourceName`), `fist_data/FIST.db` (`CODE_Institution`) | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-21) | Surfaced 2026-04-20 investigating CUL T-S NS 325.82 (sys_id 990051720350205171): FJMS website shows source attributions "The Fleischer Piyut Project", "GRU – Cambridge", and "Uri Ehrlich, as displayed in CUL website", but the app's catalog dialog renders empty. **Root cause:** `fjms_enrichment.db::catalog` has 267,104 rows with `SourceName='Instatution'` (sic — typo inherited from export logic) across 88,313 distinct UnitCatalogRecIds and 140,226 distinct AlmaIds; `catalog_free_desc` adds 47,800 more `Instatution` rows across 44,669 AlmaIds. `shared/fjms_service.py::GENERIC_SOURCE_NAMES` includes `'Instatution'`, so `get_source_names()` / `get_catalog_source_counts()` and `show_catalog_dialog()` (`web/components/catalog_dialog.py:62-65`) all suppress these records. The previous attribution pass (`scripts/attribute_sources_via_api.py`) only processed rows with `SourceName='Personal handlist - preliminary'`, so it never touched the `Instatution` rows. **Scope clarification:** 31,618 AlmaIds currently have **only generic** catalog sources and therefore render empty dialogs; 30,625 of those are specifically in the `Instatution` bucket, while the remaining 993 are separate `Inventory` / `Nuscha` generic-only cases. **Important review finding:** the existing `source_attribution_checkpoint.json` Panel 7 map is not trustworthy for this repair (it maps SubIds 1/4/7 to catalog names like `Zulay/Widder Catalog`, `Worman Catalog`, `Davis/Outhwaite IV Catalog`, which conflicts with both the manuscript sample and `FIST.db::CODE_Institution`). `FIST.db` already contains the correct local institution lookup: `CODE_Institution.InstitutionId = SubId`, with the exact desired names for SubIds 1..8 (`The Fleischer Piyut Project`, `GRU – Cambridge`, `Uri Ehrlich...`, etc.). **Recommended fix path:** do **not** rely on the saved Panel 7 API bridge for SourceId 400. Instead, update both `catalog` and `catalog_free_desc` by joining through `dbo_Signature` (`SourceId=400`) to `CODE_Institution`, and write both English and Hebrew labels (`EngDescAc`/`HebDescAc` or agreed display fields) so Hebrew UI headers stop showing generic `מוסדות`. Back up `fjms_enrichment.db` first, then validate T-S NS 325.82 plus a few SubId 5/6/8 samples. **Reproducer:** `python -c "import sqlite3; c=sqlite3.connect('fist_data/fjms_enrichment.db').execute('SELECT COUNT(*) FROM catalog WHERE SourceName=\"Instatution\"').fetchone()[0]"` previously returned 267,104; now returns 0. **Fix landed 2026-04-21 (commit `74c2e1f2` + migration run):** `scripts/fix_instatution_sources.py --apply` rewrote 267,104 `catalog` rows and 47,800 `catalog_free_desc` rows using the local `CODE_Institution` join (not the Panel 7 API checkpoint). 100% resolution rate across 8 distinct SubIds — top by volume: GRU – Cambridge (161K), Schocken – Zulay Poetry Catalog (51K), The Fleischer Piyut Project (30K), Yad Harav Herzog (15K), Uri Ehrlich (8K). Backup written to `fist_data/fjms_enrichment_pre_instatution_20260421_140512.db`. Post-apply service-layer verification on sample sys_id 990051720350205171 (T-S NS 325.82): `get_source_names` returns 3 named sources (was `[]`), `get_catalog_source_counts` returns 3 (was 0), `get_catalog_detail` records carry populated `source_name_heb`. 4 new regression tests in `tests/test_fjms_service.py` (service-layer, not UI-level) assert suppression vs recovery across all three consumers and catalog/free_desc label consistency. **Residual `Inventory`/`Nuscha` buckets — accepted as-is (2026-04-21).** After the Instatution migration, `catalog` still has 28,004 `Nuscha` + 21,629 `Inventory` rows and `catalog_free_desc` still has 16,563 `Nuscha` + 2 `Inventory` rows, producing 993 catalog-only-generic AlmaIds and 3,398 free_desc-only-generic AlmaIds. Decision: leave suppressed. Investigation: `Inventory` (SourceId=700) SubIds map to `CODE_Collection.CollectionId` (e.g. 740→Manchester, 101→T-S A-K) — these are library accession records, not scholarly attributions, so suppression is semantically correct. `Nuscha` (SourceId=200) SubIds map to `CODE_Author.AuthorId`, but per the 2026-03-12 handlist audit the `CODE_Author` join is STALE for SourceId=300 (wrong scholars) and cannot be trusted for SourceId=200 without per-sample FJMS-site validation. Spot check: sample all-generic catalog AlmaIds (e.g. sys_id 990000893660205171 = Yevr. III A 6) all trace to `(200, 2)` → proposed "Abitbol, Michel", which is implausibly uniform for Russian NL Yevr. III material. Migrating without validation risks painting wrong-attribution labels onto ~44K records, worse than current suppression. A future follow-up could (a) un-generic-filter `Inventory`/`Nuscha` so the labels appear as-is, or (b) validate the `CODE_Author` mapping against FJMS per-sample and migrate if safe — but is deliberately out of scope for this entry. |
| **Internal NiceGUI `/_nicegui/` asset URLs can surface in Search Console as pages** | `web/framework_patches.py`, `web/main.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-20) | NiceGUI's internal ESM/module asset URLs are crawlable for rendering, and Google can occasionally report them as "pages" when it hits directory-style `/_nicegui/{version}/esm/{key}/` requests. The existing `web/framework_patches.py` monkey-patch already converts the upstream NiceGUI directory crash from `500` to `404` by adding an `is_file()` guard. Follow-up fix on 2026-04-20: added `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` for all `/_nicegui/` responses in `web/main.py`, so valid framework assets remain fetchable for rendering but are explicitly marked non-indexable. This should allow the reported `5xx`/`404` asset URLs to age out of Coverage after revalidation. |
| **Recurring NiceGUI `parent_slot has been deleted` RuntimeError spams journalctl** | `web/main.py:519`, `web/pages/home.py:38,222` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-22) | Found 3 remaining `ui.timer(...)` callsites in ephemeral containers: (1) What's New banner auto-dismiss on site header (`web/main.py:519`, 10s), (2) OCR disclaimer banner auto-dismiss on /home (`web/pages/home.py:38`, 10s), (3) carousel slide auto-rotate on /home (`web/pages/home.py:222`, 15s recurring). All three converted to `asyncio` scheduling per the memory note: one-shot dismiss banners use `asyncio.get_event_loop().call_later(10.0, _auto_dismiss)` with idempotent `try/except` around `.delete()` so re-firing on a user-dismissed banner is a no-op; the recurring carousel uses `asyncio.ensure_future(_auto_rotate())` with a `while True: asyncio.sleep(15.0)` loop that `return`s on the first exception when `_next_slide()` hits deleted slide cards. No `ui.timer(` callsites remain under `web/` after this pass. |
| **Phase 70 partial puzzle extraction removed required Qt imports before removing the inline puzzle classes, causing desktop startup to crash with `NameError: QGraphicsPixmapItem`** | `genizah_app.py`, `desktop/puzzle.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-16) | Restored the missing `math`/PyQt imports in `genizah_app.py` so the still-present inline puzzle block remains importable while Phase 70 work is in progress. Follow-up cleanup should finish removing the duplicate inline puzzle classes so `genizah_app.py` relies solely on `desktop/puzzle.py`. |
| **CI still ran Ubuntu jobs on Python 3.10 after the pinned dependency set moved to Python 3.11** | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `requirements*.txt`, `.cursorrules`, `docs/guides/DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-15) | `requirements.txt` / `requirements-lock.txt` pin `numpy==2.4.3`, so the repo no longer has a coherent Python 3.10 story. | **Refinement counts switch units after replay/session restore** | `shared/refinement.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `genizah_app.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-29) | Fixed in v7.4.0: `replay_chain()` now uses `len(results)` (page-level) consistently. |
| **Phase 54 desktop post-search measurement filters run before fresh summaries arrive, so rows can be filtered against stale prior-search data and are not re-applied after the new batch fetch completes** | `genizah_app.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-27) | Re-review confirmed `_launch_enrichment_workers()` now reapplies `_apply_results_table_filters()` after `get_measurement_summaries_batch()` completes, so active post-search measurement filters are reevaluated against fresh data. |

Cache only persists non-empty results to avoid warmup poisoning. |
| **Direct `/search?q=` requests inherit stale saved mode/filters/results from prior sessions** | `web/pages/search.py`, `web/search_bootstrap.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-27) | Route-driven searches now bootstrap from a clean deterministic state: URL query/tag/mode/browse requests no longer restore old `search_mode`, hidden filters, exclusions, or cached results from NiceGUI user storage unless the route explicitly requests that context. This prevents live-site quick searches from unexpectedly collapsing to a tiny result set because of stale per-origin browser state. |
| **Desktop What's New dialog: RTL text alignment not fully right-aligned in Hebrew** | `genizah_app.py` | 🟡 Partially Fixed (2026-04-22) | Multi-layer block-alignment RTL fix in `WhatsNewDialog.__init__`: (1) wrapping `<div>` now carries `dir='rtl'` + `align='right'` + `text-align: right` so the block-level container itself is RTL instead of just the inner `<p>` elements; (2) each `<p>` gets explicit `dir='rtl' align='right'` alongside the pre-existing `text-align: right`; (3) the QLabel itself now gets `setAlignment(Qt.AlignRight | Qt.AlignTop)` in Hebrew (and `AlignLeft | AlignTop` in English), which forces the QLabel to align its rich-text content block correctly regardless of word-wrap behavior. **REMAINING (2026-04-22, surfaced during 7.9.2 drafting):** inline parenthesized Latin runs inside Hebrew text (e.g., `(PGP)`) can still render with mirrored parens in the Qt rich-text widget because the BiDi algorithm resolves the neutral paren direction from the surrounding Hebrew context. Current workaround: manually wrap such runs in LRM (`\u200E`) marks in the translation strings — e.g., `פרינסטון \u200E(PGP)\u200E — ...`. Proper fix: either auto-inject LRM around Latin-in-parens runs in `tr()` or the HTML builder, or wrap such runs in `<bdi>` / LRI+PDI isolates in the rich-text template so translators don't have to remember the marks per-string. |
| **Shared filter recompute generation guard still allows stale results after clearing the last active filter** | `web/components/filter_panel.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-21) | Follow-up review confirmed `c2734168` increments `_filter_recompute_gen` before the empty-filter early return, so older in-flight recomputes now discard their stale results instead of repopulating cleared filters. |
| **Shared filter option builders still read UI language inside `run.io_bound()` wrappers** | `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/parallels.py`, `web/components/filter_panel.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-21) | Follow-up review confirmed both pages now capture `lang = get_language()` in client context and pass the shared `build_*_options` functions directly into `run.io_bound()`, removing the worker-thread language lookup. |
| **Fullscreen browse fallback now targets `fsEditViewer`, but the shared fallback handler assumes every viewer has `init()`** | `web/pages/browse.py`, `web/static/manuscript_viewer.js` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-21) | Follow-up review confirmed `7dda767d` guards all three `window[viewerName].init()` calls with `typeof ... === 'function'`, so fallback success no longer throws for fullscreen `fsEditViewer` while main-page viewers still reinitialize normally. |
| **`state.current_search_query` declared in `web/state.py` but never assigned anywhere — Excel/Word filenames silently default to "genizah.xlsx" (regression from v7.9 decomposition)** | `web/state.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/parallels.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-27) | Phase 77 Plan 01 (commit `2c5e94d5`): `state.current_search_query`, `current_search_mode`, `current_search_gap`, `last_filters_applied`, `last_search_warnings` now populated at all three search-execute paths in `web/pages/search.py` (~4076 happy path, ~4026 cancelled-partial, ~3740 history-restore) and the parallels paths in `web/pages/parallels.py` (~2182 success, ~1894 history-restore) — driven by the JSON-export envelope-echo work, so Excel/Word filenames now produce meaningful timestamped names instead of defaulting to `genizah.xlsx`. Phase 77 Plan 04 (commit `f8b508de`) wires the JSON download handlers that consume the same fields. |
| **Browse enrichment worker cleanup no longer disconnects stale callbacks after generation-guard refactor** | `genizah_app.py:14691-14699` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-20) | Follow-up review confirmed `_start_browse_enrichment()` now stores the lambda in `_browse_enrich_slot` and disconnects that exact callable before replacing the worker, so the generation-guard refactor once again removes old browse enrichment connections correctly. |
| **Parallels page extreme rageclicks (365/822 total, 44% of all site frustration)** | `web/pages/parallels.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-24) | PostHog showed 365 rageclicks on /parallels out of 822 site-wide. | **Google OAuth PKCE code_verifier lost between redirect and callback** | `web/supabase_client.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-24) | Supabase Python client defaulted to PKCE flow but stored code_verifier in singleton memory storage that was lost on client reset or multi-user race. Also added PostHog tracking for OAuth login_success/login_failed on the callback page (with POSTHOG_SCRIPT injection). |
| **Login PostHog tracking gaps: 67% failure rate appeared inflated** | `web/auth_state.py`, `web/supabase_client.py`, `web/main.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-24) | Multiple tracking gaps: (1) "no user returned" path never fired login_failed, (2) login_success fired right before page reload (JS injection race), (3) Google OAuth completely untracked, (4) login_failed lacked error_code/status_code, (5) sign_in blocked event loop. Fixes: added missing login_failed event, 300ms delay before reload for PostHog flush, OAuth callback tracking, enriched error properties, wrapped sign_in in run.io_bound(). |

| **Dev mode zombie processes block port on Windows** | `web/main.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-24) | Added _find_free_port() that auto-finds next available port (8081→8090) in dev mode (reload=true). Production mode uses configured port strictly. |
| **Browse shelfmark search drops alternate CSV call-number variants for non-Mosseri records** | `genizah_core.py`, `web/services.py`, `web/pages/browse.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-24) | Fixed in `MetadataManager`: all `libraries.csv` `call_numbers` variants are now stored for every record, `_iter_shelfmark_sources()` indexes those variants instead of just one preferred label, and `_get_shelfmark_index()` now rebuilds when a background-startup search had cached an empty index before `csv_bank` finished loading. | **Web auth can get stuck retrying an already-used refresh token** | `web/supabase_client.py`, `web/auth_state.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-25) | `_clear_stale_auth()` added to `supabase_client.py` — detects terminal token errors (consumed/expired/invalid refresh tokens) and clears `auth_session` from NiceGUI user storage, breaking the retry loop. | **NLI manifest failures are retried immediately and can hammer iiif.nli.org.il during thumbnail bursts** | `web/api.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-25); further hardened (2026-05-25, Phase 98) | `fetch_fl_ids_from_nli()` now has a 60-second negative cache (`NLI_FAIL_CACHE_TTL`). Failed sys_ids return empty immediately during cooldown instead of retrying, preventing synchronized timeout bursts. **2026-05-25 (Phase 98):** further hardened with shared circuit breaker (`shared/nli_circuit_breaker.py`) that trips after 3 consecutive failures across ANY NLI call site and short-circuits for 60s; per-call timeouts reduced from 15s to 3-5s read + 3s connect; semaphore wait dropped from 20s to 1s. The per-sys_id negative cache and the global breaker now coexist as complementary layers. See `docs/INCIDENT-2026-05-25-nli-iiif-hang.md`. |
| **NLI FL-ID cache resets on service restart and triggers semaphore timeout storms while the cache rewarms** | `web/api.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-25) | Added a restart-persistent positive FL-ID cache at `Config.INDEX_DIR/nli_fl_ids_cache.json`, loaded on startup with a 30-day disk TTL. Also made the semaphore cap/timeout env-configurable (`NLI_MAX_CONCURRENT_FETCHES`, `NLI_SEMAPHORE_TIMEOUT`) for future tuning. |

| **Web puzzle canvas v49 is not app-ready: fragment-meta race, dead threshold slider, delete not persisted, and processed-image response can advertise the wrong MIME type** | `web/pages/puzzle.py`, `web/api.py`, `shared/puzzle_image_service.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-03-16) | Fixed in multiple passes on 2026-03-16. The final root cause for “images are not loading” was that `ui.html('<canvas id="puzzleCanvas"></canvas>')` rendered as an empty `<div>` in NiceGUI, so Fabric never found a canvas element and every add was queued forever. Replaced it with a real `ui.element('canvas').props('id=puzzleCanvas')`, then verified in a live headless browser that Fabric initializes, `/api/puzzle_image` loads, and the fragment is present on the canvas. Earlier same-day fixes also added JS→Python add-result/meta events, persisted delete handling, selection sync, processed/original toggle reloads, threshold reprocessing wiring, and MIME-safe API responses. |

| **LOCAL PDF text extraction reverses word order on some books (RTL line read last-word-first)** | `shared/local_indexer.py` / PDF text-extraction path (PyMuPDF `get_text`) | ✅ Fixed for new scans (2026-05-28); existing libraries need manual Reset + re-scan | Root cause: PyMuPDF `get_text("text", sort=True)` (the Phase 96 single-word-per-line fallback) sorts glyph runs by ascending x-coordinate (LTR visual order), which reverses Hebrew/Arabic reading order for the entire line. Fixed in Phase 101 (v7.15): per-line **directional-run word-token reversal** (USER-DEC-1 / S-1) inside the `get_text("text", sort=True)` fallback in `shared/local_indexer.py::extract_pdf_pages`, gated on `_rtl_ratio > 0.4` (helpers `_fix_sort_true_rtl_line` / `_fix_sort_true_rtl_page`). Directional-run preserves embedded Latin shelfmarks/folio numbers adjacent (e.g. `T-S 12.123` stays as one Latin run while the surrounding Hebrew flips). **NOT** `python-bidi` — that char-reverses already-correct Hebrew letters (`שלם`→`מלש`); we reverse word tokens, not characters. **D-04 auto-self-heal-on-launch ROLLED BACK 2026-05-28 post-UAT** — the initial Phase 101 attempt flipped every committed PDF row to `'pending'` via an extractor-version marker bump in `LocalIndexer.__init__`, expecting the system to auto-re-extract them. Hillel's UAT exposed that `MyLibraryTab._init_indexer()` calls `startup_recovery()` synchronously on the UI thread, and Pass B (re-extract every pending row) is sequential — a 12,513-PDF library would have frozen app launch for hours. Rolled back to: code change only, no auto-flip, no marker. **Recovery procedure for existing libraries:** use the "Reset My Library" / "אפס ספריה שלי" button in the My Library tab to clear the index, then re-add the folders to trigger a full scan via the `LocalIndexerWorker` background thread (cancellable, progress-tracked). New PDFs added to existing libraries get the RTL fix automatically. PDF OCR for image-only PDFs (D-F2) remains deferred. A proper "auto-re-extract on extractor version bump" mechanism (writes a marker but defers the flip-and-rescan to the background worker, not `startup_recovery`) is deferred to a future phase. |
| **Phase 100 review WR-01/WR-02: Browse double-lookup empty pane + discard_scope _pending retention** | `genizah_app.py::_open_local_browse_page`, `desktop/pdf_image_controller.py`, `tests/test_pdf_image_controller.py`, `tests/test_wr01_open_local_browse_page_ast.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-28) | WR-01: `_open_local_browse_page` now does ONE `self._lookup_local_filepath` call AND has exactly ONE `filepath = ...` assignment (machine-verified by AST reachability + uniqueness checks across all rebind forms — Assign / AugAssign / AnnAssign / NamedExpr / unpack / For / except-as), so `is_pdf` is derived from the same filepath the pane reveal + `controller.request` use — divergence closed (no more revealed-but-empty image pane). WR-02: added `test_discard_scope_clears_pending` pinning that `PdfImageController._pending` has NO entry for the scope (`"dialog" not in ctrl._pending`, NOT `.get(scope) is None`) immediately after `discard_scope`, and that `discard_scope` is idempotent. Both items surfaced in Phase 100 `100-REVIEW.md`, closed in Phase 101 Wave 2 (Plan 101-02). |
| **Phase 101 UAT-driven fixes: LAB-rebuild log spam + bulk-delete os-error-5 storm + i18n leak in remove-folder dialog** | `shared/local_indexer.py::build_lab_side_index`, `shared/local_indexer.py::_delete_file` + `remove_folder`, `desktop/my_library_tab.py` (remove-folder confirm), `genizah_translations.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-28) | Hillel's Phase 101 UAT surfaced three pre-existing bugs that were either dormant or only manifested at his library size (12,513 PDFs). (1) `build_lab_side_index` with a broken callback (the pre-existing Phase 97.3 Finding 2 — `SearchEngine` is missing `lab_index_normalize`; the staticmethod lives on `LabEngine`) used to log one warning per row × ~1.9M rows + freeze the stderr handler; added a 5-consecutive-failure bail + a pre-flight callback probe that aborts before opening the writer (commits `599db50e`, `21378680`). (2) Removing a folder triggered N per-file Tantivy commits, each fighting the live LOCAL searcher's reader handle on Windows = N × ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED warnings + minutes of UI freeze; `_delete_file` gained a `commit=False` kwarg, `remove_folder` now does ONE retry-protected commit via `_commit_writer_with_retry()` followed by a batched SQLite cleanup (commit `c1fef1f6`). (3) The remove-folder confirm dialog interpolated the folder path into the `tr()` lookup key via f-string, so the translation could never match in Hebrew mode; switched to `tr("...{}...").format(folder_path)` matching the codebase pattern at lines 1211/2323/2351/2366 of `desktop/my_library_tab.py`, and added the Hebrew translation to `genizah_translations.py` (commit `d5ed0e3a`). The LAB underlying bug (`lab_index_normalize` missing on `SearchEngine`) is still deferred per Phase 97.3 Finding 2 — Hillel: "LOCAL lab index is not crucial" — but it no longer freezes anything. |
| **LOCAL PDF text extraction: in-paragraph line breaks not reflowed (each visual line wrapped at the PDF's column break becomes its own line in transcription)** | `shared/local_indexer.py::extract_pdf_pages`, `shared/local_indexer.py::_collapse_intra_block_newlines` | ✅ Fixed for new scans (2026-05-28); existing libraries need manual Reset + re-scan | Source PDF flows continuous Hebrew paragraphs inside narrow columns; PyMuPDF's `get_text("blocks")` returned blocks ≈ paragraphs but each block's body still contained the source PDF's column-wrap `\n` characters — and on RTL Hebrew the bidi engine made it dramatically worse, putting individual characters / commas / quote marks on their own line within a block (e.g. `הכרעת רבי העומדת במרכז ה\nסוגיא\n,\nהינה הכרעה` for what should be one sentence). Hillel's UAT 2026-05-28 paste showed ~30 fragment-per-line entries for what should be a few continuous paragraphs. **Fix:** new helper `_collapse_intra_block_newlines(block_text)` in `shared/local_indexer.py` aggressively collapses ALL intra-block `\n` (and surrounding whitespace) to a single space; `extract_pdf_pages` calls it for each PyMuPDF block before the `\n\n` inter-block join. Per-block boundaries from PyMuPDF still mark true paragraph breaks — only intra-block visual wraps + bidi fragmentation are joined. Per Hillel's guidance: "Join is better than divide if any problem exists." Tests in `tests/test_local_pdf_extraction_fallback.py` cover empty/single-line/multi-line/whitespace-surrounded-newline cases plus an end-to-end `_MultilineBlocksFakePage` integration test. **Recovery for existing libraries:** Reset My Library + re-add folders to trigger a full re-scan via the `LocalIndexerWorker` background thread; new PDFs added to existing libraries get the fix automatically. |
| **Pre-existing i18n coverage gaps: many user-facing strings not in `TRANSLATIONS` (Hebrew users see English)** | desktop: `genizah_app.py` (Reading Desk toolbar/menu ~7351/6930, Settings HTR-aware checkbox+tooltip 932, variant/search-mode controls ~744/6040/6067/6174, Version Selector 10435, lists cloud-sync dialog 3720+, data-update dialog 24220+, shelfmark/document not-found bodies 9800+, raw `QMessageBox` titles 25685/23300), `desktop/viewers.py` (image-viewer tooltips 432-529), `desktop/dialogs_filter.py` (864+), `corrections_ui.py` (discovery/comment dialogs 2013+); web: `web/main.py` (theme/nav/copy aria-labels 933/1007/1198/1228), `web/components/project_tree.py`, `web/pages/search_results.py`, `web/pages/browse.py`, `web/pages/search.py`, `web/pages/settings.py`, `web/components/add_to_list_dialog.py`, `web/pages/corrections.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-06-01) | **Resolved 2026-06-01:** added **246 Hebrew translations** to `genizah_translations.py` via a `TRANSLATIONS.update({...})` block appended after the main literal (222 previously-missing user-facing strings + 24 case/punctuation near-misses that reuse the existing Hebrew of their sibling key; the proper noun `PGP` intentionally left in English). An AST scan that extracts every `tr("literal")` arg and diffs against `TRANSLATIONS` now reports **0 remaining genuine gaps** (was 223). Inline-bilingual patterns (`tr("עברית") if is_heb else tr("English")` and `(tr(en), tr(he))` tuples) were detected and excluded so no English-user-sees-Hebrew reverse-leak was introduced; both `tr()` impls are language-gated (`genizah_core.py:2735` desktop, `web/translations.py` web) so English is unaffected. 121 translation tests pass; ruff clean. Scan helper: `_tmp/find_missing_tr2.py`. **Original finding** — surfaced by the v7.17 pre-release i18n hunt (42-agent workflow + Codex). Mechanism: a `tr("X")` whose exact literal key is absent from `genizah_translations.py::TRANSLATIONS` returns the English input unchanged (desktop `genizah_core.py:tr`; web `web/translations.py:tr`, default lang `he`), so Hebrew users see English. Watch for near-miss keys that differ only by **case or punctuation** (dict has `Zoom in`/`Zoom out` but `viewers.py` calls `Zoom In`/`Zoom Out`; dict has `Shelfmark not found` but a call site uses `Shelfmark not found: {}`). Fix path = dedicated i18n-coverage pass: extract every `tr()` string-literal argument across desktop + web, diff against `TRANSLATIONS` keys, add the missing Hebrew values. Also clean up dead orphaned keys `Welcome to Genizah Search Pro` (`genizah_translations.py:1478`) and `Genizah Search Pro v5.0` (`:1564`). **Note:** the three hardcoded-Hebrew *leaks* from the same hunt (Visual Similarity header gloss `genizah_app.py:4784`; copy-info fallback labels `genizah_app.py:13725-13738`; parallels `קטגוריה:` inverted ternary `web/pages/parallels.py:1347`) were FIXED in the 2026-06-01 rebrand polish pass, as was a broken Help-page GitHub link (`/tree/main/`→`/tree/master-main/`, `web/pages/help.py:630/1247`). |

## Deferred to v7.15+ (Phase 95 follow-up backlog)

| D-F4 | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-24) PDF text extraction quality audit | Phase 96 plan 96-02 fixed the one-word-per-line extraction defect: switched PyMuPDF extraction to `get_text("dict")` block-level join, collapsing per-word spans into full-line text. Test fixture `tests/fixtures/local_indexer/single_word_per_line.pdf` regression guard installed. | P2 |
| D-F7 | ⚠️ Known limit (2026-05-24) View All capped at 200 pages for large LOCAL files | Phase 96-09 iter-6: `_open_local_browse` (view-all path) now shows a warning dialog when a LOCAL file has > 200 pages. Default action is "Show first 200 pages"; Cancel returns to per-page mode. Cap prevents main-thread freeze when aggregating multi-hundred-page PDFs into a single QTextEdit. Follow-up option: background thread (QThread) to build the aggregated text off-main-thread and set it when done, removing the cap entirely. See `genizah_app.py:_VIEW_ALL_PAGE_CAP`. | P3 |
| D-F13b | ✅ Improved (2026-05-31) De-space rewrite: edge-gap + Unicode-Mn + per-line adaptive + LTR-guard fix | Follow-up to D-F13. The Phase 102 de-space used **center-x→center-x** gaps vs a 1.8× per-line median, which conflated letter *width* with *spacing* (wide letters מ/ש/ה shattered off justified words: `פירוש המשנה`→`פירו ש ה מ ש נה`). Replaced with **edge-to-edge whitespace** (`next.x0 − prev.x1`) normalized by font size — the corpus is cleanly bimodal here with a valley at ~0.45×em (verified across 90 books / 300K+ gaps). Three further fixes: (1) combining-mark test switched from the range `0x05B0–0x05C7` to Unicode category `Mn` — the old range mis-treated maqaf `־`/sof-pasuq as vowels (corrupting ranges like `סב־סג`) and missed te'amim; (2) **per-line 1-D Otsu valley** on the normalized edge gaps (`_word_gap_fraction`, clipped + bounded) — a single global fraction cannot separate every book (word-spaces are ~0.3×em in tightly-set modern books like *רביצקי*, ~0.7×em normal, vs letter-spaced HEADING tracking ~0.5×em — they overlap), but per line the intra/inter clusters are always bimodal so Otsu finds each line's valley; superseded the earlier `max(0.45, 1.6×median)` adaptive (which fixed shatter but MERGED tight books — `על כן עליכם`→`עלכןעליכם`); (3) **`_ltr_damage_guard` bug** — its token-count check applied to RTL pages, so the *better* (fewer-token) de-space output was discarded for the shattered blocks fallback; gated the count/Jaccard checks to LTR pages per the docstring's own intent. **Measured (full pipeline, identical pages):** אוצר הגאונים ברכות 73.5%→3.0% single-letter tokens; איגרות הרמב״ם 5.2%→0.17%; **על דעת המקום (רביצקי, tight-set) word-merge 15.8%→0.07%** (mean token length 12.3→4.5 — the Otsu refinement specifically fixed this tight-book over-merge that the 0.45 floor caused). Files: `shared/local_indexer_rtl.py` (`despace_line_to_word_units`, `_word_gap_fraction`, `_is_nikud`), `shared/local_indexer.py` (`_ltr_damage_guard`, `extraction_format_version` 2→3). Real-PDF regression fixtures added (`tests/fixtures/local_indexer/glyph_traces/real_*.json` incl. `real_otzar_heading` shatter + `real_ravitzky_tight` merge guards); pinned by `tests/test_local_pdf_rtl_helpers.py::test_real_pdf_despace_regression`, `test_word_gap_fraction_otsu_valley` + `TestLtrDamageGuardRtlTrust`. **Existing LOCAL libraries need a manual "Re-index All" to benefit** (extraction_format_version bumped 2→3; no auto-flip). | P2 |

## 2026-08-12 C-track complete + first rendered_relation asset (superseded header)

*Moved verbatim from `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md`'s Last-Updated line when the region-band amendment landed and the work it narrates became closed. Nothing deleted.*

> **Last Updated:** 2026-08-12 (C-track COMPLETE + the first `rendered_relation` asset + QUOTER re-derived) — **the deploy-1 CANDIDATE exists**: `_tmp/cd_out/discovery-cd-PUBLIC.db` (402 MB, 55,377 identifications), rebuilt by `_tmp/ctrack_bake.ps1` in 5.1 min and the first asset ever built with `discovery_identification.rendered_relation`. **Frame identical on both sides** (`dc15864d…`) — the CD batch is provably additive, asserted against real assets rather than fixtures. Measured on it: `direct_witness` 81.10% / `uncertain` 13.62% / `quotes_this_work` 5.29%; through the member-grain cap **9.15% of claim rows change their chip and 4.75% of stored-direct rows stop asserting a direct match** (the naive stored-vs-verdict comparison says 17.23% and is wrong — the surface renders the CAP, which only ever weakens). **❌ THE ONE RELEASE BLOCKER is `works.genre`:** both verifiers fail with exactly one violation — 181 private / 58 public works reachable from a public surface carry a NULL genre where the contract wants an explicit `Unassigned` bucket. **Pre-existing, not the batch** (the pre-batch asset fails SIX under the same verifier), and closing it is an **owner decision**, because `Unassigned` carries display semantics ruling Q declined once. **QUOTER re-derived through B against the RENDERED baseline, and it changed the answer:** A0c priced expansion T=60 at 20.7% reduction / 3.2% loss on stored relations; on the population the surface now shows it is **16.5% / 3.8%** — less benefit, more cost, because C-track had already demoted some of the rows T=60 would have caught. **A0c's “the bar is unreachable” no longer holds:** it tested only 30/40/50/60, and the frontier now has points at/inside Contract 1's ≤2-point bar on the expansion pane — **T=0.70 = 9.7% at 2.1%**, **T=0.80 = 6.9% at 0.0%**, neither ever priced. On the findings page nothing is worth buying (≤1.5% except T=0.30's 62.3%-at-21.1% collapse), and M2 explains why: **every false findings row is a `quote_ab`** — dropping that grade takes findings false 12.47% → 0.0%, so the failure class is “B quotes A”, which a work-level flag keys on the wrong axis for. ⚠ The high-T points rest on 2–4 rows of 63 (A0c's own floor was n≥30): the frontier's SHAPE is informative, individual points are not precise. **The threshold ruling is the owner's, and there is no bake knob for it** — the builder hardcodes `DEPLOY_1_PARAMETERIZATION`, so shipping T>0 needs that knob added first. Two defects found by running the gates over the bake and fixed: `3e292130` (the verifier's non-fatal genre report counted every NULL-genre work and called them “reachable from NO public surface” one line above the violation counting the same rows as reachable — a leftover from a scoping the code itself records as superseded; mutation-proved both directions) and `78347009` (three suppression tests raised `OperationalError` against a pre-batch local artifact; the guard now checks the COLUMN and skips with a named reason — verified not to be a silencing by staging the new asset: 15 passed, 0 skipped). Full state, artifacts and method: `_tmp/NEXT-SESSION-cd-batch-progress.md`. The superseded Last-Updated narrative moved verbatim to the archive under “2026-08-12 CD batch + C-track (superseded header)”.
