## 2026-08-12 CD batch + C-track (superseded header)

*Moved verbatim from `docs/OPEN_ISSUES.md`'s Last-Updated line when C-track completed and the work it narrates became closed. Nothing deleted.*

> **Last Updated:** 2026-08-12 (CD schema batch COMPLETE + C-track engine half + surface steps 3a/3b) — **schema Amendment 2026-08-12 is written and implemented end-to-end for the STORAGE half of the batch**: the three locus tables (opaque `work_id` keys — raw `locus_ref_id` is deliberately NOT stored; the verifier's raw-id sweep now covers the new tables), `discovery_identification.rendered_relation` (fail-closed `uncertain` until C-track lands the matrix; no surface reads it yet) and `.routing_reason` (the best row's reason, Codex finding 2's rule, pinned by from-scratch recompute test), Contract-4 withholding + stratum-membership storage, the region-map + curated-quoter input tables, the Contract-0 basis-pin gate (`reference_corpus_sha256` = `locus_reference_corpus_sha256`, mutation-proven), `scripts/check_frame_regression.py` (CERT-01 before/after gate; **measured: the regenerated golden's `frame_content_hash` is byte-identical** — the amendment adds nothing to the frame's tuple set, proven by test), projector `PROJECTION_RULES`/FK-closure/count registration, and the loader's `locus_schema_version` marker-conditional requirements (pre-batch assets keep loading; rollback-safe). Lockstep: builder + projector + loader + verifier + regenerated golden fixture + `tests/test_discovery_cd_batch.py` (16 tests) in one commit set; discovery suites green (~1,900 tests), ruff/masking/check_docs exit 0. **Owner rulings 2026-08-12 (recorded in `_tmp/OWNER-RULINGS-2026-08-12.md`):** expansion honesty cue = PER-ROW catalogue-disagree mark (reads existing `novelty_status='diverges_work'`; display wiring in C-track/F1, wording through the honesty gate, "correspond"-register); curated quoter list v1 = BOTH ילקוט שמעוני works (rows relabel to `quotes_this_work`, never deleted); הגדה של פסח section names DELEGATED to the Sefaria structure (D-track skip-branch fix derives them; owner writes nothing). **The population lock is LANDED (same day, second commit set):** `shared/discovery_family.py` (fam-v1 — the A0c-frozen genre/corpus rule + 25 pinned daf-override canonical ids, measured: `base=other_staged ∧ locus grain='daf_rif'`), `scripts/emit_population_lock.py`, the tracked lock at `docs/specs/discovery-population-lock-v1.json` — **reproducing the preflight §2 denominators EXACTLY** (bible 19,885 / canonical 1,823 / daf 1,026 / ja 3,842 / other_staged 1,888 = 28,464) — the `--population-lock` builder input (constants COPIED into meta, never recomputed), and the verifier retention gate (overall ≥90%, per-family ≥80%, breach blocks deploy 2; refuses a fam-version mismatch; mutation-proven). Also corrected same-day: `locus_work.family` CHECK gains `msource_daf` (measured in the artifact; the first draft would have rejected the D-track import). **The Contract-1 input ingests are LANDED (third commit set):** `--curated-quoter` + `--region-map` builder inputs; the tracked curated v1 at `docs/specs/discovery-curated-quoter-v1.json` (BOTH Yalkut works, resolved: `w001383` על נ"ך 35 main-pool rows + `w001384` על התורה 66 — the matrix spec's "101" line matches the PAIR'S total, not נ"ך alone; A0b re-ratifies); region ingest re-keys the owner's 260-row input via the crosswalk, fails closed before the locus import (it rules ON locus units, so it travels with D-track), stores the tri-state (open → NULL), and never stores raw ref ids or Hebrew display labels. Batch storage work is COMPLETE. **C-track's ENGINE half is LANDED (fourth commit set, schema Amendment 2026-08-12b):** `shared/discovery_relation_matrix.py` (`matrix-v1`) implements the frozen six-step matrix as ONE module imported by the builder, the projector, the verifier and (next) the read path, so the verifier's gate compares an asset against the contract rather than the code against itself. `rendered_relation` is now COMPUTED, not fail-closed: `populate_discovery_identification` inserts the placeholder then recomputes over the complete table in the same transaction (step 4's divergence ratio is a per-work aggregate, so no row can know its relation while the table is filling), and **every matrix input proved recoverable from stored columns** (`eligibility_basis`→step 1, `routing_reason`→2, `max_coverage_ppm`→5, `relation_kind`→6, `novelty_status`→4a, `discovery_curated_quoter`→4b) — which is exactly what makes the recompute a function of the artifact. The parameterization travels IN the asset (three `relation_matrix_*` meta keys) and the verifier reads it back rather than assuming deploy 1. `check_relation_matrix_recompute` is row-for-row and mutation-proven four ways: one mis-stored row of eighteen fails; a curated ruling the stored values predate fails; a foreign `matrix-v1`→`v9` version is refused; step 3 activated before a footprint recipe exists fails CLOSED (`RegionInputUnavailable`) instead of recomputing region-blind. QUOTER recipe taken verbatim from the A0a-2 census script (work-level divergence over novelty-checked rows, denominator floor 5), with sub-floor works ABSENT rather than 0.0 — provably equivalent for every admissible T, and the equivalence is exercised. **Frame hash byte-identical again** (`b5f7970e…`) across the golden regen. 32 new tests (65 semantics + 19 wiring). Two honest corrections recorded rather than buried: (1) the SYNTHETIC asset has no coverage at all, so a stock synthetic build reaches **step 5 on every row** and exercises no other branch — the asset-level tests drive the stored columns explicitly, and the first real census arrives with the C-track bake; (2) an apparent projector ordering bug (inputs copied after the grain) was **measured NOT to be one** — `project()` iterates tables `sorted` and sequences the grain in an explicit post-pass, so the curated table is always populated first; the reorder was reverted, the comment corrected to say order is not the mechanism, and the test rewritten to pin the post-pass instead of a vacuous dict order. **The shared eligibility SQL builder is LANDED (fifth commit set, Codex finding 3):** four read paths decided "which rows may a reader see" independently and disagreed three ways — D-13g in the claims query, pre-D-13g shipped-only in the three shared_text/related-pages queries, and NO routing predicate at all in the work-expansion CTE. One `eligibility_clause(mode, prefix)` now serves all four, `_CLAIMS_DEFAULT_ROUTING_CLAUSE` IS its output rather than a second literal, an unknown mode raises instead of falling back to something weaker, and a source-level drift guard fails if the shipped-only literal reappears. **The two halves were measured BEFORE anything changed, and they are wildly asymmetric:** the shared_text population is **zero** (28,684 rows, 28,673 shipped, 0 human-confirmed), so moving those three sites onto D-13g restores nothing today — a consistency fix with no visible effect, hence safe to make, and its behavioural test needs a FABRICATED human-confirmed row plus a mutation control proving the old predicate hid it. The expansion CTE is the opposite: **80,718 of 222,972 rows (36%) are review_only display evidence** (65,719 `gen2_parallel_surface` / 14,213 `gen2_router_not_shipped` / 786 `later_shared_text`), which at the pane's real (work, unit) grain is **88,337 → 48,701**, with 589 of 613 works changing, **58 panes emptying**, and 39,636 pairs reachable ONLY through a review_only row. Both curated Yalkut works lose ~84% of their pane presence (w001384 1,752→273; w001383 1,126→184) — much of what they contribute to the pane is gen-2 parallel-surface material the router deliberately did not ship. That is a decision about what the pane CLAIMS, so the CTE takes an explicit `eligibility` mode **defaulting to today's behaviour**. **✅ RULED the same day (owner, 2026-08-12): the pane keeps every row — publication is NOT gated on human review.** So `ELIGIBILITY_ALL` stays and no code change is owed; the numbers above are the cost of the road not taken. (Terminology note worth keeping: `routing_status='review_only'` is the ROUTER's verdict; human review is the orthogonal `adjudication_status` axis. Hiding those rows would not have created a review queue, it would have removed them.) **The ruling exposed a genuine gap in matrix-spec §3.2, and a live over-claim:** keeping the rows means each still renders something, and **39,036 of the 39,636** pairs reachable only via a router-declined row have **NO published identification at all** (80,718 ineligible-display rows split 28,208 with / 52,510 without). §3.2's cap rule presumes an identification whose verdict caps the member; there is none. Today the pane fills that gap with the raw `claim_type` — so it currently prints **"Direct match"/"התאמה ישירה" on 35,754 rows the router declined to ship (19,981 of them with no published identification)** and "Partial match" on 41,607 more. Resolution needs no new decision: §2's own missing-input rule says absent `relation_kind` → `uncertain`, so such a row renders "Needs review"/"דורש בדיקה" and keeps its place, link and counts. Recorded as §5a.1; lands with C-track's surface half (A0b ratifies wording, not semantics). **Next in C-track:** one shared `rendered_relation()` read on all five surfaces + the softer-register strings + the per-row catalogue mark (these last three touch `genizah_translations.py` and `web/pages/browse_enrichment.py`, held until the parallel `/start` session commits). **Surface half, in progress:** the softer register has LANDED in `shared/discovery_display_strings.py::_RELATION_CHIP` — re-keyed on the RENDERED vocabulary (`ids.RENDERED_RELATION_*`, identical literals for the three shared with `claim_type`, so transition-safe), "Direct match"/"התאמה ישירה" retired, "Partial match" corrected as a MISNOMER to "Includes a quotation"/"כולל ציטוט", and **`uncertain` added — the load-bearing bit**: `relation_chip` raises on an unknown key and `findings_rows.py:1693` wraps it in `except ValueError`, so a missing entry would have silently dropped the chip from every fail-closed row rather than failing loudly. `work_quotes_page` is deliberately absent (owner strings deferred; the matrix provably never emits it, so the raise IS the proof). 902 tests green across display-strings, panel-model, panel-render and both render-smoke suites — the new strings pass `assert_surface_honesty` on real HTML, and the frozen "quotation" wording is now pinned WITH its reason (the gate prohibits the bare word "quotes"). Also corrected: the `sketch-findings-genizahsearch` skill's prose still told future sessions to use the retired labels (`SKILL.md` + 2 reference files); its `sources/` mockups are the historical design record and were left alone. **Step 3b has LANDED (panel claim rows):** the panel's page-level rows now render the matrix output CAPPED at member grain (§3.2), and one field feeds the chip, the headline, the filter code AND D-08a's percentage gate — so a demoted row can no longer advertise "68% of page" beside a chip that says it merely shares text. `cap_member_relation` is the ONE implementation (in the matrix module, mutation-proven four ways: cap disabled, both display reads reverted to the stored column, and the generic-group member chip). Writing it forced three spec amendments: (1) **§3.2's two bullets and its own lead-in sentence disagree in exactly one cell** — identification `shared_text` × member `quotes_this_work`, because §3.1's frozen order puts quotes ABOVE shared — resolved for the principle ("never out-asserts"), priced first at **≤53 of the panel's 150,604 default claim rows**; (2) **§3.2a** extends §5a.1's no-identification→`uncertain` to claim grain, measured **0 of 150,604** default rows and **52,510 of 231,322** behind the review toggle (reconciling exactly with §5a.1's split); (3) **§3.2b** fails `work_quotes_page` closed on either side rather than inventing a rank for a state with no reader strings. Two deliberate asymmetries with the findings page: `relation_kind` SURVIVES on this surface, because it is the anchor identity that travels into the expansion query and is compared there against other rows' stored claim types — a rendered verdict there would mark every demoted row as differing from itself; and `relation_code` is now ABSENT on an `uncertain` row rather than growing a fourth reader-facing filter code nobody has ruled on. The pane's own two chips are step 3d, so until it lands the expansion pane is the one place a reader still sees the pre-matrix register. **Step 3c has LANDED (manuscript summary pane), and it turned out to be a display GAP rather than a wrong label:** the model composed a `relation_chip` for every work on "elsewhere in this manuscript" and `_render_manuscript_pane` dropped it on the floor, so a pane naming a dozen works said nothing about how any of them relates to the manuscript and every one read as an identification. Owner ruled 2026-08-12 that it should show a relation; it now renders as a second neutral chip beside the work, using the panel's OWN established pattern (`_render_generic_group`) rather than a new one. The SQL half went with it: the pane's relation is now the strongest MATRIX output over the work's identifications here (§3.1's last bullet), and the two hand-typed `CASE claim_type` comparators became ONE rank expression GENERATED by `relation_rank_sql` from the matrix module's order, read back by `relation_from_rank` — a query module cannot hold half an ordering without eventually disagreeing with the module holding the other half. Two corrections fell out: the spec's §3 cited `discovery_panel.py:786` for this pane's chip, but 786 is `_render_generic_group` (citation now names the function); and `relation_from_rank` was written coercing (`int(rank)`), which turned the string `"0"` into "Matches this work" and `1.5` into "Includes a quotation" — caught by its own test and tightened to exact ints, since the SQL it inverts only ever returns integers. Mutation-proven three ways (SQL ranking raw claim types, the renderer dropping the chip again, the chip membership narrowed back to `CLAIM_TYPES` so `uncertain` silently vanishes). **Step 3d has LANDED — the expansion pane, and with it the LAST surface that rendered a stored claim type.** Both sides of every pair now render the matrix output capped at member grain: the shared ranked CTE joins each claim's identification (`works` by PK, then `discovery_identification` on the pair its `UNIQUE (sys_id, canonical_work_id)` already indexes), and both producers — the SQL presenter and the pure reference the mirror test compares against — cap through `cap_member_relation`. Codex's pre-flight earned its keep twice: it BLOCKED the cheaper join shape (resolve the work's `canonical_work_id` once and bind it — wrong, because the same fragment builds the CORPUS-WIDE probe form where a bound constant would apply one work's verdict to every work), and it predicted that the pure producer would need the verdict handed to it or the mirror would compare `uncertain` against a real value — which is exactly how the mirror failed first. Measured independently before committing: the count query, which is the latency-critical path a cold panel blocks on, goes 320→440 ms / 225→306 ms / 228→303 ms on the three heaviest works (+33–37%) against a 2.0 s browse budget, and EXPLAIN confirms an index SEARCH. Three decisions worth keeping: (1) **`relations_differ` now compares the RENDERED pair** (spec §3.2c) — on stored values it would both claim a difference where the two chips are identical and hide a real one, and both directions are pinned; (2) the anchor crosses every layer as its ALREADY-CAPPED relation, so `_ANCHOR_IDENTITY_FIELDS` stays three wide with `anchor_rendered_relation` REPLACING the stored type rather than growing to four; (3) that retired the last consumer of `relation_kind` on `SURFACE_CLAIM_FIELDS`, so **the claim surface now carries ONE relation field** and step 3b's "why it carries two" comment is gone — and the honesty gate's carrier floor drops `relation_kind`/`claim_type`/`anchor_claim_type` entirely, which its exact-partition rule required (a classification for a field no allowlist carries is a DEAD ENTRY it fails on, and rightly). Also fixed along the way: the real-artifact expansion probe now checks for the COLUMN and not only the tables, so a pre-batch asset skips with a named reason instead of raising OperationalError inside a test body. Mutation-proven three ways (the SQL presenter skipping the cap; `relations_differ` back on the stored value; the renderer reading `claim_type` again). 2,005 tests green; the single failure is the tracked changelog-anchor test. **Step 4 (the per-row catalogue-disagree mark) is DEFERRED until after B** on Codex's strategy read: it is reader context, not a correction — expansion disagree rows are 66.4% false, so the mark is a risk cue that reduces nothing. **A loader gap found by probing rather than reasoning, and CLOSED the same day:** with three read paths now selecting `discovery_identification.rendered_relation` unconditionally, a PRE-batch asset (which the loader admitted by design — the column was marker-conditional) does not degrade, it **loads, reports itself available, and answers every claims / manuscript-pane / findings read with `unavailable / query_failed`** — measured against the currently-served `discovery-v3-PUBLIC`, not inferred. The column is therefore now in `_REQUIRED_COLUMNS` unconditionally, so such an asset is refused and discovery hides cleanly, which is this loader's own stated job ("the only thing standing between a partially-built asset and a live nav entry that errors on its first query"). That retires HALF of the CD batch's rollback story on purpose: "the deployed asset keeps loading under new code" stopped being reachable the moment a read path depended on the column, and the remaining choice was clean-hide vs. visible outage on every panel. Everything else in the batch — the seven locus tables, the count meta keys, `routing_reason` — stays marker-conditional, and the amended test now asserts BOTH halves. No production exposure: `DISCOVERY_ENABLED` is off, and the C-track bake is already a prerequisite of flag-on. **D-track** (locus import + coverage + `--region-map`, multi-week) unchanged. QUOTER@60 re-derivation through B per rendered surface still owed before A0b. Plan: `_tmp/PLAN-granularity-ship.md` v11; dispositions `_tmp/CODEX-PREFLIGHT-CD.md`. A0c/region/B close records live in `docs/archive/OPEN_ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — grep, don't read.

- ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-13) — `works.genre` was NULL on every work reachable only through the review opt-in, and the release gate now fails on it.** The frozen contract says genre is *"an explicit `Unassigned` bucket, never NULL-as-absent"*. `scripts/build_discovery_sidecar.py::apply_work_genres` writes `works.genre` only for works matched in the curated 136-09 artifact and leaves the rest NULL — **58 of 613 works in the public artifact, 181 of 1,269 private.** Phase 136's first version of the verifier check scoped "reachable" to the DEFAULT population (an identification, or shipped, or human-confirmed evidence), measured **zero** of those NULL rows reachable, and concluded they were inert. That measurement was against the wrong surface: `shared/discovery_service.py::get_claims_for_page` takes `include_review=True`, which sets `routing_clause = ""` — dropping the routing predicate entirely and returning review-only and unreviewed claims with `works.genre` among their columns. Re-measured on that surface, **58/58 public and 181/181 private are reachable — every one.** `scripts/verify_discovery_sidecar.py::check_works_genre_vocabulary` now scopes reachability to *any* surviving claim and consequently **FAILS on the artifacts as built today.** That is a real gap against the contract, not a false alarm — but closing it is a decision, not a one-liner: backfilling `Unassigned` is honest and mechanical, yet `Unassigned` carries display semantics (ruling Q deliberately declined it for `זיכרונות מימי נעוריי` precisely because it would hide the row). **OWNER DECISION 2026-08-04: curate the remaining domains** (over accepting `Unassigned` or dropping genre from the opt-in view). Found by Codex code review 2A, finding 3; control is `tests/test_discovery_release_contract.py::test_null_genre_reachable_only_through_the_review_opt_in_is_a_violation`.  **RESOLVED — and it was not the decision it was filed as.** The `Unassigned` display-semantics question (ruling Q) was barely in play; the cause was a SCOPE DISAGREEMENT between the gate and the artifact that feeds it. `check_works_genre_vocabulary` defines reachable as "any surviving claim, or an identification" (wide, because `include_review=True` drops the routing predicate), while `curate_work_domains.load_worklist` — the producer for the artifact `apply_work_genres` writes from — selected works carrying at least one SHIPPED claim. Works in the gap got no assignment, so the column stayed NULL and the gate failed. The gap WAS the failure population exactly (170 private / 53 public), and all 58 public NULL-genre works were simply ABSENT from the artifact's key set — none was a withheld ruling. Same scope error, same asset, that `assert_author_key_coverage` was corrected for on 2026-08-04 (Codex finding 6), which cited this very docstring as its justification and then widened without the worklist following. Fix: widen `load_worklist` to the verifier's scope, re-emit both curated artifacts (work_domains 1073→1254 rows `sha256:ad6ef23f…`; aliases 96→108 `sha256:ded52f3f…`). 0 changed assignments, 0 changed alias resolutions, all 29 owner rulings preserved verbatim — the rule table places the newly-in-scope works under rules the artifact already applied to their own series. `Unassigned` lands on 30 private / 3 public, and all 3 public carry zero identifications; it is the rule-table fall-through, which `validate_artifact` has always accepted as "a REAL value with its own parent", NOT the held-row default ruling Q declined. Re-baked end to end: **both verifiers report "all invariants pass — clean", the first zero-violation candidate**, with every table count, the full `rendered_relation` census and `frame_content_hash dc15864d…` identical on both sides. 6 tests, mutation-proved by restoring the old predicate in source (5 of 6 go red; the live-asset containment test names all 181). Commit `2e9b409e`.

> **Last Updated:** 2026-08-14 — **SEED-033 salvaged from a worktree it was never committed out of.** The seed and the first two P2 rows below existed only inside `.claude/worktrees/search-preview-mismatch-ff2b24`, on a branch with **zero commits ahead of master-main**, so pruning that worktree would have destroyed the only copy; the three mechanical fixes from the same investigation had already landed as `48506010`. Landed with the salvage: the V0.7-only population is now **exact — 15,539 of 232,450 sys_ids (6.68%)**, audited over the entire live index rather than extrapolated from the original 400-row sample (whether they are invisible *in production* is still conditional on production shipping V0.8 only, which remains unverified); plus a third P2 row for two measured version-display defects — the browse badge can never render "V0.7", and `get_full_text_by_id` has no source filter and was observed returning a 10-character V0.7 record in place of its 491-character V0.8 twin. The remaining open question from SEED-033 is the owner's Option A / B / C decision on PGP default precedence. 2026-08-13 — evening addition: **NiceGUI 3.x client-side `ui.html` sanitization strips `class` attributes** (new P2, root cause of the invisible excerpt highlights; excerpt renderer fixed via `sanitize=False`, sitewide `ui.html` audit still owed — /parallels highlighting is exposed the moment a venv actually installs the `nicegui==3.8.0` pin). Earlier the same day: (**the deploy-1 candidate passes its release verifier with ZERO violations** — a first) — `works.genre` is closed, and it was never the owner decision it was filed as: `check_works_genre_vocabulary` scopes "reachable" to any surviving claim (because `include_review=True` drops the routing predicate), while `curate_work_domains.load_worklist` — the producer for the artifact that populates the column — scoped to SHIPPED claims. The gap between the two scopes WAS the failure population exactly (170 private / 53 public), and all 58 public NULL-genre works were absent from the artifact's key set rather than held for a ruling. Widening the worklist and re-emitting both curated artifacts changed 0 existing assignments and preserved all 29 owner rulings; `Unassigned` lands on 3 public works, all with zero identifications. Re-baked: both verifiers clean, every count / the full `rendered_relation` census / `frame_content_hash dc15864d…` identical on both sides (commit `2e9b409e`). **ONE owner decision now gates the milestone, down from two: the QUOTER threshold** — and it is shippable whichever way it goes. Earlier the same day: matrix step 3 got a source (`discovery_region_band`) — **the contamination the owner named is a BAND inside a work, not the work**, so the work-keyed curated quoter list is the wrong instrument for it: measured, listing `משנה תורה, ספר אהבה` would demote 2,990 claim rows to catch 2,022 prayer-order ones and wrongly take 779 halakhic-body witnesses with them; `תנא דבי אליהו רבה` is midrash, not prayer, but 20 of its 37 direct-rendering rows sit in ONE 1,274-letter window that is verbatim daily liturgy (`רבון כל העולמים`). Schema Amendment 2026-08-13 (V) adds `discovery_region_band` — matrix step 3's offset-keyed region source, keyed on `work_id` (canonical ids collide across editions: 15 groups), half-open, `shared_text` output, same frozen tri-state. **Step 3 could never fire before this**: its input was hardcoded `None` because the only address space was the locus unit, which needs the multi-week D-track. Bands need no locus tables — `discovery_evidence.w_start/w_end` are already 88.1% populated. **It ships DARK** (`region_active=False` is still the owner's ruling) and is priced offline over a copy. Measured with two `source='derived'` candidate bands: private 1,327 identifications leave `direct_witness`, public 21 — the public number is small BECAUSE ספר אהבה is msource/private and reaches no public surface, so the claim is about the MECHANISM, not these rows. Also closes a latent hole the DDL comment always claimed and nothing enforced: `check_single_input_version` now asserts one version per Contract-1 input table, `discovery_curated_quoter` included. 46 new tests, **17/17 mutation-proved**, golden `frame_content_hash` UNCHANGED (`b5f7970e…`). **⚠ A pre-amendment asset is now REFUSED by the verifier** (gate-bearing table absent) — same designed refusal as `21026512`, so re-bake before deploying. **Amendment 2026-08-13 (W) removes the other half of the blockage: the matrix parameterization was a hardcoded CONSTANT in two places, so NO owner ruling on either open parameter was shippable** ("anything shipped at T>0 needs that knob added first"). `--quoter-threshold` and `--region-active` are now bake inputs defaulting to deploy 1 (golden fixture regenerates to identical `content_hash` AND `frame_content_hash`), the two references collapse to one argument, the projector reads the PRIVATE asset's parameterization from meta rather than the constant (a projector using the wrong one emits an artifact failing its OWN recompute gate), and `--golden`/`--smoke` REFUSE the flags rather than ignoring them. **Proved on a real bake, not only a fixture** — the first asset ever baked at a threshold: T=0.70 moves 1,171 identifications into `quotes_this_work` (542 from `direct_witness`, **629 from `uncertain`** — step 4 fires ahead of step 5), frame still `dc15864d…`. Every prior pricing of T was an offline recompute over a copy. Also fixed, from an adversarial review that ran 20,000 randomised trials rather than reading the code: `covering_verdict` returned a CONFIDENT verdict on a malformed evidence span (`discovery_evidence` has no `w_start < w_end` CHECK, unlike the band table), so a degenerate or inverted witness span produced an unwarranted demotion from a meaningless footprint. **The QUOTER threshold is the remaining gate** (`works.genre` closed, above) — and it is shippable whichever way it goes. A third is now answerable: the deploy-1 bake still omits `--reference-corpus-sha256`, but the reason it was declined is resolved on the bake side — slicing `ref_corpus_v2.pkl` at the assets' own stored `w_start` offsets lands exactly on the passages they should name (colophon 166,647; קדיש 183,692; עלינו 195,046), which is direct evidence for what the flag documents. The SQL filter was correct (only committed PDFs flipped; error/failed/skipped untouched), the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` + marker-after-commit ordering was correct, but the architectural assumption that `startup_recovery()` Pass B could digest a 12K-row pending list at app launch was wrong: `MyLibraryTab._init_indexer()` calls `startup_recovery()` synchronously on the UI thread, and Pass B re-extracts every pending row sequentially via `_index_one_file`. Hillel's UAT killed the launch after 60s with no UI visible. Recovery: SQL one-shot flipped 12,513 PDF rows back to `'committed'` (`local_index.sqlite3.phase101-bumped.bak` preserved alongside the live DB), marker deleted. Code: `_CURRENT_EXTRACTOR_VERSION` / `_read_extractor_version` / `_write_extractor_version` / the `__init__` UPDATE block all removed; the 2 corresponding tests deleted. RTL fix itself is intact and applies to all new scans. **Future work:** "Auto-re-extract on extractor version bump" needs a different architecture — write a sentinel marker in `__init__`, but defer the flip-and-rescan to the existing `LocalIndexerWorker` background thread (which is already cancellable and progress-tracked), NOT to the synchronous startup_recovery path. Schedule as a follow-up phase when prioritized. |

### From `5. Code Quality Debt`

| **Supabase URL + anon key defaults** | `supabase_corrections_client.py:62-63` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-22) | Desktop now imports `SUPABASE_URL` / `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` from `shared/supabase_provider.py` (same single source the web uses), dropping the duplicated `os.environ.get(...)` defaults + the ~15-line manual `.env` file parser. To keep desktop entry points working without needing their own `load_dotenv()` call, `shared/supabase_provider.py` now opportunistically calls `load_dotenv()` at module import (wrapped in try/except so missing `python-dotenv` is non-fatal). `load_dotenv()` does not override existing env vars, so the web path that already calls it earlier is unaffected. Closes Phase 64 CR-01. |
| **ilike injection via unescaped user input** | `supabase_corrections_client.py:950,1384,1494,1528` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-22) | Added `_sanitize_ilike_pattern(value)` helper next to the client module's configuration block. Strips PostgREST filter separators (`,` `(` `)` `*`), the LIKE wildcards (`%` `_`), backslashes, and newlines, since PostgREST's `.or_(...)` filter has no portable `ESCAPE` clause to safely pass these through. All 4 callsites now sanitize before interpolation: `list_corrections` (search_text across original/corrected/notes), `get_connected_fragments` (shelfmark), `list_all_fragment_joins` and `list_user_fragment_joins` (query across fragment_a/b shelfmarks). Empty-post-sanitize inputs are treated as no-filter (not a wildcard match). `get_connected_fragments` short-circuits to an empty `ConnectedFragments(shelfmark=..., shelfmark_normalized=...)` response on empty-post-sanitize. Normal shelfmark queries (`T-S NS 37.2`) pass through unchanged. Closes Phase 64 CR-02. |
| **WR-01: `_re` used before import in middleware** | `web/main.py:65,70,95` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-22) | Moved `import re as _re` to the top-of-file import block alongside `asyncio`/`logging`/`os`/`sys`, and dropped the late `import re as _re` at the former L110. Middleware now references `_re.sub()` after the import has been evaluated. |

Return value unchanged. |
| **WR-03: Wrong comment on dead-thread prune exception handlers** | `shared/thread_local_db.py:80,133` | ✅ Fixed (2026-04-15) | Comments corrected during Phase 65 execution to "conn.close() can raise ProgrammingError; safe to ignore". |

| D-F5 | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-24) Search-term highlighting broken for LOCAL hits | Phase 96 plan 96-03 normalized LOCAL hit dict shape: `_build_local_result_dict` now accepts `regex` + `pattern_str` params, returns `highlight_pattern` field matching Genizah hit shape, and returns `None` (filter out) when regex does not match content (D-04.1 Codex HIGH #2 closure). `ResultDialog.load_local_page` uses `highlight_pattern` for in-dialog highlighting. | P1 |
| D-F11 | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-24, Phase 96-09 iter-8) Genizah-only buttons not disabled for LOCAL hits | ResultDialog and Browse panel showed Edit, Comment, Corrections, Joins, Puzzle, View on Ktiv, Version buttons for LOCAL file hits — these actions have no meaning for user-owned files (no Friedberg/NLI catalog entry). Fix: `load_result_by_index` disables the 6 community buttons + rd_version_combo when `_is_local_hit` is True, re-enables on Genizah hits. `_open_local_browse_page` disables the same set plus `btn_b_catalog` and `browse_version_combo`; they are re-enabled by the normal Genizah enrichment callback. | P2 |
| D-F13 | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-29, Phase 102) Hebrew letter-spaced emphasis indexed as separate single-letter "words" | Hebrew typographic convention spaces individual letters for emphasis (e.g. `מ ש נ ה  ת ו ר ה` for "Mishneh Torah"). **Spike 001 found this is FAR bigger than emphasis** — whole justified books (אוצר הגאונים 46% single-letter tokens, רמבם 21%) shatter into single letters. The RTL-gated rawdict rewrite + per-line ~1.8×-median de-space (run before reorder) collapses letter-spaced single-letter tokens back into searchable words; regression-pinned by `tests/test_local_pdf_extraction_e2e.py::test_letter_spaced_defragments`. | P2 |
| D-F13c | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-31) App launch froze: `startup_recovery` Pass B re-extracted a bulk pending backlog synchronously on the UI thread | Surfaced when Hillel ran "Re-index All" (to pick up the D-F13b de-space) and the background worker didn't finish — 169 rows left `status='pending'`. On the next launch `MyLibraryTab._init_indexer` calls `self._indexer.startup_recovery()` **synchronously on the UI thread**, and Pass B (D-21) re-extracts EVERY pending PDF inline → window never showed (same failure mode as the rolled-back Phase 101 D-04 auto-flip; the `startup_recovery` Pass B path was the underlying weapon, never neutralized). **Fix:** `startup_recovery(reextract_pending: bool = True)` — desktop `_init_indexer` now passes `reextract_pending=False`, so Pass B is skipped and the pending rows are left for the `_auto_rescan_on_startup` background worker (which re-extracts pending rows anyway — they miss `scan_all`'s cache-hit branch — cancellable + progress-tracked, off the UI thread). Pass A (pending deletes) stays synchronous (fast, few rows). Tests keep the default `True`. Regression: `tests/test_two_phase_durability.py::test_startup_recovery_defers_pending_when_reextract_false` + `::test_startup_recovery_reextracts_pending_by_default`. Files: `shared/local_indexer.py::startup_recovery`, `desktop/my_library_tab.py::_init_indexer`. NOT caused by the D-F13b de-space change (which never flips rows to pending). | P1 |
| D-F13d | ✅ Fixed (2026-05-31) De-space follow-ups: structured-line over-merge (N1), dropped maqaf (N2), reversed digit runs (N3) | Hillel UAT of the D-F13b output surfaced three residual defects. **N1 (over-merge):** tightly-set headings/citations (e.g. `דו שיח` p103 `פרנץ רוזנצווייגושמואלהוגוברגמן`, abbreviation tables, bibliographies) encode the inter-word space as a **zero-WIDTH space glyph** — the edge gap collapses to ~0 em so the Otsu test in D-F13b cannot see it, and the words merge. (Hypothesis was an "Otsu outlier" — a probe DISPROVED it: the word gaps were ~0.02 em, identical to intra-word gaps; the only signal is the space glyph.) **Fix:** re-introduced the embedded space glyph as a SECONDARY boundary signal, gated LOCALLY so letter-spacing (a space between EVERY letter, e.g. Otzar `ה כ ו כ ב י ם`) is not mistaken for a word-space: a space forces a boundary only when neither immediately-adjacent inter-base position also carries a space. Purely additive (a line with no space glyphs splits exactly as before); kill-switch `_SPACE_BOUNDARY_ENABLED`. A/B on identical pages: Du-Siach merge 0.13%→0.04%, Hakdamot 0.14%→0.00%, **zero shatter added** (Otzar 5.37%→5.36%, Igrot 0.21%→0.20% single-letter — unchanged). **N2 (maqaf):** `דו־שיח`→`דושיח`, `ובעת־ובעונה`→`ובעתובעונה` — already resolved by the D-F13b `Mn` combining-mark fix (the old range-based test stripped maqaf U+05BE as a vowel); the new extractor preserves `דו־שיח`/`ובעת־ובעונה`/`ארץ־ישראל`/`לב־לבה`/`על־ידו`. **N3 (digit reversal):** a year `1977` rendered as `7791`. Root cause: `_order_unit_text_rtl` ordered ALL base glyphs by descending center-x (RTL), reversing inherently-LTR digit runs; the F-A re-reversal in `reorder_word_units_rtl` only fixed the order BETWEEN digit units, never WITHIN one. **Fix:** standard bidi "reverse embedded level run" step — within an RTL unit, flip maximal LTR runs (digits / Latin / numeric separators) back to ascending. A/B on Du-Siach years: OLD `3191,5191,6191,7291,9691` → NEW `1913,1915,1916,1927,1969` (all correct). Files: `shared/local_indexer_rtl.py` (`despace_line_to_word_units` space-glyph pass + `_is_space_glyph`, `_order_unit_text_rtl` + `_is_ltr_base`, `_SPACE_BOUNDARY_ENABLED`). Tests: `test_zero_width_space_glyph_forces_boundary`, `test_letter_spaced_run_spaces_suppressed`, `test_order_unit_keeps_embedded_ltr_run_ascending`, `test_year_in_rtl_line_not_reversed` + real fixtures `real_dusiach_packed_names.json` (N1) / `real_dusiach_year.json` (N3). `extraction_format_version` UNCHANGED at 3 (same Re-index All as D-F13b carries these fixes). **UAT-2 (2026-05-31) — two more cases checked, both NOT new bugs:** (a) `על דעת המקום (רביצקי)` `לאחרמכן` was the OLD format-v2 index — the new code already emits `לאחר מכן` (verified on the real page); needs only the Re-index All. (b) `הקדמות הרמבם למשנה - שילת` p22 abbreviation table `כתביהיד`/`כתבייד` — the maqaf is **entirely absent from the PDF** (no U+05BE text glyph; `get_drawings()` shows 1 drawing / 0 horizontal segments, so not vector-drawn either; letters packed uniformly at +0.08 em with no gap or space). `get_text("words")` returns `כתבייד` as one raw word — the author inconsistently typed the cell without maqaf/space while a sibling cell has a real `כתבי־יד` glyph. **Unrecoverable from text extraction** (no maqaf to restore, no boundary signal to split on) — source-data defect, same class as D-F16; would need OCR or a lexicon. Documented as a known limit, NOT fixed. | P2 |
| D-F14 | ✅ Addressed (2026-05-29, Phase 102 — Meiri reorder core adopted, RTL-gated) Adopt Ephraim Meiri's glyph-level PDF parser for better text extraction | The current LOCAL PDF extraction uses `page.get_text("blocks")` + intra-block newline collapse + S-1 word-token reversal — a heuristic stack that works for most PDFs but has known edge cases (letter-spaced emphasis, OCRed PDFs with bad bbox metadata, mixed-direction lines with attached punctuation, etc.). Phase 102 adopted Meiri's `_normalize_span_dir` reorder core adapted to emit per-page plain text, gated to Hebrew/RTL content per line so LTR/Latin PDFs do not regress (NOT wholesale, NOT the DOCX pipeline); OCR for image-only scans remains split out to `SEED-003`. | P2 |
| D-F16 | ✅ Addressed (2026-05-29, Phase 102) Corrupt PDF text-layer encoding (garbage bytes, no valid ToUnicode cmap) | Spike 001 visual review found `Israeli_Vilna_shabbat_part_2.pdf` extracts garbled text — the PDF's text layer itself is corrupt (bad/missing ToUnicode cmap), so NO reorder or de-spacing can rescue it; the bytes are wrong. Corrupt text-layer PDFs are now detected via a conservative codepoint-garbage ratio (file-level, ≥50% of pages, decided BEFORE any write so no garbage is indexed) and marked `extraction_status = 'corrupt_encoding'` (a future-OCR/`SEED-003` candidate) instead of indexing garbage. | P2 |

| **Excerpt bake dereferences the dead `_tmp\` acquisition-manifest path and accepts only one V4 manifest** | `scripts/bake_discovery_excerpts.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-08-16) | V4.2 plan condition C2. `main()` now takes a required `--sources-bundle` (schema `discovery-excerpt-sources-bundle-v1`): per-stage reference-manifest hash pins, the acquisition manifest supplied by EXPLICIT path and verified against the reference manifest's recorded `acquisition_manifest_sha256` (binding unchanged, only the path source moved), `base_reference_sha256` chain-continuity across REF4→REF5→REF6 stages, and namespace-generalized source loading (`WorkSources` dispatches any bundle namespace, not a hard-coded `REF4:`). The recorded-path dereference survives only in an explicit legacy fallback of `load_v4_public_sources` that `main()` never takes, kept so `tests/test_bake_excerpt_cleaning.py` pins the old behavior unmodified. Every new gate mutation-proven RED→GREEN (11 gates); regression test proves a manifest recording a NONEXISTENT absolute path loads fine through the bundle. Meta keys: `excerpt_v4_*` → per-namespace `excerpt_{ns}_*` + `excerpt_sources_bundle_sha256` (grep-verified unread elsewhere); `excerpt_refs_manifest_sha256` unchanged. CLI is breaking (`--v4-reference-manifest`/`--v4-normalized-dir` removed) — the V4.2 bake invocation must use the bundle. |

The frozen `_TRACK1_V4_CONTRACT_KEYS` untouched. Seven gates mutation-proven RED→GREEN, including restoring the original REF4-only filtering to prove the REF5-exclusion test catches exactly that regression (no pre-existing test covered the REF4 routing path end-to-end through `finalize_build` — that coverage was added too). 17 new tests; 471 + 930 existing tests pass unmodified. |


## Closed entries moved out of the tracker on 2026-08-19

### From `1. Outstanding Bugs / P2 - Medium`

| **`/admin` blocked the single event loop on every page build — six synchronous Supabase loader calls (up to eight queries), two of them duplicates** | `web/pages/admin.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-08-19) | Found by reading the owner's own perf-watch output, which logged `/admin` at 2.0–4.5 s with `event loop BLOCKED for 2062 / 2907 / 3140 / 2125 ms` interleaved. NiceGUI builds a page ON the loop and uvicorn runs ONE worker, so each round trip stalled every concurrent request, static assets included. The build called `get_pending_corrections()` (two queries), `get_pending_identification_reviews()`, `get_all_users()` and `get_all_corrections_count()` inline — and the Statistics tab then re-fetched the users and the pending corrections the other two tabs had already loaded, to show four numbers. The four mutating handlers (approve / reject / change role / delete user) blocked the loop the same way. **Fix:** one `_load_admin_data` payload run through `run.io_bound` and shared across the tabs (6 loader calls → 4; at most 8 queries → 5), every mutating handler offloaded too, and `get_user_client()` resolved ON the loop and threaded in as an explicit `client=` — building it inside the worker would read the auth session through `safe_user_get` outside the NiceGUI context, get `{}`, and silently hand the moderation queue an ANONYMOUS client, i.e. an empty queue that reads as "nothing pending". Guards in `tests/test_offloaded_readers.py`: an AST check that no Supabase-touching name is ever *called* in `web/pages/admin.py` outside the worker payload (it may only be *passed* to `run.io_bound`), plus behavioural tests for the dedup, the threaded client, and a loop ticker that keeps running during the load. Six mutations of the fix, each watched failing a named test. |

| **perf-watch misattributed slow requests to the loop, and reported a sleeping laptop as a 51-minute stall** | `web/perf_watch.py` | ✅ Fixed (2026-08-19) | Two defects in the diagnostic itself, both visible in one log. **(1)** The slow-request hint compared each request against the **all-time maximum** lag, so a single 4094 ms stall at startup made every later slow request print "loop lag is comparable to this request, so suspect the loop was blocked elsewhere" — including seven consecutive `/computed-identifications` builds during which the breach counter never moved off 1, i.e. the loop demonstrably was NOT blocked and the findings page really was that slow. The module's own comment says a diagnostic that misattributes is worse than a quiet one; the 2026-07-30 round had removed the *unconditional* claim but left the all-time comparison, which re-arms the same bug after any one stall. A bounded ring of stall windows now answers "did a stall OVERLAP this request", plus an in-flight branch for the stall the monitor is itself stuck behind and therefore cannot have recorded yet. **(2)** `event loop BLOCKED for 3069031 ms` was a suspended process, and it poisoned the maximum every other line quotes. A tick that is minutes late having burned almost no CPU is now `monitor NOT SCHEDULED`, counted separately and kept out of `max_lag_ms` (`GENIZAH_NOT_SCHEDULED_MS`, default 60000). The same `time.process_time()` measurement now names each real stall's KIND — GIL-bound Python (a `run.io_bound` worker counts; it is the same process) vs blocking I/O on the loop — where the old line asserted the I/O cause unconditionally. Five mutations, each watched failing a named test in `tests/test_perf_watch.py`. |

### From `1. Outstanding Bugs / P1 - Critical`

| **The citation-range filter on `/computed-identifications` always times out — the feature is non-functional, not slow** | `shared/discovery_service.py:1315-1352` (correlated locus `EXISTS`) | ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-20)** — deployed to production in `4f6e31f4` and confirmed by the owner in a browser (found 2026-08-19) | Measured 19.2 s / 38.4 s against a 5.0 s findings timeout, so a reader who picks a work and sets a range gets a timeout every time. Pre-existing (22.4 s on the previous artifact), not caused by the V4.2 deploy. 527 of 550 main-pool works are addressable by the range UI. Worse than one slow page: executor threads are not cancellable, so a timed-out read holds its heavy worker ~14 s and four concurrent readers push other heavy reads to `busy`. **FIXED in the working tree (2026-08-20), not yet verified in production.** Rewritten as an UNCORRELATED `IN (SELECT ...)` driven from `locus_unit` and kept inside the `WHERE` clause: `CORRELATED SCALAR SUBQUERY` becomes `LIST SUBQUERY`, 10,478 ms -> 61.3 ms, result sets byte-identical across 15 probed work x bound-shape cases, parameter order and every call site unchanged. The doc's `WITH ... AS MATERIALIZED` variant measured the same (62.6 ms) and was rejected: statement-level so it cannot live in the filter, and un-spliced params returned WRONG sets on 6 of those 15 cases while looking correct on the 9 that were empty anyway. A SECOND bug was found and fixed with it: `web/pages/findings.py::_fetch_children` dropped `locus_from`/`locus_to`, so a range-filtered parent expanded into unfiltered children -- unreachable until the query was fixed. A composite index on `discovery_locus_piece` (114 ms) is now redundant and needs a new artifact; it rides the next canonical rebuild if at all. Do NOT raise the timeout, and do NOT precompute per-piece citation bounds (citation positions are not monotone within every piece — false positives). Full assessment, evidence grades and verification recipe: [`docs/specs/discovery-performance-situation-2026-08-20.md`](specs/discovery-performance-situation-2026-08-20.md). |

### From `1. Outstanding Bugs / P2 - Medium` (Problem 3, discovery deploy path)

| **`bench_discovery.py` is not a smoke test, and it hung the V4.2 deploy** | `scripts/bench_discovery.py`, `_tmp/deploy_v42_discovery.ps1` step 8 | ✅ **Fixed (2026-08-20)** — all four parts: the runbook and the deploy script now run `scripts/smoke_discovery_readiness.py` (~1 s, through the real loader and service, including a citation-range read) instead of the benchmark; every statement the benchmark runs is bounded by a `set_progress_handler` budget (`--query-timeout-s`, default 30 s, ON by default) raising a NAMED `QueryBudgetExceeded` distinct from a cap breach; `Invoke-Box` sets `ServerAliveInterval=15 ServerAliveCountMax=8` on both ssh helpers; and the bucket picks are drawn from `_build_findings_filter`'s real predicate rather than a hand-written `di.main_pool = 1`, so they match the population the timed query measures (the divergence default removes 12.6% of main and 38.8% of "more"). `_state_skip`'s keys map gained `suppressed` and `sys_id`. 7 tests, 9 mutations each watched failing (found 2026-08-19) | `bench_findings_page()` runs unconditionally in `main()`; neither `--sample` nor `--warm-passes` bounds it. 1,024 filter states x 3 units x 3 sorts + deep-page + count = 15,363 combinations x 5 repeats = ~76,815 timed executions — one to two hours today, still ~25 min after the P1 fix. The deploy died as `ssh exit 255` (connection reset under an idle bench) and is recorded FAILED although steps 0-7 succeeded and the swap is live and healthy. Four fixes: take it off the deploy path; bound every statement via `set_progress_handler`; add ssh `ServerAliveInterval`; and make the picks use `_build_findings_filter` (they use `di.main_pool` alone, missing the default divergence predicate that removes 12.6%/38.7% of the buckets — a real defect, though NOT what hung this run). Detail: [`docs/specs/discovery-performance-situation-2026-08-20.md`](specs/discovery-performance-situation-2026-08-20.md). |
